# The Essence of the Stars (Astronomy)
## 134 Folios (Complete)
### 145 Pages

**The Essence of the Stars, Page 145**

<note>The following terms refer to the physical and thematic categorization of the text:</note>
<term>Sāravālī</term> (The Essence of the Stars: a classic 8th-century treatise on predictive astrology), <term>Jyotiṣam</term> (Astronomy/Astrology: one of the six auxiliary disciplines of Vedic study), <term>Patrāṇi</term> (Folios/Leaves), <term>Sampūrṇam</term> (Complete/Finished), <term>Pṛṣṭhāni</term> (Pages).

--- Page 3 ---
<meta>This is the opening page of the Saravali, a foundational text of Indian predictive astrology. It establishes the lineage of the work, following the tradition of Varahamihira while aiming for greater clarity and detail.</meta>

<page-num>1</page-num>

<margin>
Saravali
1
</margin>

->Om! Salutations to the illustrious Ganesha!<-

Om! May that Sun protect you—He, at whose rising this world awakens, at whose sleeping the vital breath <note>original: "marut"; refers to the life-force or wind</note> flows through all activities, and at whose setting the world sleeps and He alone remains awake, generating a light that knows no setting. (1)

Having set aside the ancient scriptures spoken at great length by the Sages, the *Horashastra* <term>Horashastra: The science of judicial astrology or natal horoscopy</term> was composed by Varahamihira because of the doubts arising from those earlier works. (2) However, because he did not make the division of subjects—such as the zodiac signs, planetary periods, divisional charts, royal yogas <note>combinations for power</note>, and the various strengths of the planets—sufficiently clear, (3) I have therefore extracted the essence from the extensive works composed by the Yavana kings <note>referring to Greco-Bactrian or Western astrological authorities</note> and others, considering all that was non-essential to be discarded. (4)

The illustrious Kalyanavarman, whose mind has been purified by the sacred scriptures, has exerted himself to produce this *Saravali* <note>The "Essence of the Row [of teachings]"</note>, the very essence of worldly existence, situated on the shore of the ocean of astrological science. His fame is like a lion’s sport, and his devotion is such that it seems the Goddess of Speech herself has come to him. (5) For the sake of clarifying the true meaning of the principles of horoscopy for students, this *Saravali* is presented by me, Kalyanavarman, like cool water or a fundamental garland. (6) Having placed this garland upon the forehead of the learned, may this pure-minded work on astrology shine like the trees of heaven. (7)

Without the light of this book, the science of horoscopy would be like a night without a lamp; for through this, the entire movement of the planets and the constellations is contemplated. (8) It is the cause for attaining the fruits of one's past actions <note>karma</note> and it reveals the certain destiny of a person. The technical name for this science is *Hora*, which is also the term used for the *Lagna* <term>Lagna: The Ascendant or the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term>. (9)

<summary>This introductory page contains the auspicious invocation to the Sun and Ganesha, followed by Kalyanavarman's justification for writing the Saravali as a more accessible and comprehensive alternative to Varahamihira's earlier works.</summary>
<keywords>Kalyanavarman, Saravali, Varahamihira, Horashastra, Astrology, Karma, Lagna, Yavana, Sanskrit tradition</keywords>

<meta>This page introduces the foundational concepts of Horoscopy (Hora) and the zodiac signs, establishing the astrological framework used in subsequent sections.</meta>

# The Science of Time

-> Dharahotra 2 <-

What is known in the world as the "Science of Knowledge" <note>Sanskrit: Jnanakam; referring to the branch of astrology dealing with birth charts</note> is here proclaimed as **Hora** <note>The term 'Hora' is derived from 'Ahoratra' (day and night) and refers to horoscopic astrology</note>. Furthermore, this word is truly a synonym for the investigation of fate itself. It is a help to men in the acquisition of wealth, and in times of misfortune, it is indeed a boat to safety. At the time of a journey, there is no counselor other than a birth chart <term>Jataka: a personal horoscope based on birth time</term>.

### Reflections on Hora

Salutations to the Sun, who sustains the earth from all sides with water! From where has the Lord Sun arisen, illuminating all with his perfected power? He emits the entire universe, with his limbs fashioned from the continuous succession of planets and their meanings. Time itself has set forth from him, made diverse by the differences of planets, places, and seasons.

**Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, and Leo; then Virgo, Libra, and Scorpio. Here are Sagittarius, Capricorn, and then Pisces—these are the names of the signs.**

Aquarius is a man carrying a water-pot; Gemini is a man and woman carrying a lute and a club; Pisces is a pair of fish; Sagittarius is a horse with a human upper body holding a bow. Capricorn has the face of a deer; Virgo stands in a boat holding a lamp and grain <note>original: "nisasya sahita" - accompanied by grain/crops</note>. Whatever the name of the group of signs, one should describe its characteristics according to that likeness.

The head, face, arms, heart, belly, hip, bladder, the two thighs, the two knees, the two shanks, and the feet are the limbs of Time <term>Kalapurusha: the personification of Time as a human body</term> to which the signs are joined in order. One should imagine the limbs of the "Time-Man" in all creatures at all times. Through the conjunction of good or bad planets, these limbs experience strength or weakness.

### The Names of Aries and the Others

**Kriya, Tavuri, Jituma, Kulira, Leya, Pathona** <note>These are the Sanskritized versions of the Greek names for the zodiac signs: Krios (Aries), Tauros (Taurus), Didymos (Gemini), Karkinos (Cancer), Leon (Leo), and Parthenos (Virgo)</note>...

<summary>This text introduces the spiritual and practical importance of Horoscopy (Hora), describes the visual symbols of the twelve zodiac signs, and maps them onto the physical body of the 'Time-Man'. It notably includes Sanskritized Greek names for the zodiac, showing the historical exchange between Greek and Indian astronomical traditions.</summary>

<keywords>Hora, Astrology, Zodiac signs, Kalapurusha, Sun worship, Greek-Indian astrology, Horoscopy</keywords>

...those who produce offspring, those who are <term>Kaurpika</term><note>likely referring to those born under Scorpio or having qualities of a crab/scorpion</note>, and those suffering from heart disease. 18. The names of the houses, the signs, and the fields <note>original: "kshetra"</note> have been declared according to their true meaning by all the best of the sages. 19. The circle of the zodiac consists of twelve signs; the Sun is known to rule the half starting from Leo, and the Moon rules the half starting from Cancer in reverse order. 20. All planets born from the Sun are brave, energetic, and daring; those born from the Moon are gentle, mild, and bringers of good fortune. 21.

The lords of the houses are Mars, Venus, Mercury, the Moon, the Sun, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Saturn, and Jupiter <note>original: "Kuja, Bhrigu, Budha, Chandra, Ravi, Budha, Bhrigu, Kuja, Guru, Shani, Shani, Guru"</note> respectively for the signs starting from Aries. 22. A wise man should consider the entire horoscope based on these lords of the houses; without these, it is impossible to take a single step in this Great Science <term>Mahashastra</term><note>referring to the vast science of Jyotisha or Astrology</note>. 23.

The <term>Vargottama Navamsha</term><note>the best division where a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth divisional chart</note> are the first, middle, and last portions of the movable, fixed, and dual signs respectively. The twelve divisions of the signs are ruled by the planets in their own order. 24. The <term>Drekkana</term><note>a one-third division of a sign (10 degrees)</note> of a sign belongs to the lord of that sign, the lord of the fifth house from it, and the lord of the ninth house from it. The solar and lunar <term>Horas</term><note>a half-sign division of 15 degrees</note> are ruled by the Sun and Moon in odd and even signs respectively. 25.

In odd signs, the <term>Trimsamsha</term><note>a division of one degree, though traditionally 1/30th, here used for specific degree-blocks</note> degrees of 5, 5, 8, 7, and 5 belong to Mars, Saturn, Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus. In even signs, this order is reversed, starting with Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and Mars. 26. Aries, Scorpio, Gemini, Capricorn, Leo, Pisces, Libra, Taurus, Sagittarius, Cancer, Virgo <note>original: "Mesha, Ali, Mithuna, Mriga, Hari, Mina, Tula, Vrishabha, Chapa, Karki, Kanya"</note>—all these are described in their equal divisions. 27. The sixty <term>Mairichi</term> divisions and the thirty <term>Phula</term> divisions... <unclear>the remaining text regarding specific degree calculations is fragmented</unclear>.

<summary>This page details the planetary rulership of the zodiac signs and introduces the technical sub-divisions (vargas) such as Horas, Drekkanas, and Navamshas, which are essential for precise astrological calculation.</summary>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of astrological calculations, specifically focusing on planetary periods (dashas) and the classification of zodiac signs (rashis) by their characteristics and strengths.</meta>

Equally. For <term>women</term> <note>Sanskrit: stri; referring here to female horoscopy</note>, there are twelve cycles of change. By dividing the breath-sounds <note>a technical calculation involving the numerical values of sounds/phonemes</note> by twenty-eight, the <term>ascendants</term> <note>Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> and others for a woman are known. Having multiplied the divisions of the breath-sounds and others, the <term>planetary periods</term> <note>Graha-dasha; the spans of time during which specific planets influence a person's life</note> are determined; this is the white <note>pure or clear</note> division. 29. I shall not describe their merits and faults in great detail here, as that belongs to the <term>science of birth-horoscopy</term> <note>Jataka; the branch of astrology dealing with individual births</note>. One should consider these factors together only after examining the essential principles. 30.

Starting from the sign of Aries: <term>cruel</term> <note>Krura; malevolent or harsh planets/signs</note> and <term>auspicious</term> <note>Shubha; benevolent</note>; <term>masculine</term> and <term>feminine</term>; <term>movable</term> <note>Chara; signs indicating change</note> and <term>fixed</term> <note>Sthira; signs indicating stability</note>; and <term>mixed</term> <note>Dvisvabhava; dual-natured signs</note>. These follow in groups of three. Here, <term>Cancer</term>, <term>Pisces</term>, and <term>Scorpio</term> are not considered to have <term>junctions</term> <note>Sandhi; the transitional points between signs</note>; this is well-known among the learned in this science. 31. [The text here becomes highly technical regarding life-breath and elemental transitions]. If the life-force is situated in its own sign, the person becomes healthy; if in a debilitated sign, the person becomes sickly. 32. By cycles of three, three, and three, the first ones are determined. One should analyze the various tasks and outcomes through these directions. 33.

<term>Human</term>, <term>quadruped</term>, <term>lion-like</term>, and <term>water-born</term> signs are strong in their respective directions. During the night, day, and twilight, the <term>quadrupeds</term>, <term>humans</term>, and others are powerful. 34. [Technical calculation of planetary strength follows]. <term>Taurus</term> and the others, including <term>Gemini</term>—except for those remaining—are said to rise by their <term>heads</term> <note>Shirshodaya; signs that emerge head-first on the horizon, usually considered auspicious</note>, except for <term>Pisces</term>. 35. Whether in a village or a forest, whether moving or stationary, a <term>zodiac sign</term> <note>Rashi</note> becomes strong when associated with <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Jiva</note> or the Moon. 36. From the ascendant, one calculates the body, wealth, siblings, knowledge, children, enemies, spouse, death, fortune, profession, income, and loss.

The degrees are thirty 30 in all directions <note>original: "sarvatastviṃśāḥ"; referring to the 30 degrees that constitute each zodiac sign</note>. The names of the houses are established by the ancient ancestors <note>original: "pitāmahyarāṇināmāni"</note> 31. The fourth and fifth houses are designated as the northern path. The *Horā* <note>term: Horā; refers to the half of a zodiac sign or the rising sign itself</note> and the others are known as the water-dwellers, the crossroads, the "thorn" <note>term: Kaṇṭaka; a technical name for the angular houses (1, 4, 7, 10) which act as pillars or "thorns" of the chart</note>, and the Center <note>term: Kendra; the angular houses</note> 32. The names for the fourth, fifth, and seventh houses include the "Water-Dweller," the "Underworld" <note>original: "pātāla"</note>, and the "Foundation" <note>original: "hibuka"; a Sanskritization of the Greek 'hypogeion'</note>. The tenth house is known as the Place of Action <note>original: "karmasthāna"</note>, the Sight, and the Proclamation 40. The ninth and seventh are the Trine <note>term: Trikoṇa; houses 1, 5, and 9, though here specifically discussing the 9th</note>; the seventh is also called the Setting, the Spouse, and the Desire 41. The sixth house is the Corner, the House of Enemies; the third is the Increase <note>term: Upacaya; houses 3, 6, 10, and 11 where planets grow in strength over time</note>. The twelfth house is the House of Loss <note>original: "riphpha"; from the Greek 'rhiphē'</note>, and the second house is known as the House of Family 42. Beyond the Centers <note>Kendras</note> are the Succedent houses <note>term: Panaphara; from the Greek 'epanaphora'</note>, and beyond those are the Cadent houses <note>term: Āpoklima; from the Greek 'apoklima'</note>; the planets give their results in these houses in a specific order 43. The sixth and tenth houses are also called the "Houses of Toil" by others. <unclear>svananumattasāvāsana</unclear> are the names for the self 44. Leo, Taurus, Aries, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Scorpio are the well-known Trine Houses <note>original: "trikoṇabhavanāni"</note> for the Sun and the other planets 45. The exaltation signs for the Sun and the others are stated as: Aries, Taurus, Capricorn, Virgo, Cancer, Pisces, and Libra. These are the houses where they reach their highest point of exaltation <note>original: "paramoccāḥ"</note> 49. The degrees of exaltation are ten, three, twenty, fifteen, five... <note>The text ends mid-calculation of specific exaltation degrees for each planet</note>

# On the Nature of the Planets and the Cosmic Body

...twenty-one. From its highest point of exaltation, the seventh sign is the point of fall <note>The 'fall' or debilitation is the point where a planet's influence is weakest, exactly 180 degrees from its exaltation.</note>. At forty-nine and a half degrees, the influence is most intense. From the Ascendant <note>The 'Lagna' or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth.</note> to the setting sign, the various parts of the body are produced. These physical forms are born resembling the celestial wanderers <note>The planets</note> and are equal to them in nature.

The lords of the houses are said to be connected to the various winds <note>The humors or 'doshas' of the body</note>. The Sun is the king, moving upward with tireless energy.

->The Colors of the Planets<-

Red, white, bright green, pale pink, variegated, and grey-brown are known in order. Black, gold, and reddish-brown; these are the colors of the variegated and tawny ones. The colors of the planets are thus described by the masters: like the luster of a jewel, or like the eye of a deer, or like the glow of a lamp.

### From the Saravali: Chapter on the Combinations of the Signs

The **Sun** <term>Ravi: the soul</term> is the soul of the Cosmic Man; the **Moon** is the mind; **Mars** is strength; **Mercury** <term>Shashija: born of the moon</term> is the power of speech; **Jupiter** <term>Devaguru: teacher of the gods</term> is knowledge and happiness; **Venus** <term>Shukra</term> is desire; and **Saturn** <term>Shani</term> is the sorrow of the Cosmic Man.

Men are powerful when these celestial travelers are strong; when they are weak, men are weak. This is the knowledge handed down. One should judge the physical form and the various distinctions of the body through the lords of the Ascendant and the planets. The passage of time and the various limbs of the body should be spoken of in their proper order.

From the Ascendant, one considers the head, the eyes, the ears, the nostrils, the cheeks, the chin, and the face. The strength of these parts is determined by whether they are influenced by "gentle" <note>Benefic planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> or "harsh" <note>Malefic planets like Saturn or Mars</note> forces. If the influence is gentle, it is auspicious; if otherwise, it is middling; and thus the strength is calculated in sequence. The head, the eyes, the ears, and the mouth...

The face, the neck, the shoulders, the sides, the heart, the stomach, and the navel; then the bladder, the genitals, the anus, the knees, the ankles, and the feet—these are the two sets of limbs imagined to correspond to the houses of the zodiac. 5 <note>This verse describes the 'Kalapurusha' or the cosmic man, where the twelve signs of the zodiac represent parts of the body from head to toe.</note> Malefic planets cause scars or marks, while benefics <insert>located in their own divisions</insert> grant beauty or distinctive signs according to their nature. If the planets are positioned in the opposite manner, they produce contrary results. 8 The Sun is the King; the Moon is also a ruler; Mercury is the Prince; <margin> : </margin> Mars, the son of the Earth, is the Commander-in-Chief; Venus and Jupiter are the Ministers; and Saturn, the son of the Sun, is the Servant. When these planets are strong, they bestow their respective forms and natures upon men. 9 The Sun, the Moon, the Son of Darkness <note>Rahu, the north node of the moon</note>, the Son of Simhika <note>another name for Rahu</note>, Saturn, and the Moon; the Benefics and the Teacher of the Gods <note>Jupiter</note> are the lords of the directions starting from the East. <margin> 8 </margin> Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus are naturally Benefic; Saturn and Mars are naturally Malefic. The Moon is considered auspicious when full, but the Night-maker <note>the Moon</note> is Malefic when waning. <margin> 12 </margin> The Sun is the Soul; the Moon is the Mind; the cruel Mars is Strength; Jupiter is Wisdom and Happiness; Venus is Desire; and Saturn is Sorrow. 10 Jupiter is the Teacher of the Gods; Venus is the Priest of the Demons; Saturn is the Slow-mover, the Son of Yama, the Cruel-eyed, and of a windy temperament. 11 Copper, white, red, gold, yellow, variegated, and dark are the colors of the planets. Fire, Water, Skanda <note>the god of war</note>, Vishnu, Indra, Indrani, and Brahma are the presiding deities of the planets from the Sun onwards. By worshipping these planetary deities, one attains gold, elephants, vehicles, and fame. 15 The Moon and Venus are feminine; [Saturn and Mercury] are eunuchs...

# The Nature and Governance of the Planets

Copper, the Sun, and Mercury belong to the deer <note>original: "mṛgau"; likely referring to specific animal associations or zodiacal classifications</note>; Jupiter and the Sun are of the day; Venus and the Moon are of the priestly class <note>original: "vipra"; the Brahmin caste</note>. Mercury governs speech. The Sun and Mercury are red, or the priest <note>Jupiter</note> is so; the Moon is of mixed nature, and Mercury with Saturn belongs to the laborers <note>original: "śūdrāṇām"; the Shudra caste</note>. Thus are the famous waters and winds described.

### On Tastes and Objects
The specific flavors—pungent, salty, bitter, mixed, sweet, acidic, and astringent—are governed by the planets. They also rule over cities, houses, pleasure gardens, beds, and collections of books. Furthermore, they govern garments, <unclear>sewn items</unclear>, and the various metals and gems: copper, jewels, gold, mixed metals, silver, pearls, and iron.

### On Time and Seasons
The divisions of time—moments, days, fortnights, months, and seasons—are ruled by the lords of the day. The masters of the seasons, starting with the cold season <note>original: "śiśira"</note>, are Saturn, Venus, Mars, the Moon, Mercury, and Jupiter respectively.

### On Sacred Knowledge and Realms
Jupiter is the lord of the **Rigveda** <note>the Veda of hymns</note>; Venus is the lord of the **Yajurveda** <note>the Veda of rituals</note>; Mercury is the lord of the **Samaveda** <note>the Veda of chants</note>; and Mars is the lord of the **Atharvaveda** <note>the Veda of formulas</note>. The Moon and Venus rule over gemstones. The Sun, Mars, Mercury, and Jupiter govern the realms of the gods, humans, and ancestors, as well as the paths to heaven and hell.

### The Physical Appearance of the Moon
The Moon is small <note>referring to its waning phase</note> or elevated, with a clever mind, tall, handsome, and possessing soft hair. He is beloved, with beautiful eyes, brave, eloquent, and steady.

### The Physical Appearance of Mercury
Mercury is red-eyed, with a firm touch and steady feet. He is a great scholar of the sciences, profound, and possesses a resonant voice. He is handsome, with charming eyes and sweet speech, of a fair or yellowish complexion. He is tall, truthful, and has beautiful hair. He is born of the wind and possesses a virtuous nature <note>original: "sāttvika"</note>.

<summary>This manuscript page details the astrological correspondences of the planets, including their governance over social classes, flavors, seasons, the four Vedas, and physical descriptions of the Moon and Mercury.</summary>

Summary

->**The Characteristics of the Planets**<-

One who is like molten gold, a dear friend, and the sole essence of strength; a man who appears like a woman <note>original: "viprastrī puruṣasalo," suggesting an effeminate or delicate appearance</note>, unfortunate, yet dressed in white like the Moon. || 21 || Short of stature, with reddish-brown eyes, a slender body, and a digestive fire that is easily disturbed; not tall, with marrow-filled bones, wearing dark garments, a friend to the learned, yet prone to violence. || 19 <note>The numbering in the manuscript appears non-sequential or corrected</note> ||

With high-set hair, of a reddish hue, possessing a paternal nature and a dark complexion; daring, skilled in deliberation, with a blood-red yet fair skin, and straightforward. || 22 || With blood-red eyes, sweet of speech, happy, possessing a slender life-force <note>possibly referring to a delicate constitution</note>, a knower of the truth, having few friends but speaking beautiful words, and possessing a thick neck. || 23 ||

Like a swan, of medium form, clever in speech, humble, with visible veins; one who brings light to all, speaking with certainty, and wise in the pursuit of results—this is Mercury <term>Budha: the planet Mercury, associated with intellect and communication</term>. || 24 ||

With slightly reddish-brown eyes, a scholar of the scriptures, with a voice as steady and loud as a lion's roar; of stable mind, <unclear>...</unclear>, with a body that is not thin, and a broad, elevated chest. Short, devoted to righteousness <term>Dharma: religious and moral duty</term>, restrained, clever, possessing great strength, forgiving, all-pervading, a clear speaker, and a leader among men—this is Jupiter <term>Guru: the planet Jupiter, the preceptor of the gods</term>. || 25 ||

Possessing beautiful arms, a broad face, and great radiance like a pearl; with a dark and spotless chin, free from bad physical marks. Lustful, of a windy constitution <term>Vata: one of the three humors in Ayurveda, associated with air and movement</term>, unfortunate, dressed in moon-white garments, influenced by the quality of passion <term>Rajas: the quality of activity, passion, and energy</term>, moving with grace, having large eyes, a stout body, and a constant smile. || 26 ||

Reddish-brown with deep-set eyes, of a lean mind, tall, with prominent veins and a lazy disposition; with dark limbs, of a windy nature, a backbiter, yet pleasant in dreams...

# On the Nature and Relationships of the Planets

The planets are embodied beings: the Sun is the soul-self; the Moon is the mind; Mars is strength; Mercury is speech; Jupiter is knowledge and happiness; Venus is desire; and Saturn is sorrow. <note>This verse describes the 'planetary cabinet' or the psychological aspects governed by each celestial body.</note> Among these, the Sun and Moon are kings, Mars is the commander-in-chief, Mercury is the prince-apparent, Jupiter and Venus are the ministers, and Saturn is the servant.

### On Natural Friendships
The Sun, Jupiter, Mars, and the Moon are mutual friends. <note>original: "mitrāṇi"</note> The Sun finds its exaltation in Aries; the Moon in Taurus; Mars in Capricorn; Mercury in Virgo; Jupiter in Cancer; Venus in Pisces; and Saturn in Libra. 

The Sun, Moon, and Mars are friends to one another. Mercury is friendly with the Sun and Venus. <margin>The relationship between Mercury and the Moon is unique; the Moon considers Mercury a friend, but Mercury views the Moon as an enemy.</margin> Jupiter is friendly with the Sun, Moon, and Mars, while Venus and Mercury are its enemies. Venus is friendly with Mercury and Saturn, but views the Sun and Moon as enemies. Saturn considers Mercury and Venus as friends, while the Sun, Moon, and Mars are its enemies.

### On Temporal Relationships
Planets situated in the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 10th, 11th, and 12th signs from another planet become its **Temporal Friends** <term>Tatkalika Mitra: friends based on current position rather than inherent nature</term> for that specific time. Those in other houses—the 1st, 5th, 6th, 7th, 8th, and 9th—act as enemies. 

### On Combined Relationships
By combining natural <note>Nisarga</note> and temporal <note>Tatkalika</note> relationships, five grades are determined:
1. **Great Friend**: Naturally friendly and temporally friendly.
2. **Friend**: Naturally neutral but temporally friendly, or vice versa.
3. **Neutral**: Naturally friendly but temporally inimical (or vice versa).
4. **Enemy**: Naturally neutral but temporally inimical.
5. **Great Enemy**: Naturally inimical and temporally inimical.

### On Planetary Strength
The strength <term>Bala: the calculated potency of a planet</term> of the planets must be considered in all judgments. This strength is fourfold:
* **Positional Strength** <note>Sthana Bala</note>
* **Directional Strength** <note>Dig Bala</note>
* **Temporal Strength** <note>Kala Bala</note>
* **Motional Strength** <note>Chesta Bala</note>

Jupiter and Mercury are powerful in the Ascendant (East). The Sun and Mars are powerful in the 10th House (South). Saturn is powerful in the 7th House (West). The Moon and Venus are powerful in the 4th House (North). <note>This refers to 'Dig Bala' or directional strength, where planets gain power by being in specific cardinal houses.</note>

<summary>This page details the complex system of planetary friendships and enmities, both inherent and situational, and introduces the four categories of planetary strength (Bala) used in astrological calculations.</summary>
<keywords>Jyotisha, planetary friendship, exaltation, Tatkalika Mitra, Dig Bala, planetary strength, Vedic astrology</keywords>

Planets are designated as masculine, feminine, or neuter; they are strong, moderate, or weak based on the lordship of the signs they occupy. In the first division of the day, the Sun is weak; Venus and the Moon are strong at midnight. Mercury is strong in the morning, the Sun at midday, and all-knowing Jupiter at the end of the day. 28. Saturn, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus are progressively stronger in their natural state. This natural strength should be considered by the wise when other strengths are equal. 29.

## The Chapter on Miscellaneous Matters

Having described the distinctions of the signs and the origins of the planets, I shall now explain the all-encompassing chapter titled "Miscellaneous." 1. A planet may be in nine states: Blazing, Healthy, Happy, Calm, Capable, Oppressed, Pained, Deficient, or Cruel. 2. A planet is **Blazing** <note>original: "Dipta"</note> when in its sign of exaltation; **Healthy** <note>original: "Svastha"</note> in its own sign; **Happy** <note>original: "Mudita"</note> in the sign of a friend; **Calm**...

# The Results of the States of Venus

When **Venus** is situated in a <term>benefic division<note>original: "shubha-varga"; refers to a favorable zodiacal subdivision that strengthens a planet</note></term> and is endowed with a net of throbbing rays, it brings about great splendor. Even if one is troubled by fears, they become highly respected and satisfied.

### The Ten States and Their Fruits

**3. Blazing (Deepta):** When Venus is in its "Blazing" state <insert>occurring in its sign of exaltation</insert>, the person wanders with great majesty, like a fire that consumes the multitude of their enemies. Their body is embraced by **Fortune** <note>original: "Lakshmi"; the goddess of wealth and prosperity</note>, and they possess elephants whose rutting secretions moisten the very earth.

**4. Healthy (Swastha):** In the "Healthy" state <insert>located in its own zodiac sign</insert>, the planet produces a person of pure intellect, endowed with the strength of character, gold, fine clothes, jewels, and a large family. They possess beautiful eyes and achieve great prosperity for their household within a white-walled city.

**5. Happy (Mudita):** When in the "Happy" state <insert>located in the sign of a friendly planet</insert>, the person delights in the company of charming women, gold, and jewels. Having conquered all calamities, they become a vessel for every kind of happiness.

**6. Peaceful (Shanta):** The person born under this state possesses a calm and tranquil mind. They are wealthy in character, serve as a **minister to a king**, are wise, helpful to others, and devoted to <term>righteousness<note>original: "Dharma"; the moral and religious law</note></term.

**7. Joyful (Shakta):** The person shines with women, fine garments, perfumes, and garlands. Their fame spreads in all directions; they are beloved by their wife and have all people under their favorable influence.

**8. Afflicted (Peedita):** If the planet is "Afflicted" by cruel influences, the person will be tormented by diseases and mental anguish. They wander with a wasted body, deeply grieving the separation from their kinsmen.

**9. Devoid of Merit (Viguna):** Even if such a person is a king with many vehicles, if the planet is "Devoid of Merit," they become poor in spirit and wealth. Surrounded by various miseries, they live a life more wretched than the most downtrodden villager.

**10. Displaced (Dina):** Fallen from their proper place, the person becomes distressed, base, and wanders to foreign lands. Their wealth is destroyed, they are physically impaired, and their heart is constantly terrified by fear of enemies. They suffer from the loss of their wife and a mind broken by the disappearance of all possessions.

A man who remains in the company of the wicked can never, in any way, cast off his burden of sorrow. **13** When the zodiac sign is in retrograde, the calculation of its strength is not recorded here because it is bound to time; therefore, it should be observed by those who are wise. <note>The text suggests that certain temporal strengths are too complex or variable for standard tables.</note> **14** Planets situated in their highest exaltation <term>Exaltation: the specific degree where a planet is most powerful</term> are strong; they are of medium strength in their root trine <term>Mula-trikona: a primary favorable position, slightly less powerful than exaltation</term> or their own house. When they are seen in the houses of enemies, they carry very little power. **15** In the houses of friends or neutral planets, a medium strength is proclaimed; however, for those in the second or tenth decans <term>Decan: a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</term>, the strength should be considered equal and slow-moving. **16** Having calculated the sum of the previous units of time, the yoga <note>astrological combination</note>, and its own orbital circle, the leader of the heavenly hosts <note>likely referring to the dominant planet or the Sun</note> determines the certain strength of the planets. **17** For one whose Moon or Ascendant is not in a fire sign, but the Minister of the Gods <note>original: "Suramantri" - the planet Jupiter</note> is in a center house <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, which are pillars of power</term>, he is said to be in a medium state. **18** One should determine the results of the houses by the strength of the zodiac sign and its lord. The manifestation of the results from the foremost planet must be performed individually. **19** There is no natural equality between the strength of the hour <term>Hora</term> and the planets, even with the bows <note>possibly referring to planetary arcs</note>. The sages say that the strength of the planets here is equal to the Lord of the Ascendant. **18** <note>The scribe has repeated the numbering 18 and 19 in the original manuscript.</note> In Leo, the first portions are the trine, and the rest is the Sun's own house. The 3rd part of the 30 degrees is the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term>, and the rest is the trine. **19** In Aries, the first twelve degrees are the trine, and the rest is the own house of Mars. In Virgo, the first 3 degrees are like the Sun <note>original: "Arka"</note>; the Ascendant portion of Mercury should always be considered. **20** Beyond that, the trine is produced by five degrees, and the rest is born of its own sign.

# On the Fruits of Planetary Positions

Beyond this, by ten degrees, the fruit of the <term>Trine</term><note>Sanskrit: Trikona; a highly auspicious 120-degree triangular relationship between zodiac signs</note> for <term>Jupiter</term><note>Sanskrit: Jiva</note> is described. In its own sign, it yields five parts, while for <term>Venus</term><note>Sanskrit: Shukra</note>, it is twenty-one. Two or three parts are assigned when it reaches the trine position.

When a planet is in its own sign, or in <term>Aquarius</term><note>Sanskrit: Kumbha</note> within its trine, or in its own house, or in <term>Leo</term><note>Sanskrit: Simha</note>, it produces auspicious results for men. However, if it has reached its <term>Debilitation</term><note>Sanskrit: Nicha; the sign where a planet is at its weakest</note>, it becomes fruitless in these matters. 

<margin>23</margin>

One-quarter of the auspicious fruit is given in a friendly sign; in one's own sign, the work is not fully realized. Three-quarters of the fruit is granted when the planet is situated in the sky in its trine. If it has reached its <term>Debilitated Division</term><note>Sanskrit: Nichamsha; a specific sub-section of a sign where the planet is weak</note>, it produces entirely sinful or negative results, and certainly becomes fruitless if it is in an enemy's sign. Three-quarters of the fruit is given in a beneficial house... <unclear>text obscured</unclear> ...situated in the trine.

The enemies of the <term>Daityas</term><note>The gods/Devas, specifically referring here to the Sun and other solar deities</note> and the Sun... <unclear>text obscured</unclear> ...by the planets. When defeated, they destroy the auspicious fruits and certainly increase the sins of men. 

A planet endowed with the strength of <term>Exaltation</term><note>Sanskrit: Uccha; the sign where a planet is at its maximum power</note> bestows supreme prosperity. A planet with the strength of its <term>Trine</term> bestows... <unclear>text obscured</unclear> ...or even lordship. 

Endowed with the strength of its own sign, it brings about a person full of joy, wealth, grain, and prosperity. Endowed with the strength of a <term>Friend's Sign</term>, it produces a man of fame and contentment. 

One who is wise and possesses stable wealth... <unclear>text obscured</unclear> ...all wealth. A man becomes famous through the strength of his <term>Own Ninth-Division</term><note>Sanskrit: Sva-navamsha; a specific 3-degree 20-minute arc that is highly significant in Vedic astrology</note>. 

Combined with the strength of an <term>Auspicious Aspect</term><note>Sanskrit: Shubha-darshana; when a beneficial planet like Jupiter looks upon another planet</note>, it makes a man wealthy and famous. He becomes very wealthy, a leader of all, handsome in body, and possesses great happiness.

Through the strength of the masculine and feminine signs <term>Pum-stri-bhavana-bala</term> <note>The strength a planet gains by being in a zodiac sign of its own gender</note>, the planets grant a person a hundredfold auspiciousness <note>original: "kallāṇa," a variant of kalyāṇa</note>. Such a person becomes cheerful, wealthy, and in the case of a feminine sign, fearful of the afterlife <insert>or the consequences of sin</insert>. 20

When a planet is endowed with strength, it leads a man toward its own cardinal direction. Having led him there, it provides him with ornaments, vehicles, and happiness. 21 On the other hand, if a planet is weak or "burnt" <term>bhasmara</term> <note>likely referring to combustion by the Sun</note>, it makes a person narrow-minded, with diminished wealth and poor conduct. 22 If the planets are nocturnal and endowed with strength, they provide the individual with courage. 22

Those planets that possess great vigor and are auspicious take away the misfortunes of the state. They grant men the fulfillment of their desires and elevate them to high positions. 23 By the strength of victory in planetary war <term>Yuddha-bala</term> and the strength of their own physical positions, the lords of the planets create a kingdom and a sphere of influence for the person. 24

Those planets known to have "day and night strength" <note>original: "rātri divā bala," referring to temporal strength or Kālabala</note> grant satisfaction. However, a planet that is weak and "burnt" <term>bhasmara</term> makes a person foul, oppressed by enemies, and devoid of luster. 25

Regarding directional strength <term>Dig-bala</term>: when the lords of the directions are satisfied in their respective positions, they grant happiness, the power to rule, wealth, and fame. 28 Through the strength of the lunar fortnight <term>Paksha-bala</term>, the Moon—shining like a jewel or a white elephant—bestows upon men the gain of women, gold, and land, as well as fame and success. 30

When the planets are constantly radiant with a network of pure rays and carry the weight of all types of strengths, they grant a kingdom, happiness, and the fulfillment of all heart-felt desires. 31 Those born under such influences are of good conduct, energetic, devoted to the gods, and possess thousands of garlands, wealth, and ornaments. They are gentle and friendly.

<page-num>52</page-num>

Men are shaped by the strength of the planets. <note>Verse 32</note> Those born under such influences become knowledgeable in their crafts, yet are devoted to cruel deeds and intent only on their own purposes. They may become followers of the Kapalika sect <note>An ascetic order often associated with skulls and fringe rituals</note>, overwhelmed by darkness <term>Tamas: the quality of ignorance or inertia</term>, cruel, deceitful, filthy, and ungrateful. When the malefic planets <term>Papa-grahas: planets like Mars or Saturn that tend to cause hardship</term> are dominant, men become backbiters and misshapen in form. <note>Verse 40</note>

When the auspicious planets <term>Shubha-grahas: "benefics" like Jupiter or Venus</term> are strong, men are courageous, famous in battle, and powerful; yet they can also be devoid of affection, harsh, and foolishly cruel. <note>Verse 41</note> Conversely, when these planets are weak, men become cowards in battle, prone to depression, yet gentle and averse to conflict.

---

Planets situated in their own signs of exaltation <term>Ucha: the sign where a planet is at its maximum strength</term> or in their own divisions, when placed in the angles <term>Kendras: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a birth chart</term>, become "Significators" <term>Karakas: planets that act as functional drivers of a person's destiny</term> for one another. <note>Verse 1</note> For example, if the Sun is in the sign of Cancer at the time of birth, and Jupiter is in the sign of Aries, they act as mutual significators for the individual. <note>Verse 2</note>

Planets residing in their exalted signs or their own houses are declared to be significators. Specifically, when the Sun is in Aries, it creates a special chain of influence. <note>Verse 3</note> All planets can become significators relative to the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> or their own positions; this is the opinion held by the great lords of the sages. <note>Verse 4</note>

Even one born into a lowly family attains prominence through these "Significator" planets. Such a person becomes a leader of men <term>Narendra: literally a king or lord of men</term>, full of wealth and lineage, of this there is no doubt. <note>Verse 5</note> Because of the various types of powerful significators, a person surely attains success, yoga <note>auspicious planetary combinations</note>, and fame.

# Chapter on the Classification of Significators

The instruction regarding these [planets] is to be expressed through the various classifications of their causal roles <note>original: "karaka-bheda," referring to the specific functional roles planets play in a birth chart</note>. 9. Thus ends the Chapter on the Classification of Significators in the *Saravali* <note>The Saravali is a classic 10th-century Sanskrit text on predictive astrology</note>.

## The Division of Strength among Signs and Others

In a certain specific rule, some [authorities] discuss the origin [of beings]. Therefore, I shall now declare the primary creative factors <note>original: "karaka-bhutam"</note> for all living creatures. In the signs characterized by the five qualities <note>referring to the five elements: earth, water, fire, air, and ether</note>... <unclear>textual corruption</unclear> ...the houses become the source of strength. 1. From the Sun comes the soul, and from the Moon the mind <note>This follows the traditional Vedic concept where the Sun represents the Atman (Self) and the Moon represents the Manas (Mind)</note>.

2. In this manner, when the planets are assembled, the paternal seed <note>original: "pitru rajah," referring here to the male essence</note> comes forth, and in women, the menses. 3. That which occurs as the menses is declared to be the very cause for the stability of the embryo. If the Moon is situated in the "Upachaya" houses <note>The 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses, associated with growth and improvement</note>, it is said to be fruitless if the months are not complete. 

5. When the Moon is in an Upachaya house and the male planets are possessed of rays, or if it is especially aspected by Venus, it creates a yoga <note>a specific planetary combination</note> for men. 5. When the Moon is waning and joined with Mars, a union occurs. Through the influence of the Sun and Saturn, one becomes a royal official or a warrior in battle. 

10. Through each of these results, and by the influence of cruel and malefic planets, a person's nature is shaped... <unclear>textual corruption</unclear>... 8. Following the bipeds and those in holes <note>referring to different classes of zodiac signs: human and reptilian/animal</note>, at the time of the cruel planets, the division of the planets' strength should be understood. 

9. If a planet is eclipsed but aspected by a benefic, a quarrel may arise during the ascent. Through the influence of gentle planets, one should learn the practices of the *Kamasutra* <note>original: "Vatsyayana samproyogika," referring to the arts of union described by the sage Vatsyayana</note>. Indeed, the string of fate is uneven, scented by deeds that are auspicious, inauspicious, or mixed; this governs the rules of the dwelling in the womb.

# On the Formation of the Embryo

When the <term>semen</term><note>original: "Shukra"</note> and <term>uterine blood</term><note>original: "Shonita"</note> unite, and the planets Mercury and Saturn are strong in their own divisions and increasing in strength, while the Moon is waning, the formation of the embryo occurs. 11. If Venus, the Sun, and Mars are positioned in their own signs or in signs of growth <note>the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</note>, and if the Ascendant is occupied by strong planets, the conception is certain to be successful. 12.

->The Union of the Couple<-

When the desire for union arises while the Moon is in Gemini, and the house of passion is favorable, the process should be free from obstacles, provided the bodily humors <note>the three Doshas: Vata, Pitta, and Kapha</note> are in balance through proper diet and oils. 13.

### On the Determination of Gender

If the Sun, Jupiter, and the Ascendant are strong and positioned in odd signs <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, etc., considered masculine</note> and odd divisions, the birth of a male child is predicted. If they are in even signs and even divisions, the birth of a female child occurs. 14. When Jupiter and the Sun are powerful in odd signs, they grant a son; when Venus, the Moon, and Mars are strong in even signs, they grant a daughter. 15.

If the Sun and Jupiter are in Gemini, or if the Ascendant and the Moon are in a double-bodied sign <note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces</note> and aspected by masculine planets, twins may be born. 16. One should carefully examine the strength of the planetary combinations and the wealth of the house before predicting whether a son or a daughter will be born. 17.

### On the Neutral Gender

If Saturn and the Moon are in mutual aspect, or if they are in odd and even signs respectively, or if Mercury and Saturn are powerful and aspect each other, the birth of a <term>neuter child</term><note>original: "Napumsaka"; refers to a third gender or sterile individual</note> is indicated. 18. If the Lord of the Fortnight is in an even sign and the Ascendant is in an odd sign, or if Mars and the Moon are in specific conflicting positions, the sages declare that the embryo may not develop normally. 19. When the number of odd and even signs is balanced, or if Mercury is in the Ascendant in an odd sign... <note>text breaks off</note>

If the Moon is situated in an even sign <note>Even signs are Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, Scorpio, Capricorn, and Pisces, considered feminine in astrology</note> and is aspected by Saturn, or if it occupies the tenth house, there will be a birth of twins. If Mars and Saturn are in Gemini, a twin pregnancy is certain. 21. When the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of conception or birth</term> is in an even sign, and Jupiter, Mars, Mercury, and the Ascendant are in dual signs <note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces</note> and are powerful, then two males are born. 22.

If the planet in the Ascendant is associated with a dual-sign division <term>Amsha|A divisional chart, likely the Navamsha or ninth-part, used to refine predictions</term>, the sage declares that there will be two females and one male born in that pregnancy. 23. If the Ascendant is in Gemini or Virgo and is occupied by Jupiter, then three males are born. 24. If the Ascendant is in Sagittarius and is occupied by Jupiter, there are three males; if in Pisces, there are three females. If the planets are in the last portions of these signs, there are five births. 25.

During the day, Venus and the Moon represent the mother and father <note>In day births, Venus represents the mother and the Sun the father; the text here notes specific planetary significators</note>. In a night birth, these roles are reversed. 26. Benefic planets located in the houses starting from the Ascendant represent the father and paternal uncle; those in other houses represent the mother and maternal aunts. 27. The afflictions to the fetus in the various months of pregnancy are explained in sequence. The consciousness of the fetus develops gradually as the months pass. 28. In the seventh month, the fetus is endowed with life; in the ninth month, there is anxiety; and in the tenth month, the birth occurs. The fruit of the pregnancy is determined by the planetary strengths. 29. Venus, Mars, Jupiter, the Sun, the Moon, Saturn, Mercury, and the Ascendant are the lords of the months in succession <note>Each month of pregnancy is traditionally ruled by a specific planet: 1st-Venus, 2nd-Mars, 3rd-Jupiter, 4th-Sun, 5th-Moon, 6th-Saturn, 7th-Mercury</note>.

### Contemplation of the Auspicious and Inauspicious Signs of the Womb (30)

If a cruel planet <note>a "malefic" planet like Mars or Saturn</note> is positioned in the hand <note>referring to specific lunar mansions or "Hastā" nakshatra</note> at the time of rising, and is established in a wide position, the womb is affected. If Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon, considered a shadow planet</note> is in the house of the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment</term>, this yoga <note>planetary combination</note> leads to the falling of the womb <note>miscarriage</note>. (31)

Alternatively, at the time of the impregnation ceremony <term>Nisheka: the ritual or moment of conception</term>, if Mars <note>original: "Dharā-rudhira," the red son of the Earth</note> is situated in the Ascendant and is not aspected by <note>seen by</note> friendly or auspicious planets, the womb will not be sustained. (32)

If the Ascendant is occupied by Mercury or Jupiter <note>original: "Jña-putrai," the sons of wisdom</note>, or if benefic planets are situated in the trines <note>1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note>, the eleventh house of gain, the sixth house, or the fourth house <note>original: "Ambu," the water house</note>, then the womb is strong and full of vitality. If malefic planets are in those positions, they consume the benefits. (33)

If the Sun is situated in a cruel division <term>Amsha: a divisional chart used for fine-tuned predictions</term>, it leads to death. If the Moon is not aspected by benefic planets, it brings destruction to those who are pregnant. (25)

When benefic planets are in the Ascendant and are not aspected by malefic planets, the mother is safe; however, if the Sun and Jupiter are in the Ascendant but are aspected by Mars, there is danger to the woman. (25)

If the Sun and Moon are in the twelfth house <note>the house of loss</note>, the pregnant woman may perish through water-related ailments or complications. If Venus is in the fourth house from the Moon, or if malefic planets are in the Ascendant, the womb is lost. If the Moon is in the eighth house <note>the house of death</note>, the fetus dies along with the mother. (30)

If Mars is in the fourth house <note>original: "Hivuka"</note> and the Sun is in the twelfth, the fetus dies along with the mother due to the influence of malefic aspects. (28)

If the Sun is in the Ascendant and is joined by waning or weak planets, the mother dies during childbirth. Likewise, if malefic planets are in the second and twelfth houses and are not aspected by benefic planets, the same result occurs. (32)

If the Sun is in the seventh house and malefic planets are in the Ascendant at the time of conception, the death of the fetus occurs along with the mother.

When the strong planets Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus cast their gaze <note>aspect</note> upon the fetus, it flourishes. However, the fetus may be lost if the lords of the months <note>the specific planets ruling each month of pregnancy</note> are afflicted by unfavorable conditions. **41** In the third month, women experience pregnancy cravings <note>Sanskrit: dohada</note> to a lesser degree. These cravings are understood through the nature of the month-lord, the rising sign <term>Lagna|the zodiac sign ascending on the eastern horizon at the moment of conception or inquiry</term>, and various planetary combinations. **42** The procedure for the delivery of the fetus for men <note>referring to the father's role or the child's sex</note> is determined by the strength of the rising sign at that time. If conception occurs during the bright fortnight <note>the waxing moon</note> in the seventh division, the birth will be delayed. **43** Nārāyaṇa <note>likely a reference to a specific sage or a divine authority in astrology</note> states that the birth occurs from the seventh house from the sign of conception. If the seventh house from the rising sign is certainly aspected by Mars <insert>the son of the Earth</insert> and Saturn <insert>the son of the Sun</insert>, specific physical traits are formed. **46** The qualities of the day-lord and the strength of the Sun and Moon combined determine the courage and nature of the mother. **47** If the rising sign is in the portion of Leo at the time of birth and is not aspected by the Sun, or if Mars and Saturn are in the rising sign, the physical limbs will be shaped accordingly. **48** When the decans <term>Drekkana|a 10-degree subdivision of a sign</term> of Mars or Saturn are rising, or if Mercury is positioned there, and if they are joined by malefic planets, the child may be born "entwined by a serpent" <note>likely referring to the umbilical cord wrapped around the body</note>. **49** If the Lord of the Rising Sign is in the sixth house and other planets are strong in the rest of the body, the child is born with the body covered in hair, or twins may be produced. **50** If the rising sign is in the portion of Saturn and the Lord of the Day is in a masculine sign, the conception may result in a birth after three years <note>this likely refers to a symbolic or mythological delay, or a specific rare astrological calculation</note>.

->The moon with its rays fully determined should be considered thus. 51. When Pisces is rising <note>the zodiac sign Meena</note> and is aspected by Mercury, the Sun, and the Moon, auspicious combinations are formed. However, if these combinations are aspected by malefic planets, they become unfavorable, even if they are also viewed by benefic planets. 81

Now, I shall explain the chapter concerning the determination of the time of birth, based on what was stated regarding the state of the conception. 1

If a sign that rises by the head <note>Shirshodaya signs, considered auspicious</note> is the Ascendant, the delivery will be easy and healthy. If a sign rises by the feet, the birth is difficult. If a sign rises both ways <note>dual-natured</note>, and is aspected by benefics, the birth is considered favorable. 2

The birth of those born at night should be understood through the portion of the house <note>the Navamsha or ninth-part division</note> that is rising. If the rising portion belongs to a friendly planet or a moveable sign, the birth occurs quickly. 3

If the Ascendant or its ninth-part division belongs to the child's own planet <note>the ruling planet of the sign</note>, and that planet is strong, the father will be present. If it falls in the division of Mars, and is influenced by powerful planetary combinations, one should judge accordingly. 4

The physical form and appearance are determined by the strength of the benefics and whether the planets are in their signs of exaltation or depression. The location of the birth is indicated by the position of the Lord of the Ascendant. 5

If the Moon is in a watery sign <note>such as Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces</note> in the Ascendant, or if it is in the fourth house, the birth is said to take place near water. This is especially true if the Moon is in an even sign and aspected by planets associated with water. 6

If a benefic planet is in the Ascendant and a malefic planet is in the seventh house, the birth may occur in a place where water meets land...

# On the Circumstances of Birth

For one who is born when the **Ascendant** <note>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> is occupied or aspected by benefics, the results are auspicious. If the Ascendant is **Scorpio** or **Cancer** and **Saturn** <note>Sauri</note> and the **Moon** are positioned there, the birth occurs in a place near water. 7-8

When **Saturn** is in the Ascendant and **Jupiter** <note>Jiva</note> is in the ninth house <note>the house of fortune and piety</note>, the birth takes place in a forest or a grove, accompanied by wise counselors. 8

If the birth occurs during the **Solar Hour** <note>Surya-Hora</note> or in a fortified place, and the **Moon** is in a watery sign, the delivery happens near a reservoir. 9

If the Ascendant is a "Prarupa" <note>formal or specific</note> house, the birth is in a mountain, forest, or fortress. If **Mars** <note>Rudhira, "the red one"</note> is in the Sun's Ascendant, the birth is in a place of fire or a smithy. If **Saturn** is in its own house and aspected by a benefic, the birth is in a comfortable residence. 10

When the **Sun** is positioned in a temple or a house of the gods, and **Venus** and **Mercury** are in a beautiful place, the birth is said to be in a lovely location. If the planets are in the signs of **Jupiter**, the birth occurs in a two-story or three-story building. 11

If **Saturn** is in a holy place <note>Tirtha</note> and the benefics are in the seventh house, the birth is in a place of pilgrimage. 12

When two planets are in their own signs or in the signs of enemies, the division of the house and the strength of the Ascendant determine the specific room of birth. 13

The direction of the birth room is determined by the strongest planet in the angular houses. The physical characteristics of the delivery room—whether it is strong, decorated, or simple—depend on the planetary strengths. 14

If the **Moon** is in a Navamsha <note>a one-ninth division of a sign</note> of **Jupiter**, and the **Sun** is in a friendly sign, the birth is in a grand house. If the Moon is in a Navamsha of **Saturn**, the birth is in a lowly or mean place. This is the opinion of the great sage **Varahamihira**. 15

The entrance to the house and the specific direction of the door are known through the **Hora** <note>hour</note> and the **Decan** <note>Drekkana, a ten-degree division</note> of the Ascendant, and by the conjunction of planets within the houses. 16

In a temple... <note>text breaks off</note>

The positions of the planets at the time of birth, starting with the Sun and others, determine the nature of the delivery room and the circumstances of the birth. Through the combinations of the Moon, Mercury, Venus, the Sun, and Jupiter, the wise astrologer should deduce the specific details of the birth chamber. 17. In the same way, the various houses <note>original: "bhavana," referring to the twelve signs of the zodiac</note> and the planets occupying them provide knowledge about the structural aspects of the house. The learned astrologers have described these auspicious signs for the benefit of those seeking such wisdom. 18.

The directions, starting from the East, are assigned to the planets. The physical structure of the delivery room, including the bed and its characteristics, should be determined from the rising sign <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> and the planets positioned therein. 19. The strength of the planets and their placement in various signs—whether in solitary or crowded positions—indicates the number of people present and the direction of the room's entrance. The specific nature of the bed and the lying-in area is revealed through these planetary influences. 20.

If the Moon is in its sign of debilitation <note>original: "nichasthe," the point where a planet is weakest</note> or if the Moon and the rising sign are in specific configurations, the number of attendants or midwives present can be estimated by the number of planets situated between the rising sign and the Moon. 21.

The physical appearance, beauty, and ornaments of the newborn are determined by the influence of Jupiter, the Sun, and the Moon. If auspicious planets <note>benefics like Jupiter or Venus</note> exert influence, the child will have a beautiful body and auspicious marks. If cruel planets <note>malefics like Mars or Saturn</note> dominate, the body may be deformed or lacking in auspicious marks. With a mixture of influences, the results will be moderate; all this must be carefully weighed by the strength of the planets involved. 22.

When the Sun, the "thousand-rayed one," is positioned in the twelfth house <note>original: "dwadasha-bhaga"</note>, the specific nature of the birth—whether it occurs in a fixed or movable environment—should be declared according to the sign occupied at the time of delivery. 23.

As long as the rising sign is occupied by a strong planet, the lamp in the delivery room will be bright. If the planet is weak or in a waning state, the lamp's light will be dim or fading. 25. Through the strength of the planets, one can describe the oil in the lamp, the wick, and the direction the lamp faces. If the planets are devoid of strength, the light is said to be extinguished or absent.

<note>The text begins with a partial word "va-", a catchword from the previous page.</note> If a malefic planet <term>papagraha; planets like Saturn or Mars that exert a challenging influence</term> is situated in the sign of Leo at the time of birth, that person will face the messengers of Death <note>original: "Yamajha"</note> within three years; if they survive that, they may pass within a single month. (1)
If a malefic planet is in the seventh house <note>the house of partnerships and death-inflicting power</note> from the Ascendant <term>Lagna; the degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon</term> and is also aspected by other malefics, it causes the death of the newborn, unless a "nectar-like" influence <note>a beneficial planetary aspect</note> is received. (2)
When Venus is in the sign of the Sun or Moon <note>Leo or Cancer</note> and all the auspicious planets are in the twelfth, ninth, or eighth houses, the child is protected for sixty years; otherwise, death occurs within years—of this there is no doubt. (3)
If the benefics <term>saumya; gentle planets like Jupiter or Venus</term> are in the tenth, eighth, or fourth houses from the Ascendant, and the Moon is also involved, it protects the child, yet some say it may cause death within four years. (4)
The powerful results of the "Royal Yogas" <term>Rajayoga; planetary combinations indicating high status or power</term> which were formulated by the multitudes of Yavana <note>original: "Yavanoghai"; refers to the Greco-Bactrian or "Greek" astrological tradition integrated into Indian Jyotisha</note> masters—these are only known to those of noble lineage who have mastered their essence. (5)
In whichever house Ketu <term>the South Node of the Moon</term> is placed, if it is widely extended <note>strongly placed</note> at birth, one should know that the death of that newborn will occur within two months. (6)
If the Sun <note>original: "Divasvara"</note> is in the tenth house <note>the "sky" or midheaven</note> and is aspected by many malefic planets, it causes death; if Mars is also there, the deliberation of the wise suggests there is no doubt of this outcome. (7)
If the Decan <term>Drekkana; a 10-degree subdivision of a sign</term> of the Ascendant is associated with the "Serpent Noose" <note>original: "Pashadhara"; a specific symbolic omen</note> and is joined by cruel planets, the child will die within seven years. (8)
<note>The numbering skips from 8 to 14 in the original manuscript.</note>
If Rahu <term>the North Node of the Moon</term> is aspected by malefic planets at the time of birth, it leads to death within ten years; however, some Teachers <note>original: "Acharyas"</note> say this applies only if it is in certain signs. (14)
If malefic planets are in the trines <term>Trikona; the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term> and benefics are in the angles <term>Kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, but the eighth and twelfth houses are occupied by the "Serpent" <note>Rahu or Ketu</note>, the person will surely give up their life. (15)

# The Essence

<margin>
The Essence

->**The Essence**<-

If the Moon is situated in the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> and is aspected by malefic planets, while the Lord of the Birds <note>The Sun</note> and the Moon are in specific unfavorable positions, the child born under such a sky will soon perish. 22. If the lords of the twelfth, seventh, and first houses are weak, and the son <note>the child</note> is not seen by the gaze of a strong warrior <note>a powerful planet</note>, and if the <term>Kendras</term><note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, considered the pillars of a horoscope</note> are devoid of benefic planets, the presence of malefics will cause the separation of the newborn from life. 23.

If a malefic planet is in the seventh part of the wheel <note>the 7th house</note> and a benefic is similarly placed, and the birth occurs in the sign of Scorpio, the child will not live long and will quickly dwell in the abode of the serpents <note>the underworld/death</note>. 28.

If the <term>Lagna</term> is at the end of a sign and malefic planets are in the <term>Kendras</term>, death occurs. This was said by the Lord of the <term>Yavanas</term><note>original: "Yavanadhipati"; refers to the Greco-Bactrian or Greek astrological authorities who influenced Indian astrology</note> in his own treatise. 29 <note>Verse number written as 12 but contextually follows 28; likely scribal error for 29</note>.

If the birth occurs during the evening twilight in the hour of the Moon <note>the lunar Hora</note>, with the Moon in the <term>Lagna</term> and malefics occupying the <term>Kendras</term>, death is certain for the newborn. 30.

When the Moon is situated in the <term>Hora</term> <note>astrological hour/half-sign</note> of a malefic and is aspected by two malefic planets without the strength of a benefic's gaze, the child who is born will surely die. 31.

If malefics are in the seventh house and are aspected by cruel planets, the child dies along with the mother. However, if they are seen by benefics, death may be delayed until the seventh month. 32.

If an eclipse occurs and the Moon is joined with cruel planets in the <term>Lagna</term> while Mars is in the eighth house, the child dies along with the mother. If the Moon is in a <term>Kendra</term>, death occurs within a month. 33.

If the <term>Lagna</term> is at the very edge of a sign and malefic planets occupy the "thorns" <note>the <term>Kendras</term></note> without being seen by benefics, the death of the newborn is declared immediately. 34.

Whether the <term>Lagna</term> is in a movable sign or a fixed sign, if it is empty of virtues and not seen by benefics, the end of life is certain. 35. Thus ends the section on the <term>Hora</term> of death.

If malefic planets <note>planets considered naturally unfavorable, such as Saturn or Mars</note> are situated in the angles <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a birth chart</term>, even if they are not stationary, the death of the newborn occurs quickly, provided no benefic planets are present in those same angles. 38

When the lords of the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> and the house of wealth are associated with Saturn and the nodes <note>Rahu or Ketu</note> in the angles, the death of the newborn follows in due order, even if Jupiter is present. 39

If malefic planets are in the Ascendant and the trines <term>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</term>, while the 8th house <note>the house of death</note> is occupied by benefics, the **Yavanas** <note>original: "Yavanaiḥ" referring to the Greeks or Ionian astrologers</note> proclaim that immediate death occurs. 40

The Sun and Saturn joined together cause death at the time of birth; however, if they are aspected by Jupiter, death is delayed for three years. 41

When malefic planets are strong and situated in the Ascendant or the trines, and are joined with a waning Moon while being un-aspected by benefics, they cause death in the first month. 42

If strong malefic planets occupy their own signs or the Ascendant, and the Moon is strong but not aspected by benefics, death occurs within one year. 43

By the combinations of the Sun, Moon, Mars, and Jupiter, or by the assembly of Venus and others, if they are not in their proper places: the Sun, Moon, Mars, and the Moon's node <note>Shashanka here likely referring to the node or a specific lunar position</note> cause death surely within five years. 44

Death occurs in as many years as indicated by the signs, or in as many months as indicated by the position of the Ascendant. If the lord of the sign is a malefic and aspects the Ascendant, it causes death in that many months. 45

The Sun, Moon, and Venus, if afflicted by malefic planets and devoid of benefic influence, cause destruction <note>death</note> within eleven years; this is known even if only one such planet is so situated. 45 <note>The manuscript repeats the number 45 for this final verse.</note>

# Chapter on the Cancellation of Malefic Influences

<margin>
Saravali
Chapter 12
</margin>

The moments described are auspicious for destroying misfortunes, just as the rising of the sage Agastya <note>The star Canopus, whose rising is traditionally said to purify the waters</note> clears the waters of the entire world. In this world, these combinations immediately destroy all evils. 84-85. If the ascendant is Aries, Taurus, or Cancer, and the Lord of the Ascendant is possessed of strength and aspected by auspicious planets, the misfortune is pacified and pleased, just as a king might forgive a man who has committed an offense. 86.

When the Moon is in its full strength, it creates "Lotus-like" qualities and destroys all misfortunes, just as the sun dispels the darkness of the night. 87. Even if there are many malefic combinations, they will not produce their evil results if the Lord of the Moon's sign is placed in a quadrant <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, making the person like a king. 89. These cancellations of misfortune have been declared by me as established by the ancient masters; by knowing these, an astrologer becomes honored by kings. 88.

## Thus ends the Chapter on the Cancellation of Malefic Influences in the Saravali.

### The Sunapha, Anapha, and Dhurdhura Yogas

Now follow the Sunapha, Anapha, and Dhurdhura yogas, which occur in sequence based on the planets (excluding the Sun) being in the 2nd, 12th, or both houses from the Moon, who is the awakener of the night-blooming lily forest. 1. If the Moon is devoid of these yogas and no planets are in the quadrants from it, the result is the Kemadruma yoga, which is very painful and difficult. 2. There are thirty varieties each of Sunapha and Anapha, and when combined in the Dhurdhura configuration, sixty variations are described by the masters through the method of permutation. 3.

A person born under Sunapha yoga is wealthy, earns his fortune through his own efforts, is virtuous, knowledgeable in the meaning of the scriptures, widely famous, enjoys a peaceful life, is handsome, and serves as a minister or a king. <note>The text describes the high social and intellectual status granted by favorable lunar positions.</note>

Renowned for the qualities of speech, intellect, and valor, he is pure and possesses the happiness of self-attained wealth, vehicles, and enjoyments. A giver, he nourishes happy people with his acquired wealth; he is a leader born to shine like the day. 6.

### The Kemadruma Yoga
Bereft of a wife, wealth, children, and friends, he is joined with poverty, disease, and sorrow. A low-born person <note>original: "mleccha," referring to an outsider or one of non-Vedic conduct</note>, wicked, and possessing an intellect opposed to all the world; such is one born in **Kemadruma yoga**, even if he is born into a royal lineage. 7. The aspects of the planets in the Lion-like yoga <note>Kesari yoga</note> have been proclaimed; among them, the Moon's position is the most primary for determining wealth and form. 8. The wise should properly declare the measure of these strengths—enjoyments and wealth—based on the planetary fruits, for without strength, there is no fruit. 9.

### Sunapha Yoga Variations
One is characterized by valor and great wealth, yet uses harsh speech and is cruel. He is a perpetrator of violence and an enemy of the twice-born <note>The priestly or scholarly class</note> when **Sunapha yoga** is formed by Mars. 10. Skilled in the Vedas, scriptures, and music, devoted to righteousness, a composer of poetry, and pleasing to the people; he has a beautiful body and is well-disposed toward all when the yoga is formed by the Son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note>. 11. Intent on the principles of various sciences and stories, and highly prosperous; the Teacher of the Gods <note>Jupiter</note> makes one the jewel of his family and abundant in wealth when in **Sunapha**. 12. Possessing splendor, majesty, and wealth, wise in his undertakings, and full of valor; he is endowed with beauty and all...

...one becomes wealthy and successful through their own efforts. Bearing an excellent name, fortunate, fond of debate, known to the world, brave, and a leader in battle; this occurs when the Moon is situated between Venus and Mars. 23. In this yoga, one achieves success in their endeavors and becomes a collector of great wealth.

One who is angry, a backbiter, and excessively silent <note>original: "ati-mauna"</note> is born under the combination of Mars and Saturn. **(23)**

One devoted to righteousness <term>Dharma: the path of cosmic order and duty</term>, knowledgeable in the scriptures, eloquent in speech, and possessing great wealth and fame is born when the Moon is situated between Jupiter and Mercury. **(24)**

One who speaks kindly, is fortunate, handsome, and serves as a leader among men, yet remains a brave servant, is born under the combination of Mercury and Venus. **(25)**

One who wanders from country to country, is highly learned, yet lacks formal education <note>likely referring to practical wisdom versus academic schooling</note>, and opposes his own kinsmen, is born when the Moon is between Mars and Saturn. **(26)**

One who appears unpleasant but is deeply joyful, surrounded by gold and gems, and possesses all things produced in his land, is born under the combination of Jupiter and Venus. **(27)**

One who follows the wealth and traditions of his family, is sweet-spoken, wise, affectionate toward his mother, respected, self-motivated, and handsome, is born when the Moon is between Jupiter and the Sun. **(28)**

One who is a good speaker, a leader of a circle, but deceptive toward women and possessing unequal wealth, yet remains obedient to the law, is born when the Moon is between Venus and Saturn. **(29)**

Performing undesirable deeds <margin>The influence of the nodes</margin>, yet attaining all-encompassing knowledge and modest success; one who possesses all marks of wealth and happiness is guided by the elephant-headed god <note>Ganesha/Vinayaka</note>. **(30)**

One who is near the teacher, skilled with the sacred grass <term>Kusha: grass used in Vedic rituals</term>, and remains steady even in the heat of battle or the cold of winter, shall be a lord of the earth or a king if the planets align in a single degree. **(31)**

When all the benefics <term>Subhas: auspicious planets like Jupiter or Venus</term> are situated in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the rising sign</term>...

### Thus ends the chapter on Lunar Yogas in the Saravali.

Now, the Yogas formed by the Sun: If there are planets in the second house from the Sun, excluding the Moon, it is called <term>Vesi Yoga</term>. If planets are in both the second and the twelfth houses from the Sun, it is called <term>Ubhayachari Yoga</term>. 1.

The person born under Vesi Yoga is balanced, patient, stable, and possesses a refined vision. The Lord of the Yavanas <note>original: "Yavanadhipati"; likely referring to Yavanesvara, an early authority on Indian astrology influenced by Greek traditions</note> says that such a person is always upright in their dealings. 2.

If Jupiter creates this Vesi Yoga, the person is honorable, wise, and courageous. If Venus <note>original: "Bhrigu-suta"; literally 'Son of Bhrigu'</note> creates it, the person is famous but may be timid or dependent on others. 3.

If Mercury creates it, the person is polite, modest, and skilled in serving others, though perhaps physically slight. 4. If the Sun is in its own sign or exalted, the person becomes a leader or a ruler, possessing wealth and vehicles. 5.

When the Sun is in a friendly sign, the person is healthy, balanced, and virtuous <note>original: "Sattvika"; referring to the quality of purity and goodness</note>. 6. If Jupiter is in the Vesi position, the person is firm in intelligence and strength. If Venus is there, the person may be dark-complexioned, passionate, and perform many deeds. 7.

If Mercury <note>original: "Bodhana"</note> is present, the person is well-spoken and handsome. If Mars <note>original: "Bhumi-suta"; literally 'Son of the Earth'</note> is in the Vesi position, the person is famous in battle and possesses great courage. 8. If Saturn is there, the person may have the nature of a merchant but might also take the wealth of others...
