<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary combinations (Yogas) from the preceding section of the Vesindhu, specifically focusing on the effects of planets in relation to the Sun.</meta>

When the Lord of the <term>Vesi Yoga<note>An astrological configuration where planets occupy the 2nd house from the Sun.</note></term> is joined with the Moon in the ninth house, the native becomes a sage, possessing the desired strength of the planets. Born of a royal lineage, they achieve all fruits of life, surpassing others like the Moon among stars. 10

One born under this influence is all-enduring, possesses a seventh-house gaze <note>a full aspect</note>, has a well-proportioned body, and is victorious in battle. They are not overly sensual, are filled with complete knowledge, and will have sons through the practice of <term>Ubhayachari Yoga<note>A configuration where planets occupy both the 2nd and 12th houses from the Sun.</note></term>. 11

Such a person is fortunate, possessing many servants and wealth; they are a refuge for their kinsmen and devoted to the king. Disciplined and spirited, they enjoy all pleasures through the influence of the <term>Ubhayachari<note>Literally "moving on both sides" of the Sun.</note></term> configuration. 12

**Thus ends the section on the Svabhayachari Yoga in the Vesindhu.**

Now, the Great Teacher <term>Yavanacharya<note>Literally "The Greek Teacher," referring to the historical transmission of Hellenistic astrology into Sanskrit traditions.</note></term>, setting aside all malice and envy, explains the specific combinations of planets in the houses of the zodiac. 1

When the Sun and Moon are in conjunction, the person may be under the influence of women, undisciplined, skilled in deception, and fickle-minded. They may be involved in the trade of wine and spirits, yet they are clever in their actions. 2

When the Sun and Mars are together, the native is energetic, bold, perhaps foolish or stubborn, yet endowed with great strength. They are eloquent, focused on their tasks, and wealthy, though their speech may at times be harsh. 3

When the Sun and Jupiter are together, the native is noble—perhaps even a lord of the earth—contented, strong, handsome, and learned. They are very righteous, a counselor to kings, prosperous, and eager to gather friends around them. 4

When the Sun and Saturn are joined, the native is knowledgeable in the ways of the world, possessing the power of learning and the ability to grasp the sciences, though they may struggle with physical strength or consistency. 5

When the Sun and Venus are together, the native seeks the company of women and has few kinsmen. They may suffer from internal diseases but remain devoted to dharma <note>righteousness/duty</note> and are constantly engaged in their work.

A man born under the **conjunction of Jupiter and Mercury** <note>Sanskrit: *Guru-Budha-Yoga*</note> becomes a physician possessed of wisdom and science; he has a pure body, and through this union, he attains happiness, virtue, and a noble bride. 18.

Under the influence of Mercury and Venus, one becomes a master of the body's vital breaths <note>likely referring to *Pranayama* or physical health</note>, a swift runner, a scholar of the Vedas, and a gifted orator. Through his conduct, he delights in humor and takes pleasure in composing books and wearing garlands. 19.

A man born under the **conjunction of Saturn and Mercury** <note>Sanskrit: *Shani-Budha-Yoga*</note> is often engaged in cruel undertakings; he is a deceiver, devoted to the act of theft, yet he is also clever and possesses beautiful speech. 18. <note>The numbering in the original manuscript repeats 18 and 19 for different planetary pairs.</note>

Through the union of Jupiter and Venus, a man is distinguished by his knowledge of the Vedas and his steadfastness in sacred law <note>Sanskrit: *Dharma*</note>. Furthermore, by the **conjunction of Jupiter and Venus**, a man will possess an excellent wife and become wealthy. 19.

One who is cruel, yet knowledgeable in various arts, a lord of the city, and famous; such a man is born under the **conjunction of Saturn and Jupiter**. He becomes a leader of guilds, assemblies, villages, or institutions. 20.

A man born under the **conjunction of Saturn and Venus** is devoted to beautiful reflections, yet his mind is constantly distracted by his studies and work. He is highly exalted, a lord of cattle, and a man of great honor. 21.

When the planets are situated in mutual calculation with the aforementioned results, they produce these mixed effects according to the nature of the houses and other variables. 22.

A man is shameless, devoted to physical labor, an expert in machinery, and brave in seizing the cities of enemies, and skilled in primary actions when the **Sun, Saturn, and Mars** are situated together.

One is radiant, of clever intellect, a practitioner of the arts and sciences, fond of the company of virtuous men and drinking, a performer of his own duties, and steadfast, when the **Sun, Moon, and Saturn** are standing together in one place.

When the Sun, Jupiter, and Mars are together: <note>original: "dharmonmishra-bhagava-guru-bhaskarai:"</note> the person is 13. untruthful, unable to complete tasks, devoted to others, and hates their own kin. When Venus, Saturn, and the Sun are together: 15. the person will be a traveler, devoid of honor, wealth, and proper conduct. When Mars, Saturn, and the Sun are together: 16. the person becomes a sinful king <note>or leader</note>, practicing low behaviors, sickly, lacking character, and living in a village or remote area.

When Mars, the Moon, and the Sun are in the same place: 17. the person is prone to misfortune, obsessed with women, a thief, and has a mind easily swayed by women. When the Moon, Saturn, and Mars are together: 18. the person is ill-behaved, loses their children and spouse, and is described as being devoted to wandering <note>original: "brahmarana-ratah"</note> and lacking courage.

When the Sun, the Moon, and Saturn are in a specific yoga <term>Yoga: a specific planetary alignment or "yoke"</term>: 19. the person is abandoned by their mother in childhood, but later becomes famous for their valor. When Mercury, the Moon, and the Sun are together: 20. the person is wealthy, capable, eloquent, energetic, influential, possesses great fame, and has many friends and brothers.

When Mercury, Venus, and the Moon are in conjunction: 21. even if the person has a mind refined by education, they may engage in low behaviors; they are a servant, intoxicated by wealth, and greedy. When Saturn, Mercury, and the Moon are in conjunction: 22. the person is dark-complexioned <note>or "asita," possibly meaning "unbound"</note>, clever, eloquent, and a protector of the virtuous.

## Chapter on the Conjunctions of Saturn and Jupiter

A scribe, a thief, a wanderer, or one who is sickly and skilled in the expansion of wickedness; such is the result of **Mercury**, the **Sun**, and **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> being together, which is never auspicious. When **Venus, Mercury, and Rahu** <note>The North Node of the Moon</note> are joined with **the Sun** <note>original: "bhārgavaiḥ" - here implying the solar group</note>: the person born has a disproportionate body, is short in stature, lacks wealth, is soft-spoken but a great fool, and is prone to shouting or thundering.

In the conjunction of **Venus, Mercury, the Sun, and Saturn**: one becomes a goldsmith, is renowned for wisdom, is a craftsman or a great chariot-warrior, remains courageous, and is free from disease.

When **the Moon, Jupiter, Mercury, and the Sun** are joined: the person is restless or deformed, yet handsome, eloquent, short of stature, and highly respected by his people.

When **Saturn, the Sun, Mercury, and Venus** are together: one is deprived of the happiness of mother, father, and priests <note>Brahmins</note>, is prone to anger, and is a wanderer who destroys his own wealth.

With the conjunction of **the Sun, Mercury, the Moon, and Saturn**: one has defective eyes, and becomes a master of water, deer, and forests, yet remains a happy lord of men.

When **Venus, the Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn** come together: the man is skillful, has squinted eyes, gains much wealth through women, is learned, and is beloved by the wives of others.

When **the Sun, the Moon, Mars, and Saturn** are in one place: the man is a leader of justice <note>or a judge</note>, follows the conduct of women, is brave, easily angered, has a body as strong as a monkey, yet is secretly timid.

When **Mars, the Moon, and Venus** <note>with the Sun</note> are joined: one is a scholar, brave, a bearer of weapons, a conqueror of enemies, and devoted to sensual pleasures; however, he is also wicked and a wanderer.

With the union of **the Sun, Jupiter, Mercury, and Mars**: the man is devoted to the wives of others, is cruel, has a difficult temperament, is depressed, and lacks strength.

When **the Sun, the Moon, Mars, and Mercury** are joined: the combination is auspicious, yet the man may be devoid of a wife, follow low conduct, or become a poet, a chief, a minister, or a commander of an army.

# On the Combinations of Four Planets

A man born under the conjunction of the **Sun, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn** becomes endowed with good character and possesses a body free from disease. 33

One born with **Mercury, Mars, Venus, and Saturn** together is brave, learned, and eloquent, yet remains devoid of wealth; he is endowed with truthfulness and purity. 34 <note>The text suggests a "noble poverty" where intellectual and moral strength does not translate to financial gain.</note>

When **the Sun, the Moon, Mercury, and Venus** are joined, the person is famous, but also dirty, a consumer of others' food, wicked, and has a body afflicted by ailments. 35

A person becomes radiant, possesses the qualities of a priest <note>original: "Vipra-guna"</note>, yet is fickle, fond of daring acts, and obsessed with women when **Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn** are situated in one place. 36

One who is wise, finds pleasure in the scriptures, is devoted to his desires, and possesses a poisonous <note>or perhaps "fierce"</note> devotion is born when **Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn** stay together; such a man is constantly joined with women. 37

## Thus ends the Chapter on Four-Planet Combinations in the Saravali.

<note>The manuscript now transitions into further specific groupings, possibly involving the Moon or different permutations of the previous set.</note>

A man born under the wealth-giving yoga of the **Sun, Jupiter, and the Moon** <insert>and likely a fourth planet, possibly Venus</insert> suffers separation from his father's body <note>meaning early loss of the father or physical distance</note>. 1

He becomes a servant, lacks children, and wanders in various directions when born under the combination of **the Sun, Venus, Saturn, and the Moon**. 2

One who is short-lived, possesses little wealth or fortune, is devoid of all strength, and is troubled by diseases and enemies is described by the sages as being born under **Saturn, Mercury, and the Sun** <insert>with the Moon</insert>. 3

A man who is highly knowledgeable, always devoted to his father, and has a taste for music <note>original: "geya-ruchi"</note> is born under the division of **Mars, the Moon, and the Sun** <insert>with Mercury</insert>. 4

Such a person is skilled in battle, capable, a stealer of others' wealth, a tormentor of others, treacherous, and wicked.

By the combinations of Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Venus, Moon, Sun, and Mercury, the results are declared. The yogas <note>planetary combinations</note> have been described according to their positions in the signs. Now, I shall describe the combinations for asceticism for the benefit of the learned. When four or more powerful planets occupy a single house <note>astrological sign</note>, the person born becomes an ascetic, provided these planets are strong.

If the Sun, Saturn, Mercury, Jupiter, and Mars are together, or if the Sun, Moon, Mercury, and Mars are in one house, the person becomes a practitioner of austerities. If Saturn is in the Moon's sign and is aspected by other planets, the same result follows.

When Mars, the Sun, Mercury, and Jupiter are combined, or when Mercury, Saturn, and Jupiter are together, or when the Sun, Mercury, and Mars are joined, men are initiated into religious orders.

By the combination of the Sun, Mars, Saturn, and Mercury; or Mars, Saturn, Moon, and Mercury; or the Sun and Mercury together with others, the person becomes a lord of ascetics.

When the Moon, Sun, Mars, Saturn, and Venus are all powerful; or when Jupiter, Mars, Moon, Mercury, and the Sun are together; or when Mars, Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn are combined, these individuals become great sages.

Through the combination of Mars, Sun, Mercury, and Saturn; or Mars, Saturn, Moon, and Venus; or the Sun, Mercury, Mars, and Venus, men are born who are constantly engaged in spiritual efforts.

By the combination of Venus, Mars, Saturn, and Jupiter; or Mars, Jupiter, Mercury, and Saturn; or Venus, Mercury, Sun, and Mars, one becomes an ascetic.

Those born during the <term>Vanaya Parva</term><note>A specific division or period in the astrological cycle related to forest-dwelling or asceticism</note> are described today: 7. The Moon, Saturn, the Sun, Mercury, and Jupiter—if these are in one house at a man's birth, they certainly make that man a great practitioner of penance. 14. Where Mars, Mercury, the Sun, and Venus are gathered, they do not produce their usual worldly fruits, but instead ripen into the life of a wandering mendicant.

# On the Combinations of the Planets

The combinations of the planets are as follows: 15. When the Sun, Mars, Saturn, and Mars <note>The repetition of Mars suggests specific house placements or strengths</note> are joined by the Moon, the Sun, and Jupiter, or by Venus and Mercury, the person becomes one who dwells in mountain forests, covered in soot <note>original: "kajjalamayasah"; likely referring to an ascetic who smears himself with ashes</note>, and is completely detached from worldly ties. 16. If Venus, the Moon, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn are aligned with the Sun, Mars, and Jupiter, or if the Sun, Mars, Mercury, and Saturn are joined by the Moon, the person becomes a performer of sacrifices. 17. When Mars, Saturn, Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury are joined by the Moon and Mars, and these planets are strong, the person becomes a <term>Valkala-chira-vasah</term> <note>One who wears garments made of bark or rags, a specific type of forest-dwelling ascetic</note>. 18. If the Sun, Mercury, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn are all present in a single house, the person becomes a wanderer who finds joy in festivals and travels to many lands. 19. The Lord of the <term>Yavanas</term> <note>original: "Yavaneshvara"; referring to the Greco-Bactrian or Western astrological authorities cited by Indian astronomers</note> says that when many strong planets are situated in their own houses or in the houses of friends, the person becomes a king or a leader of many people. 20. Pradyumna and other masters say that if the Sun and Moon are in specific positions, the person becomes like a King of the <term>Yavanas</term> <note>original: "Yavanadhipa"</note>, possessing great wealth and power. 21. If the Moon is in the <term>Drekkana</term> <note>A ten-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</note> of Saturn and is aspected by Mars and Saturn, it makes the person a practitioner of penance. If Mars is in the <term>Drekkana</term> of the Sun, the person may have a defect in their teeth <note>original: "danta"</note> or be of a shorter stature. 22. When the lords of the birth are joined by all these planets, the person's life is shaped by these powerful forces.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Saravali, specifically focusing on the classification and naming of the Nabhasa Yogas (Celestial Configurations). It transitions from general principles to a specific list of the thirty-two varieties of these yogas.</meta>

<page-num>33</page-num>
<page-num>3</page-num>

->**The Chapter on Celestial Configurations**<-

No inauspicious result shall manifest above the influence of these configurations <note>original: "yoga"</note>; the ripening of fruits <note>The karmic results of one's life</note> is known through the total absorption of all planetary positions. This is the method described by the virtuous and cool-headed sages.

...seen by the heads of men at the rising of the planets. **Thus ends the Chapter on Celestial Configurations (Nabhasa Yogas), being the 4th chapter in the Saravali.**

The Lords of the Greeks <note>original: "Yavanatha"; likely referring to Yavanesvara or the tradition of Greco-Indian astrology</note> have described these Celestial Configurations in great detail. These configurations do not depend on the planetary periods <note>term: "Grahadasa" (the timing system where specific planets rule certain years of life)</note>, but rather exert their influence throughout the entirety of a person's life.

The names of these configurations are: **The Boat** (Nauka), **The Peak** (Kuta), **The Noose** (Pasha), **The Triangle** (Shringataka), **The Thunderbolt** (Vajra), **The Lotus** (Kamala), **The Lute** (Veena), **The Pestle** (Musala), **The Cistern** (Vapi), **The Plough** (Hala), **The Sacrificial Post** (Yupa), **The Ocean** (Samudra), and **The Wheel** (Chakra).

Furthermore: **The Garland** (Mala), **The Serpent** (Sarpi), **The Banner** (Vajra), **The Field** (Kedara), **The Mace** (Gada), **The Bird** (Vihaga), **The Yoke** (Yuga), **The Cart** (Shakata), **The Trident** (Shula), **The Spear** (Shakti), **The Barley Corn** (Yava), **The Reed** (Nala), and **The Sphere** (Gola).

The entire world of moving and unmoving beings is governed by these proclaimed configurations. Those known as the **Support Configurations** (Ashraya Yogas) are specifically named as the Pestle, the Rope, and the Reed. The Sphere, the Yoke, the Trident, the Field, and the Mace are among those designated by the ancient teachers as the **Lute Configurations**.

The **Serpent** and **Garland** configurations are determined by the signs of the zodiac. The **Shape Configurations** (Akriti Yogas) have been explained by the wise followers of the Sun God <note>original: "Savitra," referring to the solar lineage of astronomers</note>.

One born under a **Support Configuration** (Ashraya Yoga) will be wealthy and obtain happiness even if born in humble circumstances. However, if these are mixed with other qualities, the results of the **Trident** (Shula) and similar configurations may be diminished. Those born under the **Shape Configurations** (Akriti Yogas) are generally dear to kings, fortunate, and enjoy a life of happiness.

<summary>This page lists the thirty-two Nabhasa Yogas (Celestial Configurations) from the Saravali, explaining that these specific planetary patterns influence a person's entire life regardless of timing cycles. It categorizes them into groups like Support and Shape configurations, noting their general auspicious or inauspicious effects.</summary>

<keywords>Saravali, Nabhasa Yoga, Vedic Astrology, Kalyanavarman, Yavanatha, planetary configurations, Akriti Yoga, Ashraya Yoga</keywords>

By the presence of benefics <note>gentle planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> in other houses, one enters into service. Men born under these various numerical combinations are in every way destitute. (9) Sometimes by their own nature, and sometimes through these very diverse combinations, they become endowed with results. In some cases, one is a knower of happiness, following their own will in the *Phāvya* <note>likely a corruption of 'Pāśa' or 'Vāpī' yoga</note> combination, depending on the strength of the planets. (10)

When the seven planets are situated in the *Kendras* <note>the four angular houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th</note> starting from the *Hora* <note>the Ascendant or Lagna</note>, the *Sragdala* <note>Garland-branch</note> combinations are formed. These were not created as part of the *Chakra* <note>the zodiacal wheel</note> by all the *Yavanendras* <note>original: "Yavanendra" - literally 'Lords of the Greeks,' referring to the ancient Greek masters of astrology whose techniques were integrated into Indian Jyotiṣa</note>. (11)

When the seven planets are positioned in the *Kendras* starting from the *Lagna* <note>the rising sign</note>, these are considered the primary combinations by the ancient teachers. (11) When the seven planets are located in places other than the *Kendras*, it is said to result in one who lives for wealth. (12) When all seven planets are situated in the *Kendras* but restricted to only two signs, they are known by the name *Graha* <note>Planetary</note> combinations. (13)

When benefics are in the *Lagna* and the 7th house, and malefic planets <note>harsh planets like Mars or Saturn</note> are in the 4th and 10th, a birth occurs in the *Srak* <note>Garland</note> combination. In the reverse position, it is the *Vajra* <note>Thunderbolt</note> combination. If they are mixed, and the malefic planets are situated outside, it is the *Yava* <note>Barley-grain</note> combination. (14)

When the seven planets are in the *Lagna*, the 4th, and the 10th, it is the *Shringataka* <note>Triangle/Water-nut</note> combination. (32) When the planets are in the trines <note>1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note>, it is called the *Samudra* <note>Ocean</note> combination. (15)

When the planets are in the *Lagna* and the 7th house, separated by other signs, it becomes the *Jhala* <note>Waterfall</note> combination. In the same way, if the planets are in the 4th and 10th houses, it becomes the *Jaladhi* <note>Sea</note> combination. (16) Thus, these twenty varieties have been described by me. In these combinations, the strength of the planets must be considered. (17)

When the planets are in dual, fixed, or movable signs respectively in the *Kendras*, the *Nala* <note>Reed</note>, *Musala* <note>Pestle</note>, and *Rajju* <note>Rope</note> combinations are formed. When benefics and malefics are in the *Kendras*, the *Mala* <note>Wreath</note> and *Sarpa* <note>Serpent</note> combinations—known as the *Dala* <note>Branch</note> yogas—occur. (18) When planets are situated in a single house...

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Nabhasa Yogas (celestial configurations) in Vedic astrology, specifically focusing on the Sankhya (numerical) and Akriti (geometric) varieties. The text transitions from naming the patterns to describing their specific life outcomes.</meta>

# On the Results of the Numerical Configurations

The configurations known as the **Numerical Yogas** <note>Sankhya Yogas: configurations based on the number of zodiac signs occupied by the seven visible planets</note>—specifically those named **Gola, Gada, Shakata, Hala, and Kedara**—are now explained. I shall describe the results of these configurations in the proper order, as they were spoken by the ancient sages. || 19 ||

Wise astrologers should consider these results as being highly effective and potent throughout all the planetary periods <term>Dashas: time cycles in a person's life ruled by specific planets</term> of a person's life. || 20 ||

### The Results of Specific Patterns

Those born under the **Gada configuration** <note>Gada: 'The Club'—when all planets occupy two adjacent quadrants</note> will be:
*   **Artisans** or those who earn a living through manual skill <note>original: "shilpajivi"</note>
*   Possessed of wealth and fine physical features
*   Inclined toward sacrifice and famous
*   Yet they may also be stingy, though physically strong. || 21 ||

Those born under the **Shakata configuration** <note>Shakata: 'The Wagon'—when planets are placed in the 1st and 7th houses</note> are:
*   Often distressed by debts or bonds
*   Prone to being cheats or gamblers
*   Destitute and influenced by cruel planets
*   Friends with the wicked, and may even dwell in mountain caves <note>This likely refers to living in isolation or away from civilized society</note>. || 22 ||

Those born under the **Hala configuration** <note>Hala: 'The Plow'—when planets are arranged in a triangular fashion across specific houses</note> are:
*   Abandoned by their own kinsmen
*   Gluttonous and fond of manual labor
*   Possessed of wisdom and patience
*   In the middle part of their lives, these men become fortunate and long-lived, though they may suffer from physical ailments. || 23 ||

Those born under the **Karmuka configuration** <note>Karmuka: 'The Bow'</note> are:
*   Cruel and devoted to gambling or wandering in forests
*   Generally devoid of good fortune in the world. || 24 ||

### The Half-Moon and Other Patterns

Those born in the **Ardhachandra configuration** <note>Ardhachandra: 'The Half-Moon'—when planets occupy seven consecutive houses</note> are:
*   Fortunate and commanders of armies
*   Possessed of delicate bodies, yet strong in leadership
*   Adorned with ornaments of gems and gold. || 25 ||

Those born under the **Khala configuration** <note>Khala: 'The Thresher' or 'The Wicked'</note> are:
*   Brave and fortunate in their youth
*   Possessed of healthy bodies
*   However, they become devoid of fortune as time passes, often coming into conflict with wicked people. || 26 ||

Those born under the **Yama configuration** <note>Yama: 'The Twin' or 'The Restrainer'</note> are:
*   Devoted to labor, discipline, and auspicious deeds
*   In the middle of their lives, they achieve their goals through their own offspring
*   They are charitable and possess stable wealth throughout their lives. || 27 ||

Those born under the **Vapi configuration** <note>Vapi: 'The Pond'</note> are endowed with virtues... <insert>and are steady in their wealth</insert>

It emphasizes that these effects persist throughout a person's entire life rather than just during specific planetary periods.</summary>

Possessed of great intelligence but often entering into states of intoxication, these are farmers or laborers. Though they may be weary or anxious, they are blessed with sons and are devoted to the god Rudra <note>a fierce form of Shiva</note>; such are the men who exist in this world. **24**

With a passionate nature, this man becomes a lord of men whose feet are illuminated by the jewels in the crowns of bowing kings. He becomes a ruler of the earth, a conqueror who is never defeated, born under this royal combination. **25**

Possessing great wealth and enjoying vast pleasures, they are also renowned for their priestly wisdom and universal fame. **26** These men are of steady mind and are known for their virtuous and cool-headed commands. **28** Though living a simple village life, they are free from attachment, devoted to truth, and endowed with purity.

# The Effects of the Sun in the Signs

<margin>The Sun in Taurus</margin>
...fond of music and instrumental performance, and virtuous. When the **Sun** <note>original: "Divasakara," literally 'the day-maker'</note> is in the sign of the Bull <note>Taurus</note>, the person is skillful in their work but fearful of water. || 3 ||

<margin>The Sun in Gemini</margin>
Intelligent, possessing a prominent or handsome physique, truthful and virtuous, and widely renowned. <margin>The Sun in Leo</margin>
Devoted to the service of kings, gods, and teachers. A man who is distinguished, a knower of the law, and a wanderer. || 5 || Energetic, courageous, and famous; a devotee of the gods who possesses a treasury. Grave and of steady temperament, he is a protector of his people and a gatherer of wealth. When the **Sun** is situated in the Lion <note>Leo</note>, the man born becomes renowned, a conqueror of women, high-minded, and a knower of scripts and languages. He is powerful and physically robust. || 6 ||

<margin>The Sun in Virgo</margin>

Fond of music and poetry, and established in his community. When the **Sun** is in the Maiden <note>Virgo</note>, the man born is a scholar with a yellow-hued complexion. || 7 || He may have a body marked by scars or signs of travel in foreign lands, and may at times engage in lowly pursuits...

In the acquisition of women, there is a great burden of sorrow, which becomes like the quality of a wound from a weapon upon a weapon <note>original: "shastra-shastra-kshata-gunah"; implying a compounding of pain or double injury</note>. If the Lord of the Day <note>the Sun</note> is in the House of Leo and is aspected by Mars, the person possesses brilliance, courage, valor, and gains wealth and fame in battle, yet remains constantly anxious. If the Lord of the Day is aspected by Mercury, the person gains from writing and books, but suffers from diseases of the hands; such a man becomes thoughtful and possesses a wise intellect. If the Lord of the Day in the House of Leo is aspected by Jupiter, he becomes a minister to a king with many enemies and friends, possessing beautiful eyes and a lovely appearance, yet he may also be somewhat distressed or impaired.

One born when the Moon is in Aries and is aspected by Mars is seized by diseases, involved in the quarrels of others, and possesses a body deprived of wealth; such a person is unsuccessful. (2)

One born when the Moon is in Aries and is aspected by Mercury becomes a rogue of cruel nature, intent on seizing the houses of others, and of a crooked disposition. (3)

Now, the Moon in Taurus <term>Gavi</term><note>Literally "in the cow," referring to the sign of Taurus</note>:
The person is brave, reddish in complexion, free from illness, and sinks into thought <note>or "is immersed in his goals"</note>; he possesses hair, is an archer, and is of a famous character. He is lovely, the father of daughters, a knower of the proper times for the wise, and moves with the grace of a swan. When the Moon is in Taurus and is beheld by Mars <term>Koniputra</term><note>The son of the Earth</note>, he is full of passion and clear in his desires. He performs deeds and works that are difficult, and possesses wealth in both bipeds <note>servants</note> and quadrupeds <note>cattle</note>. He is industrious and becomes a protector of pastures.

When the Moon in Taurus is beheld by Mercury <term>Budha</term>, he is like a person who has the eyes of a prostitute <note>observant/alluring</note>, is knowledgeable in the arts, and is dear to people. He steals the hearts of women and is honored among them; he performs auspicious deeds and acts like a courtesan <note>in charm or diplomacy</note>. When the Moon is thus placed and beheld by the Sun, he is like a swan among living beings, and Mercury's gaze makes him a man of many virtues and great undertakings.

When the Moon in Taurus is beheld by Jupiter <term>Guru</term>, he is endowed with steady sons and a wife, is devoted to his mother and father, and is exceedingly clever. He is righteous and a vessel of knowledge. When the Moon in Taurus is beheld by Venus <term>Siti</term>, he possesses ornaments, drinks, forests, perfumes, garlands, and fine clothes. He is an enjoyer of pleasures and a consumer of wealth. When the Moon in Taurus is beheld by Saturn <term>Ravisa</term><note>Son of the Sun</note>, he is devoid of righteousness, equal to others in status, and performs deeds for the sake of women; he is said to be one who works for his own ends.

One born in the **first half** <note>the first 15 degrees of the sign</note> brings happiness to the mother without delay. However, if born in the **latter half** of **Taurus**, there is separation from the father; the person may have a defect in the eye <note>literally "one-eyed"</note> or suffer from eye ailments.

->Thus ends the section on Taurus.<-

One born with the Moon in **Gemini** is a singer of the *Sama Veda* <note>the Veda of melodies and chants</note>, an expert in the arts of love and poetry, and a seeker of pleasures. With the mark of a fish on the hand <note>an auspicious sign in palmistry</note>, they possess great merit and enjoy worldly comforts, though they may be prone to worry and have prominent veins. Such a person is handsome, blessed with good fortune, devoted to the words of priests and scholars, yet easily influenced by women. They may suffer from bodily ailments, be the youngest among siblings, and excel in trade or commerce.

If **Saturn** aspects <note>looks upon or influences</note> the Moon in Gemini, the person becomes troubled or distressed; they do not find peace. 

If **Jupiter** aspects the Moon in Gemini, the person becomes a teacher of sciences and sacred scriptures <term>Vidyashastra-acharya</term>, renowned for speaking the truth and possessing incomparable qualities. The Moon, when influenced by Jupiter, makes a person eloquent and wise.

If the Moon is in Gemini and influenced by **Mars**, the person is exceedingly brave, yet calm, possessing excellent vehicles, manliness, and great wealth. 

If **Mercury** aspects the Moon in Gemini, the person is highly intelligent, invincible, and steady. They receive honors from kings or the government.

If **Jupiter** again influences this position, the person is a famous teacher of the sciences, truthful, and a master of speech. They are blessed with excellent sons, knowledge of the scriptures, and high honors.

Salutations to the Divine. <note>original: "He Ram Sih," a common invocation to Rama or the Divine at the start of a manuscript.</note> When the Moon resides in the sign of **Virgo** <term>Kanyā: the sixth sign of the zodiac, associated with the maiden</term>, the person born will have long, dangling arms and a beautiful, graceful body. They are inclined toward love and devotion, yet may possess a skeptical or atheistic disposition <note>original: "nāstika"</note>. They are skilled in the arts and sciences, possess a prominent nose, and speak pleasant words. Such a person is steady, wise in their actions, and often interested in the affairs of others. They are destined to enjoy good fortune and prosperity. 1.

If the Moon is in Virgo and influenced by the Sun, the individual will be born with a disciplined nature, yet may be prone to physical weakness or a lack of vitality. 2.

When the Moon in Virgo is influenced by Mars, the person becomes a master of crafts and architecture <term>Shilpāchārya: a master teacher of arts, crafts, or architecture</term>. They are wealthy, well-educated, very wise, and deeply passionate. 3.

Those born with the Moon in Virgo influenced by Mercury are known by the experts of physical laws to be skilled in debate and conflict resolution. They are charming, humble, and exceptionally clever in their professional pursuits. 4.

If the Moon in Virgo is influenced by Jupiter, the person is exceptionally steady, possesses a hands-on approach to work, and is highly respected by their community. 5.

When the Moon in Virgo is influenced by Venus, the individual enjoys various ornaments and pleasures. They are free from sin, possess great strength, and are dedicated to the service of the divine. 6.

If the Moon in Virgo is influenced by Saturn, the person may have a fierce or harsh appearance, a cruel face, and may struggle with poverty or illness. They are prone to anger, yet remain courageous in their actions. Such a person may be dominated by their spouse, have a frail body, and be devoted to the worship of gods and ancestors, despite their hardships.

In **Aquarius**:

Whether in a low, high, or friendly position, the body is fashioned for the work of others. The Moon makes one cruel, or even a friend, according to the nature of the Earth <insert>5 8</insert>.

The sequence of calculations <note>original: "gaṇanākrama"</note> 1. Having spoken of the strength of the signs and the strength of the lords of the signs, one should consider the results of the Moon's sign when it is endowed with strength; otherwise, the results are considered powerless. 2. Now, the results of the signs for the Sun and Moon. In a portion <note>term: "aṃśa" (a divisional portion of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuned predictions)</note> of Mars, the Moon produces a person who is very bold, fierce, and engaged in sinful deeds, yet capable of penance; in appearance, they are like a Kinnara <note>note: a mythical celestial musician, often depicted as half-human</note>. With the Sun in Aries, one is like a ram, wandering and brave, but if the planet is in its debilitation <note>term: "nīca" (the zodiacal point where a planet is at its weakest)</note>, the person is prone to mental distress and should be propitiated through worship. In a portion of Venus, one is influenced by the strength of Venus, achieving success, status, wealth, and happiness. Through Mercury, the person becomes a protector, or perhaps fickle like a wave, yet possessing a beautiful body. Through Jupiter, one becomes famous, very learned, and honorable, though perhaps pursuing the wives of others or following the desires of others for the sake of wealth. 2. Through the Sun, one becomes a great fool, a wanderer, yet fond of chanting mantras and drinking. Through Saturn, one is associated with the qualities of the unchaste and is surrounded by the wicked. 3. Thus ends the results of the influence of the white planet <note>note: Venus</note>.

In a portion of Mercury, through the influence of Mercury, one becomes a craftsman or an artist. Through the Moon, one is a poet or a singer. Through the Sun, one is famous, great, and knowledgeable in the essence of music. One possesses beautiful eyes and a gentle smile. Through Mars, one is base, fierce, and terrifying like the Sun. 1. Through the Moon, one gains an understanding of the meaning of the scriptures <note>term: "shāstra" (sacred sciences or treatises)</note> and poetry, possesses a refined intellect, and is led toward the various arts.

# 44

-> The Moon in the Sun's portion <-

**Thus ends the results for Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon, often associated with karmic influence</note> in Gemini.**

If the Moon is in its own subdivision and aspected by the Sun, the person is sinful yet possesses a comfortable body and physical form. They may seize the wealth of others, yet remain skillful until the end, even if afflicted by their own diseases. If aspected by Saturn, the person faces sword-wounds, imprisonment, or execution <note>original: "vadhabandha"; refers to severe legal or physical restraint</note>. 

If aspected by Venus, the person gains wealth through women, though it may lead to a lack of virility or masculine strength. If the Moon is aspected by Jupiter, it produces a person of noble character. If aspected by Mars, the person is devoted to unrighteousness <note>original: "adharma"</note>, sleeps excessively, and is liberated from vices only with difficulty.

**Thus ends the results for the period in Cancer.**

In the Sun's division, if aspected by the Sun, the person is prone to anger but gains fame and wealth through battle. Though sinful, they are fearless. If aspected by Saturn, they are prone to violence. 

If aspected by Mars, the person is wealthy, marked by scars, and serves a fierce ruler. If aspected by Jupiter, the person is humble, perhaps a commander of an army, or a leader among men. If aspected by Venus, the person is peerless among men, even if of humble origin. If aspected by Mercury, they are a thinker of divine matters, a scholar, and a possessor of great strength.

**The results for the period of Saturn in Leo.**

In Jupiter's division, if aspected by Jupiter, the person is extremely sharp and attains vast fame. They may be a king, a lord, or a yogi <note>A practitioner of meditation and asceticism</note> who is detached from worldly pleasures. 

If aspected by Mercury, the person is humorous, beloved by the people, a leader, yet prone to many anxieties; their speech is praised and they are famous everywhere. If aspected by the Sun, the person is afflicted by various faults and prone to negligence. If aspected by Saturn, they are powerful but hated by men...

...not devoted to women, and possessing a slender body. <note>This completes a sentence from the previous page regarding Jupiter's influence.</note> 1. **Thus ends the results of the aspects of Jupiter.**

In the **Saturnian division** <term>Sauramsha; the Navamsha or ninth-part of a sign ruled by Saturn</term>, if the Sun is the influencer, the person is honored but miserable, sickly, and burdened by heavy labor. Even their own wealth is lost. 1. Under the influence of the **Sun**, they possess little wealth, are afflicted by disease, and have a deformed body. Under **Mars**, they are intoxicated by pride, fond of assemblies, and devoted to the enjoyment of other men's wives. Under **Venus**, they possess an uneven temperament, and their children and friends are like enemies to them. 1. Under **Mercury**, they are devoted to sinful acts, possess a contemptible character, and are always wandering. Under **Jupiter**, they are devoted to their own duties, yet they are heavy or slow, and always characterized by a passionate or restless nature <term>Rajasam; the quality of activity, passion, and movement</term>. 3. **Thus ends the results of the Saturnian division.**

Regarding the **Best Division** <term>Vargottama; when a planet occupies the same zodiac sign in both the main chart and the ninth-part divisional chart</term>, the results of one's own division versus another's division are explained. If a planet is in its own division, the results are auspicious; if in another's, the results are contrary or undesirable. 1.

The results of the signs should be calculated at the end by the wise, observing the strength of the planets. Their obstruction occurs when the planets are weak, like a disease that resists medicine. In the calculation of results, one should consider the strength or weakness of the planetary positions. Following the teachings of the **Greeks** <note>original: "Yavanas"; referring to the Greco-Indian astrological tradition which heavily influenced early Sanskrit texts like the Saravali</note>, these results are declared collectively. 1.

One born under this influence is radiant, full of strength, humble, and a speaker of truth. Devoted to courageous deeds, with a handsome face, and possessing a firm command over their senses. 4. One who is not a dweller of the earth <note>perhaps meaning a wanderer or one of high spiritual status</note>, devoted to the wealth of cattle, goats, and sheep; when **Mars** is the influencer, the person is a traveler, very wealthy, and a powerful man. 5. One who is devoted to the company of virtuous people, yet afflicted by great diseases and lacking wealth. Or, one who is intent on seizing the wealth of others and lacks a stable position.

In the house of Mercury <note>Gemini or Virgo</note>, Mars makes a man like a limb of hell, one who possesses garments but cannot enjoy them. Through the anger of ancestors and others, he becomes one of great luster, a leader of men <note>literally "leader of teeth/forces"</note>, and courageous when Mars is situated in the house of the Lion <note>Leo</note>, which is the house of the Sun.

In the sign of the Moon <note>Cancer</note>, a man becomes one who fights many battles, of low conduct, a wanderer, and knowledgeable of various forms. In the house of the Moon, he is filled with sorrow, stained, and follows sinful practices. He abandons his family and is a son born outside the lineage. In the sign of Cancer, in the house of Mercury, he becomes shameless. He may be a famous minister of policy, a learned man, and devoted to renunciation. However, in the house of the Moon, he is entirely deprived of enjoyments when Mars is retrograde.

Through association with women, his wealth is destroyed; he is overcome by faults caused by women. In the sign of Cancer, he is sent to a house of sickness and becomes wealthy <note>or "afflicted by wealth/burdens"</note>. Like the sprinkling of water or the movement of the wind, he is a protector of the people, yet a killer. When Mars is in the house of the Moon and influenced by Saturn, he remains constantly fearful. In the house of Saturn and the Moon, he is like an elephant. He finds joy in authoritative measures, yet is ruined by friends and kinsmen. He wanders in forests with cattle. When Mars is in Leo, he is like an elephant among enemies. He is not auspicious for his mother, possesses a dull intellect and a harsh body, yet attains wide-reaching fame. Such is Mars in the house of the Lion, the sign of the Night-Lord <note>Note: The text here blends the attributes of the Sun's house (Leo) and the Moon's influence</note>.

(5) Saturn with the Moon in the house of Jupiter makes one like black collyrium <note>eye pigment</note>, a warrior in battle, and a protector.

# Page 5

One who has enjoyed life without disease is suddenly deprived of what is rare to obtain. When Mercury is in the face of the Sun <note>original: "tyānane... tigmabhiḥ"; likely referring to a conjunction or proximity to the Sun's rays</note>, the individual becomes a counselor to the King, yet is troubled by fierce enemies. In the house of Venus <note>original: "sitabhe"; referring to Taurus or Libra</note>, one is seized by various illnesses and afflictions. If Mercury is positioned there, it produces a person who is a performer, possessing a sharp intellect and capable of giving good counsel in all matters.

When Mercury is influenced by a cruel Jupiter <note>original: "krūro guru"; likely referring to a malefic placement or aspect of Jupiter</note>, it creates a leader of men. One born with this influence possesses a beautiful form and generates happiness for themselves. If aspected by the Moon <note>original: "sitakaradṛṣṭaḥ"; literally 'seen by the white-rayed one'</note>, the person becomes fortunate, charming, very humble, and a possessor of fine clothes and ornaments while Mercury is in the house of Venus.

In the sign of the Virgin <note>original: "kanyānī"; Virgo, Mercury's exaltation and own sign</note>, if influenced by the fierce rays of the Sun, the person becomes a protector. Under the influence of Venus, one becomes a person of great character, though perhaps distressed by grief on certain days. If Mercury is in a cruel sign, the person may be sickly or live a life of impurity.

Aspected by the Moon: Mercury in its own house, when seen by the Sun, makes a person sweet in speech, obtaining wealth from the King, a lord of men with charming movements, and beloved by the world. Such a person may live in a balanced city or face many disputes, yet they remain a child of prosperity. Mercury brings auspiciousness to all undertakings of living beings.

In its own house, if Mercury is joined with the Red Planet <note>original: "rudhireṇa"; referring to Mars</note>, it produces a person who is very dear to others but perhaps stained by cruelty, or one who gains wealth through the King. Such a person is fit to be among the assembly of a King's ministers, possessing great wealth and resources. However, if influenced by base associations, it may produce a person attached to low-born women. When Mercury is in a friendly sign and well-behaved, it brings success to all beginnings and a gathering of many followers.

### Jupiter in Sagittarius
One born with Jupiter in this sign is intelligent, possesses many friends, and travels to foreign lands. He acquires abundant wealth, serves as a minister to a king <note>original: "bhūsachivo," literally an advisor to the lord of the earth</note>, and is humble. He is a collector of fine things, handsome, and devoted to his children. He may be a leader of a guild or a merchant <term>Sārthavāha: a leader of a merchant caravan or trading corporation</term>. He is devoted to the gods and guests. He has many sons, is skillful, has a pleasant face, and is a singer. He is fond of living in temples, cities, and palaces. He is eloquent in speech and explanations.

### Jupiter in Scorpio
<insert>When Jupiter is in Scorpio</insert>, the person is devoted to religion through outward display <note>original: "dambhena," implying a sense of pretension or vanity in religious practice</note>. He is not secretive in his conduct. He is the head of a village or a builder of gardens and tanks, as well as an organizer of sacrifices <term>Yajña: Vedic ritual sacrifices</term>. He is generous in giving, a great man, and fond of offering gifts and jewels. He may be a minister or one who earns a living through various means. He resides in many countries and possesses a mind that understands various sciences and shrines.

### Jupiter in Capricorn
When Jupiter is in the sign of its debilitation <note>Jupiter is considered weakest in Capricorn</note>, the person possesses little valor and is interested in agriculture and fields. He follows low conduct, is foolish, and lives in poverty. He is a servant to others, lacks auspicious periods <term>Maṅgaladaśā: a favorable planetary period in one's life</term>, and is devoid of purity, affection, and righteousness. He has a weak body, is fearful, lives in exile, and is indecisive.

### Jupiter in Aquarius
The person is a backbiter, of wicked character, and engaged in base deeds. He is fond of dry or useless talk and is attracted to low-born people. He is greedy, afflicted by diseases, and though he may understand the meaning of scriptures, he is devoid of wisdom and good qualities. If Jupiter is in the eleventh house <note>original: "cha te guru," referring to the 11th position</note>, he wears heavy garments and performs various deeds.

# The Results of the Signs

A scholar of the scriptures, a clever friend, and one who is content among masters; such a person practices self-study <note>Sanskrit: svādhyāya, the traditional practice of reciting sacred texts</note> and follows the dharma of the community with a steady beginning. 

O King, one born under this influence is a sage, an expert in crafts, a teacher of practical affairs and rituals, famous and peaceful in this world <unclear>in the sign of Pisces</unclear> <note>original: "mīna"</note> or in the company of the noble. || 1 || Thus end the results of the zodiac signs.

### The Effects of Jupiter in the Houses of Other Planets

**Jupiter in the House of Mars (Aries/Scorpio):**
One becomes exceptionally righteous, achieving their desires and gaining fame everywhere with great enjoyment. However, if the Sun is also present, it may cause disease of the mind. Jupiter here makes one <unclear>unclear</unclear>. (1)

**Jupiter in the House of Mars (continued):**
It produces one who is very fond of laughter, skilled in poetry, possessing many gems, and blessed with a beautiful face that attracts women. (2)

**Jupiter in the House of Venus (Taurus/Libra):**
The Living One <note>Sanskrit: "Jiva," a common name for the planet Jupiter</note> situated in the house of Venus creates a person who is brave, a leader among men, endowed with humility and discipline, and wealthy. He will have obedient servants and a devoted wife. (3)

**Jupiter in the House of Mercury (Gemini/Virgo):**
When situated in the house of the Son of the Moon <note>Sanskrit: "Saumya," referring to Mercury</note>, Jupiter makes one prone to untruthful speech and contrary behavior, seeking the faults of others, and perhaps wicked or childless. Yet, it can also produce one who is intelligent, grateful, or even a bit deceptive. (4)

**Jupiter in the House of Venus (repeated/alternate):**
Jupiter situated in the house of Venus grants a mind suited for the enjoyment of homes, fine beds, clothing, perfumes, garlands, and various luxuries, though it may also make one somewhat timid. (5)

**Jupiter in the House of Mars (alternate):**
In the house of Mars, Jupiter may also produce one who is soiled, harsh, or low-born, yet bold and respected. It makes one calm and dedicated to offering service. (6)

**Saturn in the House of Mars:**
One who is firm of heart, clever, and a sharer of fortune, yet perhaps prone to renunciation or suffering from disease. Such a person receives honors from the ruler of men <note>a King</note> and possesses gold when Jupiter is well-placed. (7)

# On the Effects of the Planets

For women, fifteen <note>original: "panchadashabhikam"</note> positions create a heroic character. The planet Mars <note>original: "Kshitija," literally "Earth-born"</note> makes one wealthy and wise in the eighth house. Venus, when positioned like the king of gods, Indra, in the house of the Sun <note>Leo</note>, brings an abundance of treasuries and vehicles. When Mercury is present, one becomes a protector of the family. If Saturn is placed in the house of Dharma <note>the 9th house</note>, it bestows a physical form like that of the Sun. Saturn in the eighth house brings a desire for longevity and wealth through the establishment of a home.

When Venus is aspected by the Sun, it produces knowledge and a passion for learning. When aspected by Jupiter, it brings fame, the favor of kings, and incomparable, vast enjoyments. If the Moon is in the sign of Jupiter <note>Sagittarius or Pisces</note>, and Venus is present there, it results in a powerful royal status. Venus in Jupiter's sign also grants women a tall stature, a variety of virtues, and great intelligence. In the sign of Jupiter, Venus further bestows foremost wealth in cattle, land, and many offspring.

Venus, the son of Bhrigu, brings a wealth of ornaments and jewelry, as well as deep knowledge. When aspected by Mercury in the sign of Jupiter, it grants many vehicles and sons. If aspected by Jupiter in its own sign, it produces a person of great majesty, endowed with royal wealth, cattle, many sons, and many wives.

Saturn, when favorable in the house of Jupiter, makes a person constantly wealthy, endowed with all enjoyments, and prosperous. In Jupiter's sign, it brings the fortune of being a leader among men and having sons.

<margin>Saturn in the house of Jupiter</margin>

In the sign of the Fish <note>Pisces</note>, women become immensely wealthy and endowed with every happiness. When aspected by the Sun and Saturn in Jupiter's sign, it results in a son who is a great destroyer of enemies. When Venus is aspected by the Moon in Jupiter's sign, it creates a person with beautiful eyes, great beauty, adherence to their own faith, many sons, and good fortune.

-> **The Influence of Saturn** <-

**Thus ends the section on Friday (Venus).**

Now, the influence of Saturn: He leads to a mind inclined toward vice, a deceitful nature, and one who is secretive in his efforts; such a person is harsh in speech, impoverished, and lacks knowledge of the scriptures. He may be known as intelligent but is a wanderer who performs low deeds; he is hostile toward loved ones, cruel, a meat-eater, and sinful. <note>Verse 1</note> Devoid of virtues, he becomes a servant, childless, and performs improper actions; he is a thief of others' wealth and is constantly joined with vice. When Saturn is in the house of kinsmen, one becomes known for serving the wives of others; he is cruel, a jester, and though involved in many activities, he remains a fool. <note>Verse 2</note> He experiences much wandering and pain, is as cunning as a jackal, and is not a counselor; though attached to the teachings of his elders, he is always lustful. <note>Verse 3</note> He is a knower of deceptive mantras, a performer of hidden deeds, and skilled in crafts; he is fond of wandering in bonds and possesses knowledge of the winds <note>term: Vata-vijnana; likely referring to Ayurvedic humors or physical ailments</note>.

<margin>4</margin>
In **Gemini (3)**: One is endowed with great beauty and fortune, yet may be afflicted by diseases in childhood. Such a person is wise and learned <note>Sanskrit: *prajña*</note>, though they may be separated from their mother. They possess vast, deep knowledge and, even if surrounded by gems and a great family, they remain distinguished. They are known to cause separation or distance from their own kinsmen, yet in middle age, they become equal to a king, though sometimes deprived of the full enjoyment of their wealth.

In **the Moon (4)** <note>Referring to the sign of Cancer, ruled by the Moon</note>: One becomes an expert in the science of writing and scripts. They are inclined toward wandering and may lack steady conduct. Such a person might be troubled by women or live as a servant to others, often lacking their own steady position. They may engage in low acts and lose their wealth through anger or impulsive desires.

In **Leo (5)**: The body may be shaped like a bell <note>possibly implying a sturdy or rounded frame</note>. Such a person is honest and not deceitful, though they may seek the food of others. They are a protector, knowledgeable in sacred chants <term>Mantras: sacred sounds or phrases used in ritual and meditation</term>, and skilled in the arts and crafts. Though they may be lazy regarding their own physical labor, they are worthy of honor.

In **Libra (7)**: One is a benefactor to others and a bringer of joy to young women. They are dedicated to their duties and possess a disciplined mind. They are skilled in trade and bringing goods from foreign lands. Such a person is a self-made warrior, focused on their own actions, and may find a place in the assembly of the learned <note>literally: "the assembly of Brahma"</note>. They are virtuous, though they may be known as a lover of women or a seeker of pleasure.

In **Scorpio (8/9)** <note>The text transitions here into the characteristics of the following signs</note>: One is intent on traveling to foreign lands and may possess a difficult or "poisonous" temperament. They are capable of seizing the wealth of others. In childhood, they may experience auspicious protection, eventually becoming like a great king or a person of singular knowledge. They are inclined toward the performance of prayers and are mindful of their expenditures.

From that position, **Saturn**, when endowed with appropriate and elevated qualities, grants the seeker a long life and freedom from disease. When aspected by Jupiter, **Saturn** becomes victorious. When situated in its own house, **Saturn** makes one devoted to the pursuit of worldly objects, fortunate, and wealthy; however, through this, one may have few children. If situated in the eighth house, **Saturn** is likewise victorious. Thus ends the section on the aspects of the planet in its own house.

### Now, the Results of the Planets in the Houses

The **Sun** is the soul of all; by him, everything is known. Therefore, one should especially attend to this chapter on the Houses. If the Sun is in the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>The first house or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note>, the person is slow to act, prone to anger, fiercely powerful, and proud. Their vision may be weak, and their body lean. They are brave, patient, and yet secretive. If the Sun is in <term>Cancer</term>, they may suffer from cataracts or eye ailments. If in <term>Leo</term>, they are a leader of men but may lack sight. If the Sun should be in <term>Libra</term>, the person loses their father and suffers a loss of wealth. They may be associated with elephants or four-legged animals, yet remain poor, perhaps lame, and unhappy. If the **Sun** is in Aries, the person is valiant, possessed of great strength, but may lose their endurance. In the world, they are pleasing to the mind and receive honors from their king. If the **Sun** is in the fourth house, the person is deprived of vehicles and kinsmen, suffers from pain, and is knowledgeable in the arts; however, they may squander their paternal inheritance and possess an unattractive form, serving others for little gain. In the seventh house, one is devoid of wealth, a wanderer of forests and mountains, and a servant. In the fifth house, one is diligent and wise, yet lacks self-control and has a short lifespan.

### Thus ends the section on the Sun.

Regarding the Moon: If the Moon is in <term>Cancer</term><note>Sanskrit: Kulira</note>, <term>Taurus</term><note>Sanskrit: Vrisha</note>, or <term>Aries</term><note>Sanskrit: Mesha</note> in the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant or 1st House</note>, the person is foremost in wealth, enjoyment, and virtues. In other signs, one might be mad, lowly, deaf, distressed, or intoxicated; who among them does not lack skill?

When the Moon is in the sign of Taurus in the second house, the person is wealthy and has many sons. If the Moon is full, the results are complete; if waning, they are the opposite.

### The Effects of Mars in the Houses

If Saturn is visible, he is cruel, devoted to daring acts, stubborn, short-lived, possessed of equal valor, and has a harsh body; or if Mars is in the first house <note>the Ascendant or "Lagna"</note>... || 1 ||

In the second house <note>the house of wealth, "dhana-sthāne"</note>, if it is the home of Mars, the man is poor, prone to the hardship of drinking, and disrespectful to priests <note>Brahmins</note>. Such a person becomes hostile and is devoid of learning. || 2 ||

In the third house <note>the house of siblings, "bhrātṛ-ge"</note>, the man has a sturdy body and is a leader, yet he is filled with anger toward his brothers and enemies. He is known as a vessel for all types of battles. || 3 ||

In the fourth house <note>the house of home and relatives, "bandhu"</note>, a man under Mars is devoid of a pleasant voice, lacks water <note>perhaps referring to comforts or irrigation</note>, is troubled by his own kinsmen, and dwells in the house of others. || 4 ||

In the fifth house <note>the house of children and intellect</note>, even if he exerts effort, he is deprived of good fortune and friends. He experiences separation, lacks righteousness, is fickle regarding his wicked sons, and is sickly. || 5 ||

In the sixth house <note>the house of enemies and disease</note>, if Mars <note>original: "rudhire," literally 'the blood-red one'</note> is present, the man suffers from stomach ailments or fire, yet has a strong body and is quick but lazy. He is conquered by his own kinsmen. || 6 ||

In the seventh house <note>the house of marriage</note>, Mars makes one constantly follow others, addicted to the road <note>wandering</note>, and generally miserable. His body is tormented by the heat of separation from his wife. || 7 ||

In the eighth house <note>the house of longevity and secrets</note>, the man is prone to disease, short-lived, has a cruel body, and performs low deeds. He is restricted and tormented by the world. || 8 ||

In the ninth house <note>the house of dharma and fortune</note>, he is a source of joy to his enemies, intent on killing living beings, devoid of righteousness <note>dharma</note>, and cruel...

->**The Effects of Mercury**<-

If Mercury <term>Budha: the planet of intellect and communication</term> is in the Ascendant <note>The first house, representing the self and physical body</note>, the person possesses incomparable physical beauty, intelligence, and success in all undertakings. They are an expert in mathematics, a skillful speaker, and have a handsome form. 1. If Mercury is in the second house <note>The house of wealth and speech</note>, the person earns wealth through their own intellect, is a consumer of pleasures, speaks beautifully, and has fine eyes. 2. If in the third house <note>The house of siblings and courage</note>, the person is devoted to their village, takes care of their body, is well-known, skillful, surrounded by siblings, but may be prone to illusion and restlessness. 3. If Mercury is situated in the fourth house <note>The house of home and comforts</note>, there is a flow of wealth; the person enjoys vehicles, is always surrounded by attendants, and is a perpetual scholar. 4. If Mercury is in the fifth house <note>The house of children and education</note>, the person may have slow-witted enemies but many children; they are endowed with a balanced education and full of joy. 5. If Mercury is in the sixth house <note>The house of debt, disease, and enemies</note>, the person is always victorious in disputes and quarrels, but may be prone to illness. 6. If in the seventh house <note>The house of marriage and partnerships</note>, the person is lazy, has conquered their anger, is a constant orator, but may be prone to confusion. 7. Because such a person wears beautiful clothes and is of noble lineage...

...and one who delights in quarrels. He obtains a wife with many skills, but also great sins. (8) When the Son of the Moon <note>original: "Somaputra," a name for Mercury</note> is in the ninth house, the man becomes wealthy, learned, virtuous in conduct, an eloquent speaker, highly skilled in ethics, and very religious. (9) One who has Mercury in the tenth house possesses superior intelligence, is a knower of the law, successful in all undertakings, skilled, wise, courageous, and knowledgeable in various ornaments and arts. (10) In the eleventh house, Mercury makes a man wealthy, devoted to his duties, endowed with happiness, and a great enjoyer of life; such a man is blessed with a wife and children. (11) When Mercury is in the house of expenditure <note>the 12th house</note>, he makes a man miserable, cruel, and lacking in speech <note>original: "sagara-hina-vak," possibly implying a lack of eloquence or truthfulness</note>, often suffering from insults. (12) Thus ends the section on Mercury.

### The Effects of Jupiter

When Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> is in the first house <note>the Ascendant</note>, the person has a beautiful body, is strong, long-lived, firm in action, wise, and courageous. (13) In the second house, Jupiter makes one wealthy, fond of excellent food, an eloquent speaker, handsome, and a donor of fine clothes and auspicious items. (14) 

<margin>3</margin>
When Jupiter is in the third house, the man may be treated with contempt, is devoted to his siblings, but may suffer from poor digestion <note>original: "mandagni"</note>, and is often subservient to women or engaged in lowly work. 

<margin>4</margin>
In the fourth house, Jupiter provides happiness through family and vehicles, as well as various enjoyments. Such a person is superior, thoughtful toward enemies, and remains devoted to his family.

<summary>This page details the astrological outcomes for Mercury in the 7th through 12th houses and begins the descriptions for Jupiter in the 1st through 4th houses. It emphasizes traits like wealth, eloquence, and physical appearance based on planetary placement.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of planetary placements in the twelve houses of a birth chart, transitioning from the effects of Jupiter to those of Venus.</meta>

One born with Jupiter in the fifth house <note>the house of children and intelligence</note> is enriched with sons, happiness, and friends; he is wise, always possessing the essence of wealth, yet he may become one who renounces all his possessions. **5**
When Jupiter is in the sixth house <note>the house of enemies and illness</note>, a man has a weak digestive fire <note>original: "alpudaragni," referring to slow metabolism or poor digestion</note>, wanders about, and is prone to fever and laziness. However, he will be a learned man who conquers his enemies, though he may be dominated by women. **6**
Born with Jupiter in the seventh house <note>the house of marriage</note>, one is fortunate with a very beautiful spouse. He suffers from an excess of bile <note>pitta</note>, but is an eloquent speaker, a poet, a leader, and attains great fame and happiness. **7**
With Jupiter situated in the eighth house <note>the house of death and mysteries</note>, one may be a gambler or a servant. Yet, he acts kindly toward his own kin, is intelligent, and enjoys the pleasures of women. **8**
In the ninth house <note>the house of fortune and religion</note>, one is devoted to the rituals for gods and ancestors, is learned, and fortunate. He is handsome, happy, honored by kings, an eloquent speaker, and a leader versed in the Vedas and traditional scriptures. **9**
When in the tenth house <note>the house of profession</note>, all his goals are accomplished; he is enriched with every kind of happiness, a leader among his people, possessing pleasant speech and the qualities of a king. **10**
With Jupiter in the eleventh house <note>the house of gains</note>, one possesses immeasurable virtues, many vehicles, and children. He is successful and his brilliance is never extinguished. **11**
In the twelfth house <note>the house of loss and liberation</note>, one is lazy, hated by the people, and may be prone to sinful acts. Though intelligent, he is intent on serving others and suffers from heart disease or chronic illness. **12**
**Thus ends the section on Jupiter.**

Venus in the first house <note>the ascendant</note> makes one a person with beautiful eyes and face, like a woman, yet sleepy, timid, and slow. He has sons and friends, but is led by his passions. **Venus 1**
In the second house <note>the house of wealth</note>, Venus grants an abundance of food, drink, and luxury. Through laughter and dalliance, he acquires great wealth and a fine residence.

A weak Venus <note>original: "Hīnasita"; refers to Venus when it lacks strength due to its zodiacal position or unfavorable aspects</note> makes a man devoid of luster and wandering, knowledgeable in the male anatomy but devoted to the wives of others. It produces a lack of sanity and a disregard for the ethics of fortune.

When Venus is in the **fourth house**, it produces a man endowed with relatives, beauty, and a good family, possessing many vehicles and ornaments, with a charming intellect and great fortune.

In the **fifth house**, Venus makes a man a protector of fixed assets and special garments, possessing great intelligence, and a leader among the people of Magadha <note>An ancient kingdom in east-central India, often used in classical astrology to denote regional prominence</note>.

When the son of Bhrigu <note>original: "Bhrigutanaya"; a common epithet for the planet Venus</note> is in the **sixth house**, it is unfavorable for women and creates many enemies. Even if the man possesses wealth, he remains distressed and suffers from a lack of steady resolve.

In the **seventh house**, Venus produces a man with a beautiful wife, though he may be prone to quarrels despite his great wealth. He is generally endowed with good fortune and charm.

If Venus is in the **eighth house**, the person is short-lived, lacks strength, but becomes wealthy and possesses a steady mind. He finds satisfaction in his youth.

When the son of Bhrigu is in the **ninth house**, the man is provided with a family and great wealth, associated with virtuous people, and devoted to the service of gods and teachers.

In the **tenth house**, the son of Bhrigu makes men famous and highly respected, possessing wealth acquired through their own efforts and enjoying great renown.

When the son of Bhrigu is located in the **eleventh house**, he always produces a man who gains wealth through others, possesses many sons, and is constantly associated with happiness and prosperity.

->**The Effects of Saturn**<-

...one who consumes little food <note>original: "mitāśanaṃ"</note>, a son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> who is skilled in the comforts of the bed, and produces a man subservient to women. (12) **Of the bent-armed.** When [Saturn] has gone to a sign...

In a friendly sign, or in its own house, or in its exaltation, if Saturn is equal to the Sun, men become lords of kings. In the remaining positions, if the planet is afflicted or weakened, they become poor, driven by lust, dirty, and lazy. One with deformed teeth, a consumer of [low] things, devoid of righteousness, and a dwelling of injustice when in a cruel sign. (1)

One with a body like bronze, possessing wealth, vehicles, and enjoyments, when Saturn is in the second house. (2) One who is dirty, with a symmetrical body, devoted to the low-born, and a servant when Saturn is in the second. (2) <note>The text provides variant readings for the second house.</note>

A hero, devoted to giving and pride, with a steady intellect, and later possessing a body, wealth, vehicles, and enjoyments when Saturn is in the third. (3) One afflicted with a diseased body, without a livelihood or intellectual work, even if born of a good family; from childhood, one has a diseased body and is slow of hair when Saturn is in the fourth. (4)

One without enemies, without heat, dwelling in a trine <note>the 5th or 9th house</note>, the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> makes a man prone to madness, according to the sages. (5)

In the fifth or seventh house, Saturn [causes] one to be addicted to wine and of an uneven character. One who is very bowed, intoxicated, and arrogant when the son of the Sun is in a trine. (6)

In the seventh house, it causes ill-health, a lack of wealth, and the loss of friends. These are the effects of the son of the Sun; in a cruel sign, he brings sinful and low actions. (7)

In a cruel sign, one is overwhelmed by diseases and deprived of wealth in the eighth house. It produces one devoid of all purposes, according to the sages. (8)

One born with Saturn in the ninth house is devoid of wealth, devoted to violence, lacking in compassion, and stripped of happiness; he becomes one who torments others. <note>The ninth house usually governs fortune and religion; Saturn here is traditionally seen as challenging these areas.</note> 1.

When the son of the Sun <note>original: "Ravinandane" - an epithet for Saturn</note> is in the tenth house, the person becomes wealthy, cruel, a minister, or a leader of people; he is a leader of cities and villages. 11.

One with the son of the Sun <note>original: "Bhanusute" - Saturn</note> in the eleventh house possesses stable wealth, is brave, skilled in the arts, and free from disease; he is endowed with riches, people, and truth.

When Saturn is in the house of expenditure <note>the twelfth house</note>, one becomes deformed, fallen, foolish, <term>unrighteous</term> <note>original: "vidharmatto" - acting against sacred law</note>, devoid of virtue, and shameless; he faces great expenses and is defeated by others. 12.

Regarding the Moon: Malefic, mixed, or auspicious planets nourish the houses starting from the ascendant. In the enemy houses or other positions, the results are reversed, whether good or bad. Through planetary combinations <note>Yogas</note> and the strength of those combinations, when planets are aspected by benefics, or situated in their signs of exaltation and so on, the stated results occur without a doubt; otherwise, it is different.

**Thus ends the Chapter on the Houses in the Saravali.**

Now begins the instruction of the seventh chapter, named the "Results of Planetary Conjunctions," involving the Ascendant, the Moon, or the combination of two planets.

If the Sun and Moon are together <note>a conjunction in the same house</note>, the person is devoid of mother, father, and wealth; he is defeated by all, lacks intelligence, and is stripped of dignity and power. 1.

If the Sun and Mars are together, the person is deprived of the happiness of kinsmen, suffers from poverty, yet performs deeds that bring fame among great people. 2.

If the Sun and Mercury are together, the person is always deprived of friends and happiness, and remains defeated by others.

# The Effects of the Sun in the Houses and Conjunctions

When the Sun <note>original: "Dinakara," the Day-maker</note> is located in the **seventh house**, the individual becomes poor. 1. He may possess a physical body that appears wealthy, yet he is merciless and lacks humble conduct.

If the Sun is positioned in the tenth house <note>original: "Gagana-stha," standing in the sky/zenith</note>, it destroys enemies. 4. When the **Sun and Mars** are in the Ascendant <note>the first house or rising sign</note>, the person becomes greatly devoted to their father. 2. Such a person may be prone to anger, have an uneven or asymmetrical body, and be cruel, stubborn, and harsh. 5.

When **Saturn and the Sun** are together and lacking strength, the person is devoid of kinsmen and friends, and their mind is troubled. 6. The conjunction of **Mercury and the Sun** <note>original: "Jna-Surya"</note> brings great splendor and praise, though they may be susceptible to delusions. 2. However, if this occurs in a certain placement, the body may be wasted by separation from a woman, or one may suffer insults on account of a woman. 7.

The combination of the **Sun and Mars** <note>original: "Ravi-Rudhira"</note> may lead to diseases of the skin or blood; 3. their undertakings often fail, and they remain perpetually anxious.
<margin>:</margin> Such a person may serve high-ranking officials. 8.

When **Venus and the Sun** <note>original: "Bhrigu-nandana and Divakara"</note> are in the tenth house or the Ascendant, the person may suffer some physical impairment. 5. They may become a chatterbox, yet they are brave, intelligent, and gain wealth. 6.

If **Mercury and the Sun** are in the Ascendant, the person will be long-lived. 1. They are equal in policy and wisdom,
<margin>2</margin> famous, yet perhaps lacking a fixed home. 7.
The wealth of such a person is like that of a hero. When **the Moon and the Sun** <note>original: "Sita-kara and Vidhu"</note> are together, the body may be stout with prominent veins.
<margin>3</margin> If **Saturn and Jupiter** are present without the Sun, the person may lack a son or be devoid of sexual pleasure with a woman. When **Mercury and the Sun** are in the seventh house: 3.
<margin>4</margin> The person becomes a protector of the people, a lord of the earth <note>a king</note> renowned in the three worlds. 10. If **the Sun and Mercury** are in the tenth house and in a debilitated sign <note>a sign where their power is weakened</note>, similar results follow. 4.
