If the Moon is in the seventh house, the person becomes a man devoid of a wife. 3. If the Moon is aspected by Jupiter <note>original: "vidyaguru"</note> and Mars <note>original: "bhumisuta"</note>, one may have great fame for their work, abundant wealth, many people in their household, and be skilled in their duties, yet they may also be cruel. 4. When Mars <note>original: "kuja"</note> and Venus <note>original: "sukra"</note> are in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the point of the zodiac that was rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term>, one is devoted to the life of a merchant, a worker in agriculture, a lover of truth, and through wealth gained from music, they become free from distress and happy. 1. A person born under this influence may be devoid of kinsmen, children, and friends, suffering from mental anguish and pain. When Mars and Venus are positioned there, one becomes completely absorbed in various curiosities. 2. Because of a woman, he gains respect; yet because of a woman, he wanders from village to village. When Mars and Venus are so placed, the man may become devoid of proper conduct, behaving like a boy. 3. Possessing a body and intellect like that of a woman, yet endowed with learning, wealth, and material comforts, he may have a body marked by anger and be joined with sons. This occurs when Venus and Jupiter are situated in the tenth house <note>original: "vyoma," literally 'the sky' or 'zenith'</note>. 5. When Mars and Saturn <note>original: "arkaja," the son of the Sun</note> are in the eleventh house <note>the house of gains</note>, the person is afflicted by wind-related diseases <note>likely referring to Vata imbalances in Ayurvedic medicine</note>, possesses great anger, acquires wealth through battle, and hates his own mother. 6. Hated by his kinsmen yet intelligent, having friends who are false, and experiencing many calamities; when Mars and Saturn are in the fourth house <note>original: "hituka," from the Greek 'hypogeion'</note>, the person suffers from stomach ailments. 1. Afflicted by "female diseases" <note>likely reproductive or venereal ailments</note>, lecherous, devoted to sin, yet attached to truth and purity; a man born under Mars and Saturn becomes lowly. 2. Blood-stained, gaining wealth in battle, knowing great crimes, and punished by the army of enemies; if Mars and the Sun are in the ninth house, he becomes miserable as described. 5. If they are in the seventh or the tenth house, he becomes equal to a king, endowed with honor, wealth, and fame.

# Now, the results of planetary conjunctions according to the opinion of Dola are declared

-> <note>original: "॥ अथ दोलमतेन ग्रहसंयोगफलमाह ॥"</note> <-

When Jupiter and the Son of the Moon <note>Mercury; original: "shashisunvoh"</note> are in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</term>, the person becomes a thinker on the meaning of the sacred scriptures. **1** He possesses a beautiful form, a virtuous character, is devoted to knowledge, and becomes a master of his senses. **2** When Mercury and Jupiter are in the Ascendant, the person is honored for their righteous path. **2** He is all-knowing, <unclear>kind-hearted</unclear>, possesses a charming intellect, and acquires wealth through friends.

If Mercury and Jupiter are in the House of Children <term>Putrastha: the 5th house of a horoscope, governing children and intelligence</term>, the person will be one of good conduct. **3** If Mercury and Jupiter are in the Tenth House <term>Dashame: the house of career and public standing</term>, he becomes a minister or a leader. He is honored, established in his position, performs auspicious deeds, and is well-behaved. **4**

When Mercury and Venus are in the Ascendant, the person has a beautiful body, is a scholar, and is truly fortunate. He is a friend to the sages and a favorite in the assembly of the twice-born <note>the priestly or scholarly class</note>. **5** If Mercury and Venus go to the Second House, they produce a person who is a cause of progress, fortunate, and eloquent. They make him a protector of the path of counsel and a possessor of auspiciousness. **6**

If Mercury and Venus are in the House of Children, the person is filled with great joy. He becomes nourished with enjoyments and wealth, happy, and respected by kings. **7** When Mercury and Saturn <note>referred to here as "Bodhanasinayoh"</note> are in the House of Action <note>the 10th house</note>, he becomes wealthy and is considered a saintly man. Though he may use humble vehicles, he is an eloquent speaker and ready for all undertakings. **4**

<note>The following section describes more difficult placements involving Mercury and the lunar node Ketu</note>

One with a soiled body, sinful, and abandoned by knowledge, wealth, and vehicles is produced when Mercury is with Ketu in the House of the Moon <note>the 4th house or the sign of Cancer</note>. He becomes <unclear>fierce</unclear>, devoid of coldness <note>perhaps meaning harsh or hot-tempered</note>, detached from his own kinsmen, and mute. **1** When Mercury is with Ketu and Venus is absent, one becomes sinful, a traveler of illusions, devoid of essence, prone to poverty, afflicted by disease, and fond of vices. **1** When Mercury is with Ketu <unclear>in the house of gems</unclear>, the person is overcome by others. **3** He may be a worshiper of God but is foolish; though he may be a benefactor to others and a saintly man, he remains "soiled" <note>malina: perhaps referring to poor reputation or physical uncleanness</note>.

<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of planetary combinations (Yogas) and their effects on a person's character and destiny, likely drawing from the classical text Sārāvalī.</meta>

<margin>
<note>Abbreviation for Sārāvalī, a 10th-century astrological treatise.</note>
</margin>

When **Mars and Mercury** are in the tenth house <note>the house of career and public life</note>, the person is truthful and an expert in policy. If they are in a debilitated state, the person is still eloquent, successful in their undertakings, and wealthy. When **Saturn and Venus** are in the Ascendant <note>the first house, representing the self and physical body</note>, the person possesses a beautiful body and becomes a leader or king. They are skilled in virtuous deeds, born into a noble family, and equal to a king in power, having subdued all rivals; they are a protector of good people and friends.

When **Jupiter and Venus** are together, the person receives the title of a "Great Teacher-King." They possess "jewels" among women <note>an idiom for an excellent wife</note>, great wealth, and enjoy happiness and fame through women. If **Jupiter and Mars** are in the upper houses, the person possesses many vehicles and enjoys great power. With **Jupiter** in the sky <note>the 10th house</note>, the person is wise, free from fear, and possesses great influence. They are disciplined, highly learned, and of excellent character.

When **Jupiter and Mars** are in the Ascendant, the person is born into a respectable family, is learned, and possesses the essence of wealth, though they may occasionally be harsh. In old age, they possess discrimination, a healthy body, and pride in their penance; they are a friend to their kinsmen and a twice-born <note>term for the upper three social classes: Brahmin, Kshatriya, or Vaishya</note>. If they are in the fourth house <note>the "Patala" or bottom of the chart</note>, the person finds happiness at the end of life.

One may experience detachment from women despite having wealth, or be cruel and addicted to vices with an unattractive form. These results arise from the signs of **Jupiter** <note>Sagittarius or Pisces</note> and the inheritance of paternal wealth. When **Saturn and Jupiter** are in the tenth house, the person is favored by the lord of kings and becomes a compassionate ruler. Through peace and strength, they become the light of their lineage.

In the path of life, such a person is joined by a wife and enjoys the pleasures of her company. When **Venus and the Sun** are in the Ascendant, the person is a brother to kings but may experience grief. They gain wealth from friends, though their body may suffer from illnesses; yet, they remain compassionate and may sit upon a throne. They possess "jewels" of women, various comforts, wealth, fame, and victory on earth. When **Saturn and Venus** are together, the person is manly, successful in their aims, and gains through worldly objects. They are liberated from all dualities <note>conflicts or opposing emotions</note>, distinguished in their actions, and as steady as a mountain.

<margin>
Thus ends the section on the combinations of **Venus and Mars**.
Now, the results of **Jupiter** being placed in the angles <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> are written.
</margin>

# Chapter on the Determination of Fortune

->Leaving aside all other matters, one must specifically contemplate the fortune <note>Sanskrit: *Bhagya*; refers to luck, destiny, and the fruits of past deeds</note> of living beings. Without fortune, a person does not attain success despite their efforts. 1<-

Whether from the Ascendant <note>Sanskrit: *Lagna*; the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> or from the Moon, one should examine the place where the lords of the houses reside. If the Lord of the Ninth is endowed with strength, one should proclaim the results of fortune from that position. 2 Fortune is bestowed by the specific bird <note>A metaphorical term for a planet</note> that occupies the house. One must determine if that planet is strong or weak, as the results depend entirely on the condition of the house lord. 3 The sages have stated that when the house is joined by its own lord, it yields its full fruit. If the Ninth House is occupied or aspected by a planet, it may indicate that one's fortune will be realized in a foreign land. 4 If Mercury or Jupiter is situated in the ninth, or if a planet in the fifth house aspects it, and if the Lord of the Hour <note>Sanskrit: *Hora*; a division of a sign</note> is powerful, the man is said to be independent and self-reliant. 5

### Effects of Specific Planets in the Ninth House

When Jupiter <note>Sanskrit: *Devaguru*; the teacher of the gods</note> is in the house of fortune, the person becomes a minister; if the Sun is there, they are endowed with great splendor. With the Moon, they are a seeker of pleasure and handsome; with Mars <note>Sanskrit: *Bhauma*; son of the Earth</note>, they possess gold and are a protector of women. 6 With Mercury <note>Sanskrit: *Saumya*; son of the Moon</note>, one is wealthy and performs penance; with Venus <note>Sanskrit: *Sita*; the bright one</note>, one possesses mountains, vehicles, and great wealth. With Saturn <note>Sanskrit: *Saura*; son of the Sun</note>, one’s fortune is found in immovable property, service, and the possession of buffaloes. 7 If the Moon and Sun are together in the ninth, the person is a favorite of their father and mother, famous, equal to a king, and possesses many wives. 8

<insert>Through the strength of the planets, one attains sovereignty, gems, gold, and the essence of fortune.</insert>

If Mars and the Sun are present, the man is devoted to the service of others and lacks a family. 12 <note>The manuscript skips from verse 8 to 12</note> One who has Mercury in this position is elegant, handsome, fortunate, and possesses clothes, women, and wealth. Such a person is skilled in the arts and mechanical works. 13 One who is fond of festivals and social gatherings, and possesses cows, buffaloes, goats, and horses, has Venus, the Sun, and Jupiter in... <note>The text breaks off here</note>

If Jupiter is in the 9th House along with Mercury, the person becomes a leader among those who suffer from diseases, yet is happy; if Mercury and Venus are with Jupiter in the 9th House, the person is an artisan, of incomparable character, fortunate, learned, but of humble speech, and possesses a beautiful appearance.

In the <term>Vedantic<note>Referring here to the concluding portions of astrological wisdom</note></term> system: 20. 21. When Jupiter is in a sign with high strength and situated in the Ninth House, one achieves victory in battle. These are the results of these six planets; if aspected by others: 22. One possesses an excellent form, is virtuous, beloved by women, a ruler of the earth, and possesses great majesty. When Jupiter is in the Ninth House and aspected by all the planets: 24. <note>The manuscript skips verse 23, likely merging its meaning here.</note> One becomes fortunate, joined with a multitude of virtues, and a wealthy king. They produce a man who, though perhaps a lowly priest or of cruel disposition, loses his ill-repute and is cleansed of impurity.

If the lords of the houses are cruel but situated in the Ninth House and joined with auspicious planets: 27. They produce excellent men, joined with many virtues, provided they are aspected by benefics. 28. If all benefics are in the Ninth House and in retrograde motion, the person is famous, preeminent, and full of vigor. Such a person is eloquent and becomes a chief among kings. 29. When all the <term>wanderers of the sky<note>Sanskrit: Sakalagaganachara, referring to the planets</note></term> are in their signs of exaltation in the Ninth House, they produce a lord of gold and wealth. If aspected by both auspicious and inauspicious birds <note>metaphor for planets</note>, he is a ruler of men who destroys hosts of enemies, possessing a divine body and great fame. 29. <note>Numbering repeated in source</note> If the Sun aspects the Ninth House, it makes a man short-lived. He may suffer from eye diseases, yet remain fortunate despite being fond of quarrels. 30. If the Sun is half-risen <note>perhaps referring to an eclipse or partial visibility</note>, it makes a man afflicted by internal illness. He is fond of strife, has dental issues, and is prone to being cheated by others.

->**The Fruits of Fortune**<-

...combined with the Sun in the House of Fortune <note>The Ninth House, known as *Bhagya*, governing luck, ethics, and fatherhood</note> makes a man a possessor of wealth and grain. He may have a body prone to theft <note>original: "steyyayina-deham"; possibly implying a lean or stealthy physique</note>, with troubled strength and finances, yet he shall serve the King. **42**

When Venus is combined with Mars <note>original: "sitijna"</note>, he is devoted to the wives of others, argumentative, and cruel. He faces ruin on account of women, is fierce, and possesses a taste for battle. **43**

When the Moon is joined by a malefic planet <note>original: "papancha sashino"</note>, he is devoted to the wives of others but possesses distinguished wealth and happiness. When Mars and Saturn <note>the son of the Sun</note> are in the House of Fortune, they make a man destitute of his own kinsmen. **44**

Mercury combined with Jupiter <note>the Guru of the Gods</note> produces one who is highly learned, a scholar, and abundant in wealth. He is a sweet-speaker, a connoisseur of the arts, and becomes very great through his good fortune. **45**

Mercury combined with Venus <note>the son of Bhrigu</note> makes one famous, a learned scholar, and steadfast. He has a beautiful face, is capable, and is praised by the lords of the people <note>kings or leaders</note> in his destiny. **46**

Saturn <note>the son of the Sun</note> combined with the Moon makes a man pure, joyful, fond of his beloved, and clever. In the portion of the King, such a man has an irregular body and many enemies. **47**

Jupiter combined with Venus in the House of Fortune makes a man a beloved householder. He is long-lived, eloquent <note>original: "savakam"</note>, and endowed with various kinds of happiness. **48**

Thus, by these specific combinations, a skilled expert of discerning mind should proceed. He should declare the results according to the Shastras <note>sacred scientific treatises</note> and not merely from outward appearance. **49**

One who has attained the qualities of the planetary hosts <note>original: "taramaiganaguga"</note> serves the King and is illustrious. He is wealthy, obedient, and devoted, though he may be joined with some small disease from his Guru. **50**

A sinner <note>or one with malefic influence</note> is devoted to the wives of others, prone to living abroad, and has a clever but diminishing intellect. **51** He is devoted to jewels, gods, and the order of things... **52** ...with a scarred body or deformed...

->...makes a man endowed with happiness. (82)<-

If **the Sun, the Moon, Mars, and Mercury** <note>original: "Ravi-Chandra-Bhauma-Shashija"</note> are situated in the ninth house, that person becomes an excellent man, though one who travels to foreign lands, and is possessed of his own gold. (84)

When **the Sun, the Moon, Mars, and Jupiter** <note>original: "Surya-Shashi-Bhauma-Guravo"</note> have entered the house of fortune <note>The 9th house is often called 'Bhagyarksha' or the sign of luck.</note>, they make a man wealthy, skilled in sciences, handsome, and capable of protecting others. (85)

**Venus, the Moon, Mars, and Mercury** <note>original: "Shukra-Indu-Bhauma-Shashino"</note> produce a man who is clever and satisfied with his own wife; if these are in the ninth house, he is associated with all kinds of people and possesses a famous name. (87)

**Saturn, Mars, the Moon, and the Sun** <note>original: "Saurara-Chandra-Ravayah"</note> in the house of fortune produce a person who is a backbiter, deceitful, and <unclear>miserly</unclear>; yet if Jupiter aspects them, his actions become worthy of respect. (88)

If **Jupiter, Saturn, Mercury, and the Sun** are situated in the house of fortune, the man is devoted to the lowly, lacks dignity, and is always intent on seizing the property of others. (89)

**The Sun, the Moon, Jupiter, and Venus** <note>original: "Ravi-Chandra-Guru-Sitah"</note> going to the ninth house produce a man of good intellect, one who is righteous, virtuous, and steadfast. (90)

**The Sun, the Moon, Saturn, and Jupiter** <note>original: "Ravi-Chandra-Saura-Guravo"</note> residing in the house of fortune make a man highly devoted to dharma <note>righteousness/duty</note>, a devotee of the gods, one who honors gods and priests, and an enjoyer of life.

**Saturn, Venus, the Sun, and the Moon** <note>original: "Saura-Sita-Ravi-Shashankah"</note> make a man wealthy but <unclear>without offspring</unclear>; he is beloved, very steadfast, and handsome, yet constantly fond of quarrels. (91)

If Venus and the Moon are not in their proper places <note>original: "sthane na shukrendu"</note>, and if Mars, Venus, Jupiter, the Sun, and Mercury are situated in foreign lands <note>referring to specific astrological houses or signs</note>, they produce a wife of good character. 12. Whatever one desires, or even that which is unspoken, or the absence of such things, all this arises from the lord of the house of action <note>original: "karmadyamim"; referring to the 10th house of the zodiac</note> for a man. Therefore, by the very nature of this birth, one does not succeed without the influence of the tenth house; from that house, the destiny of men arises. He is endowed with all desires. In the chapter concerning the internal nature of knowledge, the effects of planets situated in the house of action <term>Karmastha: the 10th house, governing career and public life</term> relative to the Ascendant were explained. Now, I shall speak specifically regarding the planets situated in the tenth house from the Moon.

When the **Sun** is in the tenth from the Moon, it brings success in undertakings and a multitude of wealth. It produces a man of excellent reputation, a leader among men, endowed with the prosperity of youth.

**Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> produces a man devoted to courageous deeds, perhaps a bit restless, yet one who has attained his worldly objectives. Though he may be inclined toward harsh disputes, he remains a master of his own station.

**Mercury** <note>original: "Saumya"</note> in the tenth house produces a man who is learned, wealthy, a performer of many great deeds, a famous leader among men, and one who remains peaceful even when highly placed.

**Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> also, in the tenth position, brings success in goals, a righteous nature, and a collection of wealth. It produces one of excellent conduct, a minister to kings, and a master of refined tastes.

**Venus** <note>original: "Shukra"</note> in the tenth from the Moon produces a man who is fortunate, charming, and wise. He achieves his aims, is fearless, and is honored by rulers.

**Saturn** <note>original: "Saro/Shani"</note> in the tenth house produces a man who may be troubled by disease, perhaps lacking in consciousness or vitality, and feeling somewhat dejected or dependent.

When the **Sun and Mars** are together in the house of action, the person performs deeds associated with rulers, yet may be surrounded by the ignorant, or be someone who takes the lives of living beings <note>original: "pranivadhayarakto"; possibly referring to a soldier or hunter</note>, possessing great strength.

# The Effects of Planets in the Tenth House

If the Lord of Speech <note>Jupiter</note> and the Son of Venus <note>an unusual phrasing, likely referring to Mercury or a specific Venusian influence</note> are in the tenth house of action, they make a man a chief servant to a king. He becomes like a Brahmin, a teacher of sciences, and possesses great equanimity. 28.

When the Teacher of the King of the Gods <note>Jupiter</note> is in the tenth with the Sun and Moon, even if in a debilitated state, he makes the person a benefactor to others. He grants the person success, a stable position, and firm beginnings in all undertakings. 30.

Venus accompanied by the Son of Saturn <note>Saturn</note> produces a man who is a painter, a perfumer, or a physician. He creates a person who is <unclear>skilled in arts</unclear> and knowledgeable in his craft. 28.

When the Sun, Mars, and the Son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note> are situated in the tenth house, they lead a man to greatness. They produce an excellent man, equal to a king, who is pleasing to all people. 32.

The Sun, Mars, and the One Worshiped by the Gods <note>Jupiter</note> located in the tenth house make a man fortunate. He becomes a leader in the royal court and is endowed with great power. 29.

When the Sun, Mars, and Venus are in the tenth from the Moon, they produce a man who is constantly engaged in sharp or cruel work <note>original: "kshura-sajña", possibly referring to a barber or surgeon</note>, dependent on others, and full of great malice. 31.

The Sun, Moon, and Mars in the tenth house of cruel actions produce a man who hides his sins behind a veneer of good conduct. 32.

The Sun, Mercury, and Jupiter in the tenth produce a man who is learned, handsome, and fortunate. They create a person who is righteous and beloved by Brahmins <note>the priestly or scholarly class</note>. 33.

Mercury, the Sun, and Venus produce a man who is famous, beloved, and free from anger. They create one who is undefeated, adorned with good fortune, and possesses great equanimity. 24.

The Sun, Mercury, and Saturn in the tenth produce a man who is cruel, fickle, and lacking in character. They produce a person who suffers from injuries caused by weapons or fire. 25. The Sun, Mer—

If **Mars, the Sun, Venus, and Mercury** are together at the time of birth <term>Prasave: at the moment of delivery or birth</term>, they make a man a garland-maker, a goldsmith, or one who works with perfumes. 53. When **the Sun, Mercury, Mercury <insert>repeated</insert>, and Saturn** are in the tenth house at the time of birth, they produce a man who is devoted to the lowly but joined with righteousness. 54. When **Venus, the Sun, Jupiter, and Mars** are in the house of action <note>the tenth house</note>, they produce men who are devoted to righteous yoga, modest, possessing much wealth and grain, and always energetic. 55. When **Saturn, Venus, Mars, and the Sun** have gone to the tenth, they produce one who is addicted to cruel deeds <term>Krurakarma: harsh or violent actions</term>...

# Chapter on Livelihood

<margin>
Sara
Va
84
</margin>

Those born under the influence of the sign of the Bull <note>Taurus</note> in the tenth house become prosperous through trade in garments, cows, and grains, and are famous for dealing in water-based products, perfumes, and fine crafts. 75

Under the influence of the Twins <note>Gemini</note>, they become merchants of wealth, grains, and roots, or traders in fruits, grass, seeds, and beans. They may also find success through the arts and as messengers. 76

Under the Crab <note>Cancer</note>, men earn through women, wine, pearls, and ornaments; they may also be priests, travelers, or servants of kings, though some may follow sinful paths. 77

<margin>78</margin> Under the Lion <note>Leo</note>, they live by medicine, forest products, or working with iron. If the Sun is strong in the tenth, they are devoted to the service of the state and earn through grains. 78

Under the Virgin <note>Virgo</note>, they live as ministers to kings, or through the study of the Vedas <note>ancient sacred scriptures</note>, or as writers and messengers. They are skilled in the performance of sacrifices and the practice of medicine. 79

<margin>80</margin> Under the Balance <note>Libra</note>, in the tenth house, they possess great wealth through trade in water-based goods, or as merchants dealing in gold, chemical elixirs <note>alchemy</note>, and sacred chants. 80

Under the Scorpion <note>Scorpio</note>, they live by weapons, fire, or acts of cruelty. If the Water-Bearer <note>Aquarius</note> is in the tenth, they may be burden-bearers or live by the strength of their own arms. 81

Under the Fish <note>Pisces</note>, they earn through water-born products, pearls, or by managing reservoirs and shipping. 82

One should determine the source of wealth—whether from the father, mother, enemy, friend, brother, wife, or servants—by observing the planets situated in the tenth house or the lords of those signs. 83

<margin>84</margin> All these matters regarding one's livelihood should be considered through the strength of the planets and the houses. Thus, the ancient teachers have explained the various ways of earning. 84

Thus ends the chapter on Livelihood in the Saravali.

# Chapter on Deliberations

If the **Sun** aspects <note>The term 'aspect' refers to a planet 'looking at' or influencing a specific house in the horoscope.</note> a malefic Ascendant, the person becomes fierce, their wife is prone to anger, and they are cruel; yet, they possess wealth inherited from their father and become a minister to a king, endowed with great honor. (1)

If the **Moon** aspects the Ascendant, the person is subservient to women, fortunate, exceptionally handsome, and a great warrior. They possess a sweet disposition and, because the Moon is in the Ascendant, the man is characterized by gentleness. (2)

When **Mars** <note>original: "kuja"</note> aspects the rising sign, the man delights in courageous deeds and battle; he is learned, devoted to his duties, and possesses a moderate amount of anger. (3)

If **Mercury** aspects a malefic Ascendant, the man is engaged in the arts or sciences; he possesses a clever mind, achieves fame, and is endowed with honor. (4)

When **Jupiter** <note>original: "suraguru" - the teacher of the gods</note> aspects the Ascendant, men are devoted to self-study and the observance of rules; they are satisfied by their traditional learning, honored by the three worlds, and endowed with treasures. (5)

If **Venus** aspects the Ascendant, men are influential, beloved by women, fortunate, and possess complete enjoyment, wealth, and happiness; they are also physically beautiful. (6)

When **Saturn** <note>original: "mandra"</note> aspects the rising sign, men are burdened by diseases, have a rough or protruding belly, appear withered or emaciated, are unclean, and behave foolishly. (7)

In a harsh house <note>a malefic sign</note> or one's own Ascendant, all planets provide happiness according to their nature. Even an auspicious planet may give inauspicious results depending on the body <note>the physical constitution or house placement</note>. If the Ascendant is devoid of even a single virtuous quality, the man is abandoned by his own nature. One should always observe the ruler of the birth in the world. The Ascendant provides auspicious results when associated with the first planet <note>the Sun</note> or any other auspicious planet; however, if it is aspected by malefic planets, it is not auspicious at all. (10) All calculations are for the sake of the gods...

It concludes with general rules on how planetary nature and aspects modify the overall quality of the horoscope.</summary>

If the *Hamsa* yoga <note>A specific auspicious planetary alignment where Jupiter is in a quadrant and in its own or exalted sign</note> is present in the **Ascendant**, the person becomes a king. If the planets are strong, he attains power, is free from fear, and lives a long life. If all three <note>likely referring to the three natural benefics: Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury</note> are in the **Ascendant**, they produce the birth of fortunate lords of the earth. However, if malefic planets are present there, the person born is overwhelmed by disease, fear, and grief, and is rejected by all people. (15)

If malefic planets are situated in the fifth or ninth houses from the **Ascendant** and are aspected by other malefics, the person destroys his advisors <note>or ministers</note> and becomes a ruler of men and a husband to many women. Under the *Lagnadhi Yoga* <note>a configuration where benefics are in the 6th, 7th, and 8th houses from the Ascendant</note>, one becomes long-lived, highly distinguished, and enjoys many pleasures without fear. As stated by the Yavana kings <note>original: "Yavanadi Raja"; referring to the Greco-Indian astrological tradition</note> and others, such a person is born to enjoy happiness. (16)

When the seven planets—the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn—are in their respective houses, they yield results. However, the sages have declared that if a planet is in its sign of debilitation, or in an enemy's house, or has lost its strength, it is considered powerless. (17)

If benefics are in the house of wealth, they grant many types of riches. (3) If Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus are in the house of wealth, they bring prosperity; however, if Saturn is there by its own nature, it creates a lack of mental peace and destroys all wealth. <meta>This page continues the astrological discourse on planetary aspects, transitioning from the effects of Mercury and Venus on wealth to the indicators of illness and the birth of children.</meta>

# On the Consideration of Wealth

When Mercury is aspected <note>aspected: in astrology, this refers to the angular relationship between planets that allows them to "see" or influence one another</note> by Saturn, it creates a person who is a leader among men, yet one who also faces opposition or conflict. 1. If Venus is positioned in its own house and is aspected by malefic planets <note>malefic: planets like Saturn or Mars that were traditionally thought to bring challenges</note>, it does not grant wealth. However, if Venus is in a benefic sign and receives a favorable aspect, it truly bestows wealth and makes one like a king. 2. Thus ends the consideration of wealth.

# On the Consideration of Illness

One should consider the entire body; any part associated with malefic planets becomes afflicted with disease. Conversely, those parts associated with benefics are healthy; I shall now explain this further. To whatever extent the nine divisions <note>original: "navamsha" - a 1/9th sub-division of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuned analysis</note> are influenced by malefic aspects or are positioned in unfavorable signs, to that same extent one should predict illness in those specific areas of the body. 2. Furthermore, if Saturn is particularly strong and exerts a malefic aspect, it destroys the vitality of the person born. If Venus is aspected by Jupiter, it always grants children and royal favor. 3. If Mercury is aspected by a "death-inflicting" planet <note>original: "maraka" - a planet occupying specific houses that signify the end of life</note>, it causes disease and wasting; yet it can also grant status. One should determine the remaining effects based on the chapter concerning the "Houses" <note>original: "Bhava" - the twelve sections of the sky representing different life areas like career or health</note>. 4. Thus ends the consideration of illness.

# On the Consideration of Children

For those whose "House of Children" <note>the 5th house of a horoscope</note> is a benefic sign or is aspected by benefic planets, the birth of sons is certain; if the opposite is true, it is not so. 1. If the house is associated with benefic divisions or signs, a son will be born. If the lords of the Ascendant <note>Lagna: the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> and the tenth house are strong, they grant wisdom. If Mercury is aspected by benefics, the lineage is doubled <note>likely referring to many descendants</note>; if aspected by malefic planets, the results are diminished. 3. In a sign of Saturn, if the Saturnian divisions are aspected by Mercury or Jupiter, the person born may lack compassion. Even Mercury, if so placed, can produce a person of such nature.

<summary>This manuscript page outlines astrological rules for predicting an individual's wealth, physical health, and the likelihood of having children based on planetary aspects and house placements.</summary>

If the Ascendant <term>Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> falls in a sign or subdivision ruled by Saturn, and Saturn is also positioned in the fifth house <note>The house of progeny</note> while being aspected by other planets, the son born is a **duly adopted son** <gloss>8</gloss>. This occurs specifically when the influence of Jupiter is absent. Similarly, if the fifth house falls in a sign ruled by Saturn and is occupied by Saturn himself, while the lord of that sign is weak, the same result follows. 5. If the fifth house is a sign of Saturn and is occupied by Saturn, while being aspected by the Moon, the son is considered "purchased." 6.

If the fifth house falls in a sign ruled by Mars, and Saturn is positioned there while being aspected by the Sun, the son is one who has been **abandoned by his biological parents** and taken in by others. 7. If the Moon is in the fifth house in a sign ruled by Mars, and is aspected by the Sun, the son is **secretly born** <note>original: "gudhotpannah"; a son born of a woman whose lover is unknown</note>, provided no other planets aspect this combination. 8. If Mars is in a subdivision of Saturn and is aspected by the Sun, the son is born of a woman who was already pregnant at the time of her marriage, according to the words of the Sages. 9.

If the Moon is in the fifth house and falls in a subdivision of Saturn, and is aspected by both Venus and the Sun, the son is the **son of a remarried woman** <note>original: "paunarbhava"</note>. 10. If Mars and Saturn are in the fifth house, and the Sun is in a subdivision of Saturn, the child is likewise considered to be of varied lineage. 11. If the Sun and Moon are in their own subdivisions and the Sun is in the fifth house, the son is a **maiden's son** <note>original: "kanina"; born to an unmarried woman</note>. 12. If the same combination involves Venus, the son is described as **brought with the marriage** <note>original: "sahodaja"; born to a woman who was pregnant at her wedding</note>. 13.

If the fifth house is occupied by malefic planets <term>planets like Mars, Saturn, or the Sun that cause hardship</term> and is not aspected by any benefic planets <term>planets like Jupiter or Venus that bring grace</term>, the lineage is said to be severed or the son is of low origin. 14. Some teachers <insert>the ancient masters</insert> say that if the Moon is in a sign of Saturn and is aspected by Saturn, the children born will be of the same nature as the sign itself. 15. <meta>This page continues the technical analysis of planetary placements within the astrological houses, specifically focusing on the fifth and sixth houses which govern children and enemies.</meta>

# On the Effects of the Planets in the Houses

When <term>Venus</term><note>original: "Bhṛgostanaye" (Son of Bhrigu)</note> is situated in the <term>Fifth House</term><note>The house associated with children, intelligence, and past merit.</note>, and is joined by or aspected by other planets, the results are generally favorable for daughters, though an unskilled astrologer might misinterpret the timing of these fruits. ॥ 15 ॥ This is seen as equal to the strength of Indra himself.

When the <term>Malefics</term><note>Naturally harsh planets like Mars or Saturn.</note> are positioned in the <term>Fourth House</term><note>The house of home, mother, and happiness.</note>, even one who knows the Vedas may become merely a simple village-knower <insert>lacking deep wisdom</insert>. ॥ 16 ॥

<term>Mars</term><note>original: "Bhauma"</note> in the fifth house destroys children, whether already born or yet to be born. However, if this position is aspected by <term>Jupiter</term> or <term>Venus</term>, the harsh effects are mitigated. ॥ 17 ॥

One becomes destitute of wealth, people, and children when cruel planets reside in the fifth house; such a person becomes distressed. According to the teachings of <term>Vyasa</term><note>A legendary sage and purported author of many foundational Indian texts.</note>, if the <term>Son of the Sun</term><note>original: "Divasakaransune" (Saturn)</note> is there, the person will be afflicted by diseases. ॥ 18 ॥ Conversely, if the fifth house contains Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus together, the person will be wealthy and surrounded by good people.

Regarding the <term>Sixth House</term><note>original: "Ripubhavame" (House of Enemies)</note>: if it is occupied by <term>Saturn</term>, there is a fear of enemies and recurring troubles. <margin>If aspected by benefics, the enemies are subdued.</margin> ॥ 19 ॥

The number of children or enemies should be determined by the strength of the lords of the houses, as explained in the **Chapter on Astrological Houses** <note>original: "Bhāvādhyāya"</note>. One must follow the sequence of their strength. ॥ 20 ॥

When Venus, Jupiter, and the Moon are all present, or even two of them, in the <term>Kumbha</term><note>Aquarius</note> sign or in the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant or Rising Sign</note>, and are aspected by benefics, the person's nature will be heavenly and virtuous. ॥ 21 ॥

In this way, if the <term>Malefics</term> are in the Ascendant, the person's body may be frail and their lifespan short. If there is a conflict between <term>Saturn</term> and the <term>Sun</term>, the person will not find peace on this earth. ॥ 22 ॥

The strength of the house lord, the planetary aspects, and the specific divisions of the zodiac must all be considered by the wise astrologer. Even if only one planet is strong, it can change the name and fame of the individual. ॥ 23 ॥

If Venus is in the seventh house <note>the house of marriage</note>, the person will have many wives. If both Mercury and Venus are in the seventh house, the wives will be of the same caste. <meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary influences on wealth and social status, likely from a classical Jyotisha (astrological) treatise. The text focuses on the specific material outcomes and professional associations linked to different planetary configurations.</meta>

Through the longing for wealth, there arise garlands, ointments, and garments. || 34 || For women, thousands of beds and fires <note>likely referring to domestic comforts or ritual fires</note>, and through the brilliance of these classes of objects, wealth is attained. In the presence of intense heat and through the acquisition of excellence, there is an increase in wealth in the later stages of life. 35 Gold, coral ornaments, and rubies are acquired; thus it shall be. Through courageous acts and constant coming and going, and for those engaged in agriculture, there is buying and selling. 12 Generally, by the classes of those to be meditated upon <note>original: "dhyeyi-vargais", possibly referring to religious or meditative groups</note>, the day becomes one of throwing off heat <note>or radiating energy</note>. One should predict skill in the arts and a devotion to poetry, generally occurring in the seat of Rama <note>original: "asana ram", possibly a specific astrological position or a geographical reference</note>. 10 Through associations with city-dwellers, through various mixtures, and through distinguished efforts, wealth consisting mostly of gold is obtained. In the life of the father, the body is covered <note>with ornaments or honors</note>. 28 Through associations with courtesans and through the comings and goings of men, it happens for people. Generally, even when it is limited, it is so. 6 The production of pearls, jewels, and gems is entered into. 32 Through associations with city and sea crossings, and through the performance of fixed works <note>original: "sthavara karma", likely real estate or permanent labor</note>, there is wealth even for the lowly. Iron, donkeys, and buffaloes are abundant; when the son of the Sun <note>term: Saturn (Ravija)</note> is in the house of worship, it is well-known. 5 Thus is the direction of the fruits <note>term: Phalanidesha (astrological predictions)</note>; it should be spoken specifically by the gentle planets, and by the cruel planets it is diminished. For men, it is always a mixture of truth and falsehood. 40 One should indicate the day by the combination of the qualities of all the planets. That which is strong becomes the reality as spoken, while the weak is destroyed. 42 When there is a multitude of friendly planets, the result is as spoken; when in conjunction or in exaltation, it is somewhat more. In the interval of an enemy planet, it is without strength and accompanied by distress. 43 For men born under auspicious signs, through the signs of Gemini and others, and with the strength of the moments, they become protectors of the earth <note>term: Kshitipala (kings or rulers)</note>. Thus ends the Garland of Desires <note>original: "Kanta Mala", likely the title of this section or chapter</note> regarding the lunar days <note>term: Tithi</note>.

<note>Verse 44</note> ...the movement of the Sun and Moon. 44. When Mars is in the eighth house <note>the house of longevity and marital bond</note>, it leads to widowhood <note>original: "vaidhavyam"</note> for the woman; however, if Mercury is also present in that house, the person becomes a maker of thunderbolts <note>likely a metaphor for a powerful or destructive artisan</note> or a great leader. 45. If Jupiter, Venus, and the Moon are in the eighth house, the person is abandoned by their family and suffers from diseases. If these are aspected <note>viewed</note> by Mars, the person follows a path of constant obstacles. 49. If Mercury is in the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> and is not aspected by the Moon, the person does not know their own family lineage; if the lord of the house is weak, they are born of another's seed.

<margin>44</margin> 50. If the Sun and Moon are in their signs of debilitation <note>the signs where their influence is weakest</note> or in the Navamsha <note>ninth divisional chart</note> of Saturn, and are aspected by malefic planets in the seventh house <note>the house of desire/Madana</note>, the person becomes a slave to a great king, even if born in a noble family. 58. <note>likely a scribal error for 51</note> When the Moon is in the second house from the Sun and is situated between the Sun and Saturn, or if the Moon is in the seventh house from Mars, the person becomes physically deformed or lacks limbs. If a malefic planet is situated in the seventh house between two other malefic planets, the person suffers from chronic cough, asthma, or respiratory distress, and possesses a lion-like temperament. 50.

<margin>51</margin> 51. If the Sun is in the sign of the Moon <note>Cancer</note> and the Moon is in the sign of the Sun <note>Leo</note>, and they are joined by Saturn, the person becomes a warrior or a traveler. If they are in the sign of Saturn <note>Capricorn/Aquarius</note>, the person is a wanderer. 
<margin>52</margin> 52. If the Sun and Moon are in the twelfth, eighth, or sixth houses, and are not aspected by powerful benefic planets, the person becomes blind or has defective vision due to the imbalance of the bodily humors. If malefic planets are in the third and seventh houses and are not aspected by benefics, they cause the destruction of hearing <note>deafness</note> for those born under such a configuration. 52.

53. If Saturn is in the Ascendant and Mars is in the fifth, seventh, or ninth house, the person suffers from insanity or mental instability. This also occurs if the Moon and Saturn are together in the twelfth house. 54. Thus ends the summary <note>original: "iti sara"</note>.

## Chapter on Various Professional and Royal Combinations

The science of Royal Combinations <note>Sanskrit: Raja Yoga; planetary alignments indicating power and status</note> is distinguished by the specific strengths of the planets. I shall now explain those very combinations. When planets are positioned in their signs of exaltation <note>Sanskrit: Svoccha; the sign where a planet is at its maximum strength</note> or in their primary triangles <note>Sanskrit: Trikona; specific auspicious zones in the zodiac</note> and are situated in the tenth house <note>Sanskrit: Gagana; literally 'the sky', referring to the midheaven or 10th house of career</note> with full strength, even a person born in humble circumstances becomes a king or a lord of the earth. If five planets are thus positioned, they produce a supreme ruler; if fewer, they produce a leader equal to a king, but not a sovereign of the whole earth.

When Jupiter <note>Sanskrit: Guru</note> and the Sun occupy the tenth house or the ascendant, and are not influenced by cruel <note>malefic</note> planets, one should declare the birth of a king who is defined by his valor. Indeed, when Jupiter is in its own sign and in a quadrant, a Royal Combination is formed that grants liberation and authority. Those born under these influences, even if from a lowly family, proceed to honor and leadership, just as the ancient masters of the scriptures have stated.

With the Sun, Saturn, Mars, and Jupiter <note>Sanskrit: Ravi, Saura, Bhauma, Guru</note> all in their signs of exaltation, or even three of them so placed, various lords of the world are shown to be born. With two such planets in their own houses and the Moon also strong, one becomes a ruler of sixteen provinces. Even one born of a low family reaches the heights of power, just as water flows into the sea.

When the rising sign <note>Sanskrit: Lagna</note> and the Moon are in the highest degree of their respective signs <note>Sanskrit: Vargottama; a specific auspicious division of the zodiac</note>, and are aspected by four planets, the person becomes a king. If the planets are in the signs of Aries and others, occupying the "ocean and mountain" positions <note>referring to specific angular houses</note>, they create a protector of the earth whose enemies are defeated and whose family prospers in every direction.

<summary>This manuscript page outlines specific astrological configurations (Raja Yogas) involving the exaltation and placement of planets like Jupiter and the Sun that elevate a person to royal status regardless of their birth origin.</summary>
<keywords>Raja Yoga, Astrology, Exaltation, Jupiter, Sun, Kingship, Jyotisha, Vargottama</keywords>

If the Moon is in the first degree of Taurus <note>original: "roṇi 1" referring to Rohini nakshatra/Taurus</note>, and if Venus, Jupiter, and Mercury are in the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</term>, while the Sun is in its own sign or exaltation, the person born becomes a lord of the earth. His enemies' armies are deprived of their luster and flee before him, and he is surrounded by wise ministers. 8

When the Moon is in the sign of Pisces at night, and Mars is in its exaltation <note>Capricorn</note>, and the Sun is in the Ascendant <note>Aries</note>, the person becomes a king of great renown, possessing the wisdom of the sciences. 9

If the Moon is in Taurus in the Ascendant, and the Sun is in the house of enemies <note>the 6th house</note>, while Venus is in the fourth house <note>Patala</note>, Jupiter is in the tenth house <note>the sky/zenith</note>, and Saturn and Mars are in the third house, the person becomes a protector of the earth. He upholds the path of righteousness <term>Dharma|Universal law and ethical duty</term> and is embraced by the Goddess of Royal Fortune <term>Rajya-Lakshmi|The personification of royal prosperity and power</term>. 10

When the Moon is in its exaltation <note>Taurus</note> in the Ascendant, and Saturn is in the seventh house, and the Sun is in the sign of Leo, and Jupiter is in the eleventh house, the person becomes a king who possesses many vehicles and is radiant with wealth and glory. 11

If Saturn is in the Ascendant, and Jupiter is in the fourth house, and Venus is in the Virgo, and Mars and Mercury are in the house of Mercury <note>Gemini or Virgo</note>, the person becomes a king equal to the Lord of the Gods <term>Surapati|Indra, the King of Heaven</term>, possessing vast wealth and great majesty. 12

If the Moon is in Pisces in the Ascendant, and the Sun is in the sign of the Lion <note>Leo</note>, and Saturn is in the eleventh house, the person becomes a ruler of men, endowed with all the arts and great fame. 13

Kings and great lords are born under these conditions; even those of hostile disposition shall acknowledge the king's speech. || 7 || The Sun, whose commands cannot be disobeyed, acts upon the mind <note>The Sun represents the soul and authority in Vedic astrology</note> while stationed in his own abode. He maintains such a form throughout the night, measuring the entire world. When the Sun is endowed with strength and bathed in fire, he brings about the destruction of the grief of friends and women. At his rising, he acts like a leader of the world. || 7 ||

If Venus is in exaltation <note>original: "shukrocche" - Venus is exalted in Pisces</note>, Mars is in Aries, and the Red Planet <note>Mars</note> is also in its own sign of exaltation, the person born will surely be a king. He will be a warrior himself, finding happiness among men. || 8 ||

When the Lord of the Day <note>The Sun</note> rises with the brilliance of gold, and Mercury is positioned in the middle, the native becomes a conqueror of the earth through the strength of his own arms. Adorned with pearls and iron-strong resolve, he becomes a lord of kings in every direction. || 9 ||

If the Teacher of the Gods <note>Jupiter</note> is in Sagittarius, and the Moon is in Capricorn with a bright form, or if the Son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note> is in the ascendant, the person becomes a king who subdues his enemies. The Lord of a Thousand Rays <note>The Sun</note>, who is Narayana himself <note>The Sun is often identified with the deity Vishnu/Narayana in his role as the sustainer</note>, when placed in the ascendant with strength equal to the Sun, makes the person a delight to the world. When the Lord of Light is so situated, his enemies are consumed by the fire of his majesty and bow to him from afar. || 10 ||

These are the planetary strengths. Even if all the cruel planets are in their orbits, if the Moon is auspicious and the lord of the night is strong, a protector of the earth is born. He shall cause the destruction of his rivals like a fire, and his fame shall reach the shores of the ocean. Even the elephants of his enemies shall shed tears of submission at his feet.

If the Lord of the Ascendant <note>The planet ruling the first house of the zodiac at the time of birth</note> is weak, but the Ascendant itself or the Ninth House <note>The house of fortune and destiny</note> falls in a *Vargottama* <term>Vargottama: the best division; when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth divisional chart</term> position, the individual becomes a leader. 27 Even if the planets are in the *Vargottama* division or in odd signs, if the "Lord of the Planets" <note>The Sun</note> is situated in a quadrant <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, which are the pillars of a horoscope</term> or is joined by benefics, the body remains free from disease and the person becomes a lord of men. 28

Even a single planet, if it is in its sign of exaltation or in a *Vargottama* position, provided it is strong and aspected by friendly planets, makes the person a ruler of the seven earths. 29 When the Moon is waning, if all the benefics are in the eleventh house <note>The house of gains</note> and the malefics are in the sixth house <note>The house of enemies</note>, and the Ascendant is aspected by a benefic, the person becomes a king possessing jewels and gold. 30

Count the number of planets in exaltation; if the Lord of the Ascendant is in a friendly house and the benefics are in quadrants, while the malefics are entirely devoid of strength and in retreat, a powerful King is born, equal in strength to a god. 31 If a planet is in its own sign, possessing a beautiful form, and is aspected by friends while transiting through a favorable position, it makes a King who is free from enemies and who effortlessly protects the earth, even if other planets are weak. 32

When a planet is in its highest exaltation in a quadrant and is aspected by Venus, it makes one a King of Kings, provided the person is free from the influence of all malefics. 33 If the quadrants are occupied by benefics or friendly planets, and three planets are in their own signs or exaltation, the person becomes a King. If the Moon and the Sun are in the ninth house or a quadrant, and the person possesses noble strength and wisdom, such a destiny is proclaimed here by the sages. 34

Planets that are not in their signs of debilitation, nor in the houses of enemies, but are in their own signs or divisions, and are endowed with full digits <note>referring to the lunar phases or planetary brilliance</note>, even if they are aspected by the Moon, they grant a kingdom. If the planets are free from the influence of enemies, they bestow great power. 40

He who has the Moon with full rays <note>a Full Moon</note> in a *Vargottama* position, or if Mercury is in a favorable position, such a person always produces auspicious results during their planetary period <note>Dasha</note> and becomes a renowned figure in the world.

Like a lotus stalk supporting the orb of the <term>Moon</term> <note>original: "shashinam"</note> in its corner, the Lord of the Lotus <note>the Sun</note> shines. If these two lords of the night and day are positioned in a friendly manner, and if one is not afflicted by inauspicious marks, they become wise. || 52

When the <term>planets</term> <note>original: "vihagai," literally "birds of the sky"</note> are in their <term>debilitated signs</term> <note>original: "nichatirashi," the weakest zodiacal positions</note> but are joined by three others in strength, or are associated with auspicious and sharp-edged influences as white as cow's milk, the <term>Moon</term> <note>original: "mrigalanchana," literally "the one with the deer-mark"</note> being in its own sign makes one a lord of the earth who conquers all enemies. || 53

When all the moving signs are favorable in the <term>first house</term> <note>original: "tanu bhavane," the house of the body</note>, in an auspicious sign and in its own divisional chart, and when the "thorns" <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> are devoid of wicked influences and full of good results, one becomes a lord of men who cannot be overthrown. || 55

The <term>Moon</term> in its <term>best divisional state</term> <note>original: "vargottama," a highly auspicious planetary strength</note>, full and in its own corner, makes a great king pleasing in all his glory. The dust raised by the hooves of his retreating enemies' horses is so great that even the Sun cannot make its form visible during his march. || 56

When a yoga <note>astrological combination</note> is formed by all the planets, one becomes a <term>universal monarch</term> <note>original: "chakravartti"</note>. By even a single such verse's condition, men become lords of great territories. || 58

# On the Destinies of Kings and the Destruction of Dynasties

If <term>the Ascendant</term><note>Sanskrit: lagne; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> is occupied by certain planets, it causes the destruction of kings, even if the <term>benefic planets</term><note>Sanskrit: saumya; planets like Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury that generally bring positive results</note> are situated in the <term>cardinal houses</term><note>Sanskrit: kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope</note>. **19** If all the planets are positioned in the six houses starting from the house of enemies <margin>21</margin> and continuing through the others, they make a man a lord of the earth, provided the seventh house is excluded. **20**

If all the planets are gathered in the <term>benefic divisions</term><note>Sanskrit: sañcikāganā; likely referring to auspicious vargas or groupings</note> while a cruel deed is performed, or if they are honored through rituals and restraints, the king becomes one who possesses much wealth. **21**

If all the planets are situated in the <term>Navamsha</term><note>Sanskrit: lhorāsa; referring to the ninth divisional chart used for fine-tuning predictions</note> of the Ascendant, they cause the destruction of the city where the lord of the earth resides. **22** If all the benefics are in their natural states and the cruel Sun is in the first or eleventh house, they overcome the king. This is a yoga <note>a specific planetary combination</note> that brings peace to the forest-dwelling lords and those <unclear>born</unclear> of royal lineage. **23**

When the benefics are in the houses of gains and dharma <note>the 11th and 9th houses</note> and the malefic planets are situated in the house of action <note>the 10th house</note>, this is called the "Vayami" yoga, which exists for a long time. **24** If three planets are in the fourth house, and the Moon and Rahu are also well-placed, and the remaining planets are in the house of the spouse <note>the 7th house</note>, this is truly the "Sarva-Kumbha" yoga. **25**

Even those born in low families, if they possess these diverse forms of planetary strength, are said to become lords of the earth. Hereafter, the yogas for those born in royal dynasties are proclaimed by the Sages. **26** When Leo is the Ascendant, and the Sun and Mars are there, while the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> is in Aquarius and the teacher of the gods <note>Jupiter</note> is in his own house...

->**The Birth of Kings and Great Leaders**<-

When the <term>Son of the Earth</term><note>original: "Bhumitanaya"; refers to the planet Mars</note> is in his own sign of exaltation, and the Moon is in the sign of the Earth <note>Taurus</note>, the person born becomes a crown-jewel among all people on earth. (27) If the journey begins under an auspicious <term>Ascendant</term><note>original: "Lagna"; the rising sign on the eastern horizon</note> and the planets are powerful, and if the remaining auspicious planets occupy the other houses in sequence starting from the Ascendant, the person becomes a glorious <term>Lord of the Earth</term><note>original: "Mahibharta"; a king or sovereign</note>. If the Moon reaches the fifth house and is joined by Jupiter, they destroy all enemies with a flood of brilliance. (28)

If the Sun is situated in its own house or in its sign of exaltation, and the Moon is similarly well-placed and full of rays, and if these planets are positioned in the third house, the person becomes a ruler of vast lands, possessing great wealth and many vehicles such as elephants and horses. (32) When the <term>Son of the Sun</term><note>Saturn</note> is exalted and Jupiter is in a favorable position, and the remaining planets are in the seventh house or the path of the gods, the person becomes a leader of men with stainless fame. (10)

If the Sun is with the Lord of the Constellation, and Saturn is in the Ascendant, and the Lord of the Day <note>the Sun</note> is in the tenth house, such a man becomes a famous King through his own virtues, conquering his enemies like the gods. If Jupiter is situated in the sign of <term>the Sea-Monster</term><note>original: "Makara"; the sign of Capricorn</note> in the eleventh house of gains, it creates a sovereign who is free from fear. If Mars is in the Ascendant along with the <term>Son of the Sun</term><note>Saturn</note>, and the Sun is in the middle, and <term>Bhargava</term><note>the planet Venus</note> is in the seventh house, and the Moon is in the fourth house <note>the house of kinsmen</note>, such a person becomes a powerful ruler.

If the gods, beginning with Brahma, are endowed with full strength and a complete form, and if the native is aspected <note>aspected: in astrology, when one planet 'looks' at another, influencing its effects</note> by a powerful, auspicious planet, such a person becomes illustrious and wealthy, even if born at the dead of night. When Jupiter <note>original: "Deva-mantri," the counselor of the gods</note> is situated in the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note> and is joined with Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note>, he produces a protector of the earth <note>original: "Vasudha-palam," a king</note> who is free from sickness and possesses the highest intelligence. **27**

Those born under the "Karakayoga" <note>a specific astrological combination where planets act as significant indicators of success</note> become rulers who enjoy the earth. For them, the obstacles to their power are destroyed, just as an elephant scatters a swarm of bees. **25**

When Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> is in the Ascendant and the Moon is pure and free from malefic influence, the person becomes a noble protector. If the Moon is well-placed and bright, it beautifies the life of the sovereign. **28**

If Jupiter is in his own sign or in his sign of exaltation, and the Moon is free from the rays of the Sun <note>meaning the Moon is not combust or hidden by the Sun's glare</note>, the person born is like a god on earth, endowed with superior wisdom. **30**

When the Moon is full and situated in Capricorn, away from enemies and joined with Venus, the person possesses many women and great wealth. Like the Sun among the stars, such a person leads an army; their power is so great that their enemies shrink away, just as the day-blooming lotus closes its petals at the end of the day. **29**

Even if born at night, if cruel or debased planets are positioned in the houses of enemies, while gentle, auspicious planets are in their highest exaltation or in the center houses <note>original: "Kendra," the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> and are pure, the native will be victorious in battle, standing like a crest-jewel among men.

By one's own low or high placement, or by being in a natural house, and understood by those wise in the appearance of the planets; when the Sun and Moon are situated in their own houses, the person born becomes a leader among men:
The first is one joined with women. 41 <note>Verse 41 describes a person of high status who is also characterized by their associations or perhaps a large harem.</note> When the lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> is in a center house <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, considered pillars of strength</term> in a great friend's sign, but the lord of the Ascendant is otherwise weak, yet joined with the lord of the sign occupied by Saturn and Mars,
this is called the "Stout Combination" <term>Sthula Yoga: a configuration suggesting a powerful or heavy physical build</term>. 42 In this Stout Combination, men are born as rulers of the earth by rule; they become possessed of all wealth, having destroyed their enemies,
and the quarters of the earth are scented by the fragrance of the ichor <note>The fluid secreted by elephants in rut, a traditional poetic symbol of a king's vast elephant army</note> of their elephants. 43 If the lord of the Ascendant is in the sign of the Sun, or if Mercury is in the Ascendant, the person becomes a village leader; if at the end of a group of signs, they are of middle status. 44 If the Sun is in the face <insert>beginning</insert> of the months of Phalguna or Magha <note>Late winter/early spring months</note> and is placed in the Ascendant, it makes a man a lord of men,
provided the lord of the Ascendant is in a favorable position. 45 If the Moon is in Krittika or Revati <term>Nakshatras: lunar mansions or star clusters</term> and the son of Bhrigu <note>The planet Venus</note> is in Pushya, it makes a king of the middle sort, even if situated in a difficult place. 46 <note>The original manuscript skips from verse 46 to 48.</note> If the lord of the Ascendant is in its own division and Mars is in a center house, this makes a universal emperor; if the lord of the Ascendant is with the lord of the second house, they are equal to a king. 48 When the full Moon is in Pisces and is aspected by its teacher Jupiter, it certainly makes a man a universal emperor within twelve years; of this there is no doubt. 49 When Mars is in the tenth house <term>Karma-sthana: the house of action and profession</term>, and Mercury, Venus, and Saturn are in the seventh, or if the Sun and Moon are together in the twelfth or the fourth house, one becomes a great lord. 50 ... <unclear>toti bhaktiyane pasyamavivalltin</unclear>

# On the Royal Combinations of the Planets

When the Sun and other planets are positioned in the sign of the Lion <note>original: "mṛgarāja" or Simha/Leo</note> and other signs, and they move toward a state of unity, the results are manifold. If even one planet is not in its proper place, the result changes. 51

If the Sun is positioned in the center <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses which act as the pillars of a horoscope</term> in the sign of the Lion, a person is born who, though perhaps a merchant with a chariot, may become a great leader; yet if poorly placed, they may be stripped of their city, village, and wealth. 52 <insert>Venus</insert>

When the Moon and Sun are in certain positions, the person becomes devoted to their duties and disciplined in their observances. However, if the planets are afflicted, they may become careless in all matters and face constant misfortune. 53

The Sun is the life-force, and the Moon and other planets are its rays. When a single planet is pure and bright with its own rays, it should be known as significant. If the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> is auspicious and Jupiter is in the sixth house, even then, the lords of the directions will favor the path of the seeker. 54

If the Moon is in the eighth degree of the Water-Bearer <note>original: "Kumbha" or Aquarius</note> and situated in a trine, the person born under this influence becomes a king who is a provider of auspicious things for all people. 55

In the seventh part of the Ram <note>original: "Mesha" or Aries</note>, if the planet of wisdom <note>Jupiter</note> is situated, it creates a ruler of men. With even more auspicious influences, such a person becomes a king of great beauty and power. 56

The Moon situated in the fifteenth degree of the Water-Bearer makes one a protector of the earth <note>original: "Mahipala" or King</note>. In the seventh part of the Crab <note>original: "Karkataka" or Cancer</note>, it makes a man successful in the assembly of war. 57

Jupiter in the twentieth degree of the Bow <note>original: "Dhanu" or Sagittarius</note> makes a man a king of men. If the Sun and Mercury are in the fifth degree of the Lion, they are to be known as indicators of great power. 58

When the Moon is in the ninth degree, among a multitude of brave kings, he stands out... <unclear>text fades at the edge of the folio</unclear>

93. When the Maker of Night <note>the Moon</note> and Venus are situated in their respective positions, they create a person of a hundred-fold wealth; such a man never falls from his status and possesses great earthly valor.

94. If all the planets are situated in the houses of increase <term>Upachaya: the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses where planets grow in strength over time</term> from the Moon, and are not in their signs of debilitation, the man becomes the lord of all houses and a master of men.

96. If the son of Bhrigu <note>Venus</note> is with the Sun, and Saturn is placed in the seventh house from them, then indeed that man becomes a King of Kings, a protector of the seas.

97. When the planets Saturn, Mars, and the Sun are in transit through the watery signs, it may cause distress to the nation; however, the man born under this becomes a Lord of the Earth who never retreats from his royal duties. 98.

If Mercury shines above the Moon, and the Sun <note>original: "Bhaskara"</note> is positioned at the end, such a person becomes a lord of the earth, excellent among men. 9 If Venus, Mercury, the Sun, and Saturn are in the angular houses <term>Kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, which are the pillars of a horoscope</term>, the man becomes a king, seated upon a throne and surrounded by other kings. 10

When malefic planets <note>original: "papa"; referring to naturally harsh planets like Mars or Saturn</note> are in the third house and the house of gains <note>the 11th house</note>, and the remaining auspicious planets are well-placed, the person becomes immensely wealthy, honored by all, and a virtuous soul. 13 If Jupiter <note>original: "Suraputra"</note> moves through Aries or Leo, and is aspected by the rays of Mars <note>original: "Bhumiputra"</note>, even if one planet is in its debilitation <term>Nicha; the sign where a planet's power is weakest</term>, the person born under this Sun becomes a lord of kings. 14

If the planets are situated in the angular houses at the time of birth, great royal combinations are formed. If any planet is in its sign of strength, it produces a lord of men; what then can be said if two such planets are present? [81] When the Sun is in the highest degree of Aquarius and Venus is in its own sign, the person becomes a lord of the earth. [82]

One whose body is intoxicated <note>full of vigor</note>, who is not crooked, whose voice is as pleasant as the blooming night-lotus, and whose limbs are well-proportioned, if born under the influence of five planets in their own signs or exaltation, becomes a ruler of the earth, provided they are not in their signs of debilitation. If Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn are in the eleventh house, even a person of humble birth becomes a protector of the earth. Those who possess an army, who are righteous, and who are favored by the Moon and the Sun, become lords of the earth.

->**The Determination of Rays**<-

Therefore, with great effort, I shall explain this clearly and without confusion, just as the Moon appears in its five-fold stages according to its own will and position. 1. By this calculation of the Moon, the entirety of one's fortune and the speed of their destiny is determined by the five-fold divisions. 2. Thus, in the <term>Great Treatise</term><note>Mahashastra</note> proclaimed by the sage Badarayana, the entire system of the planetary periods <note>dashas</note> is specified through the movements of the planets. 3. All these are authoritative words of the sages; why should there be any doubt? In this world, many paths of knowledge exist, but the light of the planets is like a guiding beam in the darkness. 4. Whether the rays are favorable or malignant depends on whether a planet is in its sign of exaltation or debilitation; I shall now explain these various forms. 5. A planet stripped of its rays by being in a debilitated state brings bondage, while one with full rays brings great power; one's life is multiplied by the quality of their own planetary rays. 6. When a planet is in its own division, its own sign, or its own exaltation, the rays are doubled; such is the strength of a planet situated in its own zodiac sign. 7. Conversely, if a planet is in the division of an enemy or in its sign of debilitation, it becomes devoid of light; such planets are considered "rayless" by the learned authorities. 8. When a planet is in retrograde motion, its rays are doubled, but if it is leaving its retrograde state, it loses its portion of strength. Thus, the rays should be examined according to their specific strengths. 9. From one to ten rays, the effects range from being destitute to becoming a great leader; now, according to the teachings, I shall describe the character of men based on these. 10. Through the specific movements of the planets, men are born either with supreme glory or are completely deprived of good fortune.

<summary>This passage outlines the mathematical and astrological determination of "planetary rays" (rashmi), explaining how a planet's position—such as exaltation, debilitation, or retrogradation—doubles or diminishes its influence on a person's fortune.</summary>

<keywords>planetary rays, Badarayana, Mahashastra, astrology, exaltation, debilitation, retrograde, destiny, Indian astronomy</keywords>

->**Victory to the son of Devaki!** <note>A salutation to Krishna</note><-

I shall now describe the Great Person Yogas <term>Mahapurusha Yoga: Five specific planetary combinations that indicate an extraordinary destiny</term> which produce lords of men. Even I, following the lineage of the sages, shall speak of these royal qualities. 1. Through the strength of the planets in their own signs or in exaltation, located in the cardinal houses <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart</note>, five types of great men are born: Hamsa, Sasa, Ruchaka, Bhadra, and Malavya. 2.

### The Characteristics of the Malavya Yoga
<note>Malavya Yoga occurs when Venus is strong and positioned in a cardinal house</note>

The person born under Malavya has limbs as graceful as a goddess, a body that is well-proportioned and firm, and a voice as sweet as a stringed instrument. Their complexion is pleasing and bright, and they possess a full and healthy physique. 3. They are devoted to their teachers, possess a steady nature, and speak with kindness to their friends. They are rarely defeated by enemies and are characterized by virtuous qualities. 4. Though they may occasionally be fierce in conflict, they possess a refined intellect and are fond of women and various arts. They are generally courageous, scholarly, and famous for their refined tastes. 5. Even if they experience moments of anger or distress, they remain pure in conduct, gracious, and are not easily overcome by others. 6.

Smiling, pure, and of a fair complexion; free from envy, with a graceful and charming gait; such a man is endowed with <term>good fortune<note>original: "saubhāgya" - a blend of prosperity, beauty, and auspiciousness</note></term>. He is a giver of food, a connoisseur of music and the arts, and fond of fine sweets. He is skilled in cleansing and purification, and even in his dreams, he is capable of enjoying worldly pleasures. 22

Possessing a multitude of virtues, with hair that is dark and lustrous; he is very cheerful, with hands and feet that seem to glow. Though he may be prone to anger, his body remains firm; he is bound by his word and is a <term>destroyer of obstacles<note>original: "vighnasaṃhārakārī" - one who removes difficulties, a quality often attributed to the deity Ganesha</note></term>. Such are the characteristics of a man who follows the path of righteousness. 23

Though his body may appear worn, he is quick to anger yet just as quick to be appeased. He is a protector of the poor and the distressed, speaking truth from his very feet to his head. He is <term>wise<note>original: "medhāvī" - possessing high intellectual capacity and memory</note></term>, intelligent, and fearless. He harbors a dislike for the wind and the sun, yet he is free from the fear of defeat. He frequently seeks out fragrant flowers and things that are dear to the heart. 24

His eyes are like the petals of a lotus, and his hands are as red as the Kimshuka flower. With a body the color of blood, he is a kin to his relatives, yet he is not overly attached to his home. Illustrious, with well-knit joints and great strength; his body always possesses a smooth and oily luster. He is a lover of truth, his mind is undisturbed by distress, and he is beloved by all people, for he is a seeker of <term>virtues<note>original: "guṇa" - qualities or merits</note></term> rather than faults. 26

### The <term>Malavya</term> Type <note>One of the five types of "Great Men" in Hindu astrology, governed by Venus (Shukra)</note>

The one born under the Malavya yoga possesses a clear and bright complexion, a steady mind, and is capable of performing daring deeds. He has a slender waist and a face like the full moon. He carries the staff topped with a skull, the bow, the lute, and the conch shell in his hands. His hands and feet are marked with the signs of the lotus. He is handsome, peaceful, and lives for a hundred years. Thus it is spoken by the gods for the one who has bowed to the divine. (32) The Vindhya mountains...
