When the Moon <note>original: "Nishakara," literally the night-maker</note> is situated in the sign of Cancer <note>original: "Atri-rashi," referring to the lineage of the sage Atri</note> while in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</term>, the "King-Maker" combination <term>Rajayoga: a planetary alignment indicating high status or power</term> is shattered. 1

If the Sun is not in an enemy division and is placed at the beginning of a moveable sign, the person remains free from suffering. Even if a single planet is positioned ahead, the status of the Lord of Kings is not destroyed. 2

When all malefic planets are in the cardinal houses <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a chart</term> and are in their signs of debilitation, and are not aspected by benefics, a "Break in Royalty" <note>original: "Naradhibhanga"</note> occurs for the person. 4

If Mars or Venus is in the Ascendant in a moveable sign, there is a cancellation of the royal status. Such combinations in moveable signs lead to poverty and a lack of renown. 5

If three planets are in their signs of debilitation, even if they are naturally gentle <note>benefic</note>, the yoga is broken. However, if even one planet is in a favorable state, the royal combination is not destroyed; rather, the negative effects perish and the King-Maker status remains certain. 6

When benefics are in the cardinal houses and the Ascendant is strong, even if the Sun is in its sign of debilitation <note>Libra</note>, the royal status is not destroyed. 7

If the Moon is in its sign of exaltation but is joined with Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note>, and is aspected by malefics while lacking the gaze of benefics, a man may achieve peace for a time, but later he will surely face destruction and lose his position. 8

When the Moon is in the Ascendant and is viewed by its enemies, even a thousand suns cannot prevent the loss of power. 9

If the cardinal houses are empty of planets and the Ascendant is occupied by benefics, the combinations for a common man are destroyed, leading to internal disease. 9 <note>The manuscript repeats the number 9 for this verse</note>

When five planets are in their signs of fall <note>debilitation</note>, the person becomes distressed and the royal status is ruined, as declared by the sages. 10

These are the combinations of sin and virtue, of falling and rising... <note>The text cuts off mid-sentence</note>

If the Moon is soiled <note>note: afflicted by malefic planets</note> and the person is born into a poor family, even if they have the marks of a king, they may end up wandering for alms or living in poverty. 20.

When seven, six, five, or four planets are in their highest positions in the sky, they grant wealth and a kingdom to the individual.

### Thus ends the Chapter on the Cancellation of Royal Yogas.

Since this entire world is driven by the desire for results, one must first perform the calculation of the life-span after setting aside all obstacles. Where the auspicious planets are seen in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the rising sign on the eastern horizon</term>, they grant a full vision of the results. One must determine the strength of these planets to understand the earthly journey (1).

When the Ascendant, the Sun, and the Moon—all three—are devoid of strength, then the person's life is like a flickering flame. If they are in their own divisions, they provide protection like a king (2). The combinations starting from the Ascendant are equal to the merit of the yoga. If they are not in their own signs, the results are said to be diminished; otherwise, they are multiplied by their own qualities (4).

When the Hour <term>Hora: a half-sign division or the Ascendant itself</term> is endowed with all strengths, it grants years equal to the number of the stars <note>referring to the numerical values associated with planetary degrees</note>. Other periods such as months are calculated through the rule of three. In the best divisions, in one's own house, or in one's own decanate <term>Drekkana: a ten-degree division of a sign</term> or ninth-part <term>Navamsha: a 3.20 degree division</term>, the strength is said to be doubled by the planets situated in their specific circles (8). <meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary strengths and longevity calculations, specifically focusing on the Vargottama positions and the methods of the sage Varahamihira.</meta>

<page-num>104</page-num>

<margin>
Summary
V.
104
</margin>

The planets situated in their own signs or similar divisions are considered by the <term>thinkers of the Shastras</term><note>Scholars of traditional scientific treatises</note> to be in the **Vargottama** <note>The best divisional rank, where a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth harmonic chart</note> state, which empowers all their effects. 7. When planets are positioned in the <term>Kendras</term><note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, considered the pillars of a horoscope</note> and other houses, their qualities are multiplied by factors of time and space. Some authorities do not accept the Vargottama status if the planet is in an enemy's sign. 8. Amidst many conflicting opinions, the great teacher **Varahamihira**, who is held in high regard by the ancients, establishes a unified system for these divisions. 9. There is a rule regarding the setting <note>combustion by the sun</note> of planets; the Moon and other planets lose their power when they are within certain degrees of the Sun, though Venus and Saturn are sometimes viewed differently. 10. All planets provide full results when in certain positions, half in others, and a quarter in the remaining; this collection of rules is essential for determining the lifespan. 11. The benefic planets <note>Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus</note> provide half their strength in certain conditions, as stated by many; however, a single strong planet can be the cause of a royal yoga <note>a combination for power and wealth</note>. 12. The specific degrees for the planets are as follows: nineteen degrees for the Moon, twenty for others; the minutes are nine and seven. For Mercury, it is his own portion; 13. for Jupiter, fifteen; for Venus, twenty-one; and for the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note>, it is twenty. These are the portions used when planets are in their own signs or exaltation. 14. When a planet is in its own sign or a friend's sign, the remaining years are calculated by the measure of the signs and portions. 15. One should consider all these factors together, especially the retrograde motions and the intervals between signs, as this special method has been described. 16. The calculation should be made from the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> or from the position of the Sun, according to the strength-based system spoken by the sage **Satya**. 17. The minutes of the Ascendant's portion are the primary cause; one should carefully determine the lifespan of the birds <note>a metaphorical term for planets moving through the sky</note>.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Ayurdaya (determination of longevity) from the Saravali, a foundational 8th-century Sanskrit astrological treatise by Kalyanavarman.</meta>

->### Chapter on the Determination of Longevity<-

Subtract the sum of the planetary periods from the total. <note>original: "bhajallittabhistyam"</note> In a similar manner, one should calculate the purification of the sun. **18** With the addition of eight, sixteen, and seven, the calculation for the auspicious day is completed. In this very way, the practitioner of yoga <note>here referring to mathematical application</note> understands the aging of the body. **19** The number seventy-eight <note>original: "vimsatirekedinayanam 78"</note> and twenty for the Sun are the natural years assigned to the planets. **20** In the signs of Pisces and Aries, when multiplied by twenty-five, and when Mercury and Venus are in their exaltation while the rest are in the nodes, Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> becomes supreme. **21**

When the planet is in the ascendant of Cancer, and Jupiter and Venus are in the quadrants <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, while the remaining planets are in the sixth and seventh houses, the lifespan is said to be full. **22** Excluding the Sun, the maximum lifespan for humans is one hundred and twenty years <note>original: "naradantinam," literally 'human-elephants,' a poetic term for 120 years</note>. The lifespan for horses is thirty-two years, and for camels and similar animals, it is sixteen. **23** The maximum lifespan for elephants and others is remembered as twelve times the base multiplier. Up to seven centuries is the memory for the lifespan of serpents and tigers. **24**

When the planets are in their highest divisions, one-fifth of the maximum life is added. Ascending toward the highest liberation <term>Nishreyas: the ultimate spiritual goal</term>, one should thus indicate the sequence of life. **25** This calculation of the lifespan of men, which shines like a thousand suns for the lords of the earth, was composed through the grace of the great Sages.

->**Thus ends the Chapter on Longevity in the Saravali.**<-

The lifespan which has been described here with respect should be reflected upon. Through the combination of one's own qualities and planetary strengths, the results are rendered fruitful.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of planetary periods (Dashas) and their specific effects based on zodiacal placement and divisional charts. It follows the standard structure of a Jyotisha (Vedic Astrology) manual.</meta>

When the planet <term>Jiva</term><note>Jupiter</note> is in the ascendant, the direction of the <term>Dasha</term><note>planetary period</note> brings life and vitality. However, if the planet <term>Saura</term><note>Saturn</note> is dominant, there may be bondage or imprisonment. One should consider the influence of the Sun's path regarding the residence and the actions of the person. 12

In the sign of <term>Simha</term><note>Leo</note>, if the planets are poorly placed, there may be disputes involving wives and sons, or even death. Through the influence of Saturn's sign, there are combinations leading to bondage or execution, yet also the potential for great skill or proficiency. 13

When the <term>Paka Swami</term><note>The lord of the current planetary period</note> is in a friendly division or associated with <term>Saumya</term><note>benefic planets like Mercury, Venus, or Jupiter</note>, the faults arising from the movement of the stars are mitigated, and the results are auspicious. 14

Whether a planet is in a friendly house, a <term>Trikona</term><note>a trine; the 1st, 5th, or 9th house</note>, or the seventh house, if it is strong in the <term>Navamsha</term><note>the ninth divisional chart used to determine a planet's true strength</note>, it will not be angry during its period. 15

Conversely, if the <term>Dasha</term> is afflicted by faults, it produces opposite results. Even a planet in its own sign may yield meager results if it is weakened by other factors. 18 <note>original: "viparīta" meaning reversed or contrary</note>

In the lineages of kings, the study of the signs and houses is essential. The periods yield mixed results based on the wisdom of the planetary ruler. 19

Regarding the <term>Drekkana</term><note>a 10-degree divisional chart</note>, the sages say the first part is the lowest, the middle is mixed, and the final part is the best. 18 <note>The numbering in the original manuscript repeats or skips here.</note>

The Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn—these are the lords of the <term>Hora</term><note>a two-part divisional chart</note> groups. The timing of the periods follows the <term>Naisargika</term><note>natural or innate</note> strength of these planets. 19

A planet that is in its own sign and possesses full strength yields complete results. Whether natural or acquired, a planet in its sign of exaltation provides the highest fruits, while one in its sign of depression yields the lowest.

The period <term>Dasha; a planetary period in Vedic astrology</term> becomes effective [20]. When a planet is in its own sign, in its own <term>Trine; a favorable 120-degree placement</term>, in its own <term>Navamsha; a one-ninth divisional chart used to determine a planet's true strength</term>, or in its sign of exaltation, the results are auspicious or moderate. This is also true when the planet is approaching its exaltation or is situated in a friendly sign [21]. An auspicious period is known by these signs; the opposite results occur in the reverse conditions. By this same method, the results of the <term>malefics; planets that generally bring challenging results</term> should be understood. **The Rule of Saturn and the Sun:**

In the period of the Sun, a person gains new lands, great fame, and various jewels. Through mountains, gems, leather, and diamonds, or through the king's jewels and musical instruments [1], or through travels and efforts, one becomes busy and acquires wealth and courage. However, there may be a fall from one's position, harm to one's status, and yet the person maintains their strength and kingly nature [1]. There is courage among servants and excellent wealth, yet there is also trouble from intoxicated elephants and forest-dwellers. One might experience affection from the wives of enemies or from kinsmen, yet remain devoted to the king's path [2]. Trees, jewels, and forests may be gained, but there are also troubles from enemies, bilious complaints <note>original: "paittika"; referring to Pitta or fire-related illnesses in Ayurveda</note>, and various diseases. Indeed, these occur when the Sun's period is unfavorable [3]. **Thus ends the Sun's period.**

In the period of the Moon, there is wealth, association with women, and various pleasures. There may be troubles caused by fire, cold, water, or disorders of the <term>humors; the bodily fluids or energies in traditional medicine</term>. There is a gain of cool-rayed objects <note>original: "himamshau"; a poetic reference to the Moon or pearls</note> [3]. One serves those dressed in fine clothes and consumes sweet foods. Even without specific merits, sudden wealth is obtained through these means [2]. Through morality, devotion to the gods, wisdom, and great joy, one gains the favor of the king. Intelligence, fortitude, and nourishment are always present when the Moon's period is auspicious [1].

->**The Chapter on the Attainment of One's Own Period**<-

When the Earth-conjunction <note>Dharayoga: a specific planetary alignment indicating land ownership</note> occurs for rulers, they become lords of the earth through their own valor. **5** If the planets Venus and Mercury are in a debilitated state <note>Nicha: the point where a planet is at its weakest</note> in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term>, but Mercury is auspiciously placed in the seventh house, a great "Action-Yoga" <note>Karmayoga: a combination indicating significant worldly achievements</note> is formed for men; this belongs specifically to those who enjoy the pleasures of a king. **7** When benefics are in the quadrants <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term>, they grant auspiciousness. Those who possess these jewels of wisdom shall always be free from poverty. **8**

->**Thus ends the Chapter on Sub-periods.**<-

### The Cruel Periods

When a cruel planet <term>Krura: a malefic planet like Mars or Saturn</term> enters the sky <insert>the transit</insert>, it brings doubt to men regarding their land and wealth, and diseases of the voice and body arise from the home. **1** In the period of the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note>, even for those who are destined for a long life, there are many hardships and the threat of death. **2** If a malefic planet is situated in a cruel sign, the father may suffer greatly or be restrained; death may come to the person themselves during that planet's specific cooking <note>Svapaake: the "ripening" or fruition of a planet's influence</note>. **3** If the Lord of the Ascendant is weak and malefic planets occupy the Ascendant, a man may face sudden death or great obstacles in his work despite his influence. **4**

->**Thus ends the section on Misfortunes in Sub-periods.**<-

### The Cancellation of Misfortunes

If a powerful planet is aspected by or associated with benefics, or if it is placed in the divisional charts <term>Varga: harmonic charts used for fine-tuning predictions</term> of gentle planets, it becomes auspicious for the man. **5** Even if the Lord of the Sub-period is weak, if the Lord of the Major Period is powerful, the potential for "breaking" <note>Bhanga: the cancellation of a negative astrological yoga</note> the misfortune exists, and he may become like a king. **6** When two auspicious planets are placed in their own or friendly signs during their periods, no suffering will occur. **7**

->**Thus ends the Chapter on the Cancellation of Misfortunes in Periods.**<-

The Sun <note>original: "Bhaskara"</note>, even when great, makes one devour food greedily at first, yet later provides sweets and excellent drinks along with ornaments. He makes a person radiant and a leader among men; then, he truly makes one a hero with excellent eyes. Through his own power, he may produce a wrestler or an official among men. Kashi...</unclear> A man with the Sun in the highest trine becomes a backbiter, yet possesses strength and power.

The teacher of the demons <note>Venus</note> makes one a celibate student <note>term: Brahmachari</note>, a destroyer of enemies, or a king. Salutations to him. The teacher of the Danavas <note>Venus</note> produces a person who is twice-born <note>Brahmin</note>, a slayer of enemies, and very fortunate in his home. Venus, in the highest trine, grants wealth and a renewed body. One born under this influence is modest, full of essence, and possesses the great effort of soft pearls. One who delights in righteousness, the Moon <note>original: "Himarasmi," the cool-rayed one</note> makes him wise and handsome. Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> makes one a village leader, fierce, and restless when in his own zodiac sign. How can the son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note> make one a lord or a speaker? Situated in his own sign, the Poet <note>Venus</note> makes one attained in speech, generous, and full of sentiment.

# Page 9

...does not bring peace without wisdom. <note>The text here is fragmented, likely referring to the mental state of the native.</note> When a person is situated in their **own zodiac sign**, they become a true man of their own nature. In one's own sign, one attains <unclear>completeness</unclear>. In a **friend's house**, one is famous for steady friendships and becomes a leader among men. One situated in a friend's sign dwells in the forest <note>possibly referring to a retreat or a quiet, powerful life</note> with happiness, strength, and honor.

1. **Mars** <note>original: "Kuja"</note> creates a person who is passionate, like a king, with a red complexion and great valor. Born in this sign, one is prone to anger but possesses great strength and crushing power over enemies.

2. The **Lord of Speech** <note>Jupiter; original: "Vachandhipati"</note>, when well-pleased and in a favorable direction, grants success in actions. In a friend's house, the son of Bhrigu <note>Venus; original: "Bhugunava"</note> grants wisdom, though one may still possess a sharp or cruel intellect in certain matters.

3. The **Son of the Sun** <note>Saturn; original: "Bhaskara sutah"</note> makes one an enjoyer of life, though sometimes of a middling or "red" <note>passionate or angry</note> temperament.

4. In a **debilitated position** <note>term: Neecha (the point where a planet is at its weakest influence)</note>, a planet makes one a servant, or one who lacks family support and righteousness. The **Son of the Moon** <note>Mercury; original: "Hima rashmi ratna putra"</note>, when debilitated, makes one sickly, unfortunate in the world, and of low intelligence. When Mercury is in a low state, the person is not skillful in speech and possesses a base mindset; however, he may remain free from enmity.

In a **debilitated position, Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> makes one tarnished, stripped of honor, and severely lacking in intelligence. The **Teacher of the Demons** <note>Venus; original: "Asura rathi"</note>, when poorly placed, leads to a difficult or inconsistent character.

One becomes of a "despised" character, devoid of wealth and purpose, when planets are poorly placed. In an **enemy's house**, a planet makes one destitute, harsh, or like a beggar clothed in grass.

In the **house of an enemy**, even a great person becomes sickly and loses their composure. Even those of royal essence become distressed or deformed when the planets are in such a broken state.

If five planets are positioned together, the person becomes a Sultan <note>original: "Sultana"; a term for a powerful ruler or king, reflecting Islamic influence on later Indian astrological texts</note>, as fierce as the Boar incarnation of Vishnu, yet as wise as Mercury. He is never of a lowly family. He possesses a steady mind. If Jupiter is among them, he increases his wealth and remains free from the coldness of poverty. 5.

If six planets are aligned, he becomes a leader among men, though he may face many enemies. He will be a man of great resolve. If the Sun is present, he will be wise but may suffer from diseases and grief. 6. If seven planets are aligned, he will be a performer of great sacrifices, possessing cows and horses, and his fame will be stainless and eternal. He will be like a protector of the city, a noble man who subdues his enemies and possesses great brilliance. 8.

He will possess various types of wealth and a vast treasury. 9. He will be the leader of thousands, performing great deeds with a disciplined mind. He will be like Rama among men, a refuge for others, and a collector of great taxes. 10.

He will be a master of his own breath <note>referring to yogic mastery</note> and the lord of the earth extending to the very edges of the ocean. He will be born as a Universal Emperor <note>original: "Chakravarttin"; a supreme ruler whose wheels of power roll everywhere without obstruction</note>, a name known to all. 11. Through these two combinations, he becomes the best among his kin, increasing the prosperity of his family. He becomes famous for his storytelling and his brilliance shines across the earth. 12.

He becomes a great traveler or a mendicant, yet also a leader of villages and the like. He is a protector of village cattle and receives hospitality and respect from the people. 13. Endowed with charity and wealth, such a man becomes truly eloquent. Even if born in a family of cowherds, he becomes a great leader.

In one's own house, the results are fully auspicious, like a flame spreading through all the regions. In one's own country and one's own home, even a low placement yields some auspicious fruit (23).

# Page 146

Through a <term>beautiful form</term><note>original: "saurupena"</note> and auspicious conduct, it is said that one's inclinations are revealed. For men, the root of their nature is established through their lineage; however, for women, it is determined by the specific planetary degrees.

If the <term>Ascendant</term><note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note> or the Moon is strong, the following results are observed:

### The Effects of the Trishansha

In the sign of **Aries**, if the birth falls in the division of **Mars**, the woman will be bold and perhaps lacking in traditional virtues. If in **Saturn's** division, she may be deceptive. If in **Jupiter's**, she will be possessed of many good qualities. If in **Venus's**, she will be of a virtuous nature.

In the sign of **Taurus**, if in the division of **Venus**, she will be renowned for her character. If in **Mercury's**, she will be skilled in the arts. If in **Jupiter's**, she will be endowed with many virtues. If in **Mars's**, she may be prone to a wicked disposition. If in **Saturn's**, she will be of a lower character.

In the sign of **Gemini**, if in the division of **Mercury**, she will be highly intelligent and scientific. If in **Venus's**, she will be full of desire. If in **Jupiter's**, she will be virtuous. If in **Mars's**, she will be deceitful. If in **Saturn's**, she will be like a servant.

In the sign of **Cancer**, if in the division of **Mars**, she will be self-willed and perhaps unchaste. If in **Saturn's**, she may be a slayer of her husband. If in **Jupiter's**, she will have many good qualities. If in **Mercury's**, she will be skilled in crafts. If in **Venus's**, she will be highly honored.

In the sign of **Leo**, if in the division of **Jupiter**, she will be like a queen. If in **Venus's**, she will be devoted to her husband. If in **Mercury's**, she will be like a man in her conduct. If in **Mars's**, she will be talkative. If in **Saturn's**, she will be of a lowly nature.

In the sign of **Virgo**, if in the division of **Mercury**, she will be very wealthy. If in **Venus's**, she will be of excellent character. If in **Jupiter's**, she will be pure and holy. If in **Mars's**, she will be cunning. If in **Saturn's**, she will be dependent on others.

If the lord of the eighth house is weak, death occurs due to disease. <note>original: "khivimala... mṛtyuḥ"</note> 1. If the Sun and other planets are situated in the eighth house, death is caused by fire, water, or weapons respectively. If the eighth house is occupied by powerful planets, death occurs in a holy place. <note>original: "yobala pukte nithanaṃ"</note> If the Moon is in the eighth house, death may be caused by a thunderbolt or lightning. 2. If the Sun and Mars are in the fourth or tenth houses, death occurs due to falling from a mountain or a vehicle. If Mars and Saturn are in the fourth house, death occurs in a well, as stated by the wise. 3. If the Moon is between malefic planets and situated in a sign of Mercury (Virgo or Gemini), death occurs due to a fall from a high place. If the Sun and Moon are in the Ascendant in a dual sign, death occurs by drowning in water. 4. If the Moon is in Cancer or Capricorn and aspected by malefic planets, death occurs from dropsy (swelling) or blood disorders. If the Moon is in Aquarius and aspected by malefic planets, death is caused by fire or weapons. 5. If the Moon is in Virgo and aspected by malefic planets, death occurs from consumption (wasting disease) or internal disorders. If the Moon is in a sign of Mars (Aries or Scorpio) and aspected by malefic planets, death is caused by secret diseases or hidden ailments. 6. According to the <term>Drekkana</term> <note>A 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign used to refine predictions.</note> and the malefic planets situated in the eighth house, death may be caused by a wooden staff. If the Moon is in Virgo and aspected by malefic planets, death occurs from blood disorders or chronic illness. 7. If Mars is in Aries or Scorpio, and the Moon is in Capricorn, and the Ascendant is occupied by malefic planets, or if Venus is in Aries, death is caused by a woman or occurs in one's own home due to heart failure. 8. If Mars is in the fourth house and the Sun is in the tenth house, and the Moon is weak and joined with malefic planets, or if malefic planets are in the trines (5th or 9th houses) from the Ascendant, death is indicated by a spear or sharp instrument. If the Moon is in the fourth house and malefic planets are in the tenth, death occurs by falling from a vehicle or through a neck injury.

->**On the Determination of Death and Disease**<-

In the middle of the portion <note>original: "vishaya," referring here to a specific degree or division</note> of the sign **Aries**, one should predict death in the twentieth year. Through the ailment of urinary disorders <note>original: "prameha"</note>, the counselor <insert>or minister</insert> suffers an end brought about by his own internal imbalances. 21

From diseases arising in the region of the waters, death occurs in the first twenty degrees of **Leo**. In the portion associated with the fatty tissues <note>original: "medas"</note>, there is a specific affliction involving an excess of the watery element. 20

Through the fault of a combination of poison and weapons, or through a lingering sickness, death occurs. In this treatise, there is no doubt that death comes to a man in the portion of **Leo**. 21

In the first part of **Virgo**, death occurs from a slow-moving disease <note>likely a reference to Saturnian or chronic ailments</note>. In the middle of the portion, death may occur in a serpent-infested mountain fortress or a forest. 22

Through the falling of a sharp weapon or by a noose <note>or snare</note>, a sharp and sudden end occurs. In this treatise, the portion of **Virgo** brings the destruction of men. 32

In the first division of **Libra**, death occurs through the fault of a mechanical restraint or falling. In the middle, it occurs through diseases of the matted hair <note>possibly referring to scalp diseases or ascetic-related ailments</note> or by the rising of a serpent. 24

In the first division of **Scorpio**, death is caused by poison, weapons, or the consumption of cold water. In the middle division, death is caused by the burden of a village-related sickness <note>possibly a local epidemic</note>. 25 In the third part of **Scorpio**, death is caused by the striking of a clod of earth or a stone. For men, death occurs through village-violence or a triple-fracture. 32

In the first portion of **Sagittarius**, death occurs through diseases caused by the ocean or sea-salt. In the middle, death occurs through the fault of swallowed poison or an imbalance of the wind <note>term: Vata; the biological humor associated with air and movement</note>. 33

In the third part of the treatise <insert>regarding this sign</insert>, death occurs in the midst of the waters, or even by a fish-shaped idol <note>or a sea creature</note>; for men, death arises from a disease of the stomach or belly. 38

# The Saravali

Endowed with good character, yet timid; possessing varied tastes and a restless mind. Such a person is endowed with intellect, a leader among men <note>original: "nṛpo," literally a king or ruler</note>, and follows the guidance of teachers. He is an aspirer toward higher things, yet sometimes prone to harming others. **14**

A seeker of difficult or unusual desires, he is constantly devoted to the worship of the twice-born <note>the priestly class or Brahmins</note> and the gods. He is dedicated to righteousness <term>Dharma: the moral and cosmic law</term> and is perpetually fond of water and lush gardens. **15**

He shall have female children and be bound to the service of leaders. He is of moderate or small stature, and his family life is often criticized. He has a great inclination toward enjoyment and consumption, and he receives a share of wealth through his own sight and efforts. **16**

<margin>Traveler to foreign lands</margin>
One who travels to foreign lands, possessing a fine voice and engaging in bold or daring deeds. He acquires wealth through his own strength. In the company of women, he finds excellent happiness and enjoys pleasures that are incomparable. **17**

When the influence is in **Leo**, he possesses pride and becomes a protector of the earth <note>a king</note> who has attained honor and wealth. He is joined with righteousness, though he bears the heavy burden of supporting his family and wife in his own residence. **18**

In **Leo**, his happiness is equal to his status; he possesses depth of character, firmness, and an understanding of righteousness. He is not a man of few words, and he acts for the benefit of others even while in service. **19**

Following the ways of the ancients, he is a man of excellence with a powerful gaze. He may be prone to carelessness, has few brothers, and is a celebrated enemy to the wicked. He is a supreme seeker of the company of the virtuous. **20**

He possesses wealth through agriculture and orchard-work and is intent upon trade, though he tends toward high expenses. His wife may be as disciplined as a cloistered woman <note>original: "veshyanariniyamini," suggesting a woman of strict conduct or one who restrains worldly impulses</note>, yet she remains free from disease. **21**

A son of the city of Brahma <note>possibly referring to a priestly or scholarly lineage</note>, he is white-limbed, devoted to righteousness, and skilled in the poetic arts. He is a beloved speaker of stories, a giver of gems, and one who exerts himself in the best of efforts. **22**

In the sign of **Virgo**, he is fond of play and sport. He is a consumer of others' property, a seeker of pleasure, and intoxicated by travel through different countries. He is devoted to women...

# On the Consideration of the Rising Signs

...driven by anger and of a restless nature. <note>Verse 40</note> He will be dear to the merchant class <term>Vaishya</term>, a performer of sacrifices, a man of merit, possessing scars, and fond of laughter. ->**The Rising of Aquarius and Pisces**<-

With the rising of **Aquarius** <note>Kumbha</note>, one is well-restrained in conduct and a speaker of truth. He is skilled in many crafts, possesses a noble mind, yet is prone to fear and sudden anger toward his kinsmen. He is strong, though often disappointed, yet remains eloquent and courageous. <note>Verse 48</note>

### Thus ends the consideration of the Rising Signs in the Star-Chart.

In the first half-sign <term>Hora</term> of Aries, one is cruel, fierce, and possesses a crooked nose and a harsh gaze. He has thick, raised joints and hands, and is inclined toward theft. <note>The Hora is a 15-degree division of a zodiac sign</note> In the second half-sign, he is a thief, full of negligence, yet maintains his own strength. He may appear feminine, yet has a well-nourished and stout body, growing in prosperity. <note>Verse 3</note> He has eyes like a deer, a broad chest, and receives honor among his peers.

# On the Signs of the Body

...those whose nostrils are well-proportioned and whose eyes are like the petals of a lotus <note>original: "pīnāyanā"</note> are beloved by their people and possess great wealth. Those with firm and well-developed breasts <insert>and a balanced gait</insert> are destined to perform many righteous deeds for the sake of others. ॐ <note>The sacred syllable Om, used here as an auspicious marker or section break</note> One whose forehead is broad and whose skin is clear will be devoted to the highest truths and remain steadfast in friendship.

Those with eyes that possess a reddish tint <note>original: "raktāna"</note> like the crane are said to be courageous and destined for great enjoyment. If the thighs are shaped like the trunk of an elephant, it signifies a person of great fortune and natural authority. 

A person with a broad chest and well-formed limbs will be a practitioner of <term>Yoga</term> <note>The discipline of physical and spiritual union</note> and a servant of the divine. If the teeth are even, white, and shimmering like pearls, the individual will be a scholar of many sciences <note>original: "shastra"</note> and a seeker of truth.

One whose voice is as deep as a rain cloud and whose skin has the luster of gold will be a self-reliant ascetic. Those with a rounded physique are wealthy, respected by women, and possess a gentle, sage-like nature. However, one with an uneven nose and a harsh voice may be prone to anger and hidden sorrows.

Those with eyes that are restless and bloodshot <note>original: "raktāna rasvi"</note> ॐ may be prone to intense heat of temperament, yet they will find happiness if they remain disciplined. A person with a steady gaze and a firm stride is a hero in battle, a loyal friend, and a protector of the virtuous. Such a person is destined to be a leader among men, possessing both courage and a noble character.

One who is veiny <note>original: "sirālaḥ"; indicating prominent veins, often associated with a nervous or intense temperament</note>, possesses a thick jaw, and has a feminine gait, yet is thin and covered in even, fine hair, will be a cruel and harsh speaker. <margin>8</margin> 1.

One whose voice resembles that of a donkey <note>original: "rāsabha-rāvaḥ"</note>, and who has white teeth and hanging, hollow cheeks, will be a man of wandering intellect, born in the ninth portion of the moon. He is like a lion among men.

One whose voice is like that of a deer <note>original: "sāraṅga-rāvo"</note> is an eloquent speaker and possesses knowledge of the primary pleasures. He will be the first among those with excellent horses and great wealth. 1.

One of a first-rate nature who is a backbiter, fond of quarrels, and speaks with heavy words, will have a fickle wife; in the second part of his life, he will have great power. 2.

One with a prominent nose and a quivering lip, who is modest and well-disposed with pale arms, will be an eloquent speaker and a beautiful person in the third stage of life; he is known for his stability. 3.

One with a delicate form, sweet speech, and very little hair on the neck, who has beautiful arms and a pale complexion, will be a leader of a village. Though he may be thin and prone to fainting, he is handsome, has strong arms, and is well-nourished like a swan. 4.

The fifth type is one who is famous, has a heart directed toward the supreme, and is [unclear]. 5.

One who is a poet, possesses a beautiful voice, is self-controlled, and performs virtuous deeds is very sweet; he is a writer or scribe, gains wealth, is highly respected, and enjoys wandering in many gardens. 6.

One with an elevated face and thick flesh is wise among women and well-cleansed...

->**The Characteristics of Men**<-

He shall be illustrious <note>original: "yashasvi"</note> and possess a lofty body <note>original: "tunganga"</note>. He is an enjoyer of wealth, firm of limb, and possesses thick, blood-red and white eyebrows. He is not peaceful in his speech, yet he is enduring, acting according to his own will, and possessing a small degree of tenderness. 

He is white, of a bright and pale complexion, with a body that is both symmetrical and beautiful. He is clever and devoted to the arts <note>original: "kala-nirata"</note>. He is intent upon kindness and laughter, yet possesses a detached nature in the first division. Thus it is said.

He may be short of stature with a prominent nose and a broad forehead; his limbs are reddish-white, and he is skillful, though his hair is coarse. In the first of the nine divisions of the head: he is pale, clear-spoken, and devoted to giving gifts and the study of sacred texts <note>original: "svadhyaya"</note>. 

In the second position: he is devoted to fierce strength and bold deeds, possessing little hair. 

In the third: his eyes are red at the corners, he has strong arms and pleasant hair; he is a learned man of wise speech, yet he is devoted to female servants and is famous, though his eyes may be bloodshot and he is prone to laughter. 

In the fourth: he is devoted to the wives of others, yet he is a seeker of security and a connoisseur of tastes; he is not tall, is fearful of sin, and is resolute and stout of limb. 

In the fifth: he attains a deep gravity <note>original: "gambhira"</note> but has a sunken nose; he speaks with opposition, his eyes are red, and he is visible in his actions; such a one shall be illustrious. 

In the sixth: he is all-encompassing, has a short nose, and is known for his deep insight; he is active in his work with his eyes, yet he has small hands. 

In the eighth: his face is greenish and veined <note>original: "sira"</note>; his veins are prominent and his body is not large. He has a sunken belly <note>original: "nimnodara"</note>, is even-tempered, and possesses a delicate frame.

# The Sixth Category and Beyond

1. Those with a nose shaped like the *priyangu* flower <note>The *priyangu* or Aglaia roxburghiana is a slender, fragrant medicinal plant; this likely refers to a long, delicate nose</note>, thick veins, and who speak with hostility toward all are known as obstacles. Such a man is considered disagreeable and prone to anger. One who has fair thighs, a prominent chest, and resides in a small dwelling is like a fire. He is refined, balanced, yet argumentative. This concludes the ninth section <note>original: "nava me"</note> and the **sixth category**.

2. One with widely spaced teeth, a large belly, and hair that stands on end is well-known. He has a fair complexion, a high-pitched voice, and is wealthy like a king. He may have a lean body, be very talkative, a traveler, deceitful, and lazy. With a curved nose and a mind focused on sin, he is balanced in fat and possesses a body as hard as a thunderbolt <term>Vajra: a mythical weapon or diamond, implying indestructible hardness</term>. He is poor and constantly distressed.

3. A lover of the **Gandharva arts** <note>The celestial music and performing arts of the gods</note> is famous for being fair-skinned, with hands and flesh that are unblemished. He is a friend to those as strong as thunderbolts, possesses a pleasant voice, and is diligent in his duties.

4. One with reddish-black eyes and a broad forehead has a slender body and is heavy <note>possibly meaning dignified or physically weighty</note>. With scattered hair and a wealth of silence, he has widely spaced teeth and is driven by desire.

5. One who speaks agitating words is a cruel husband to his wife. Those with a cruel-looking nose are like harlots. With a large belly and long arms, he is steady in his undertakings in the fifth part of his life.

6. One with a glistening complexion, who frequents courtesans and delights in sexual pleasure, has fine teeth and eats hard foods. He is a patient person with a strong jaw and a broad forehead; he is truly fortunate.

7. One who is sinful, lazy, and speaks very little has long shanks and a mocking laugh, yet is firm. With feet like a lotus petal, he is intelligent, balanced in flesh, and endowed with **excellent character**.

One who moves like a cloud, free from faults, shall enjoy the pleasures of a ruler of men <note>original: "narendra"</note>; they are a lord of wealth, fortunate, and have sparse body hair.

# The Lords of the Ninth Division

...one becomes accomplished and experiences great joy in the teeth. <note>The text refers to the eighth division of a specific sign here.</note> 8. Regarding the lords of the ninth division <term>Navamansharaja: the planetary ruler of a 3°20' arc of the zodiac</term>, they are well-known and celebrated; one becomes joyful through their influence. 9.

### The Sign of Aquarius
The person born under this influence is fair-skinned, with a well-proportioned body. They possess a steady mind and are particularly attractive to women. Though they may appear restless or agitated, they are successful. 1.

### The Sign of Pisces
One has a short neck and a slender waist. Under this influence, the individual is joyful. 1. They possess a beautiful and prominent face and nose. They are active, well-fleshed, and have a sturdy body. Such a person frequents forests and mountains and remains steadfast. 2.

One is fair-complexioned, though perhaps deceitful, yet well-dressed. They have a small frame but a mind inclined toward righteousness <term>Dharma: duty, ethics, or religious law</term>. They are learned, powerful, self-reliant, and humble. They possess the endurance to cross over difficulties and find joy in horses. 3.

One is virtuous, fond of worldly pleasures, and acts with boldness and courage. They are steady in their actions. 4. Possessing great strength and a disciplined mind, they are inclined toward humility. 4.

One is tall, perhaps a bit dull or prone to mistakes, yet possesses a profound intellect. They have a small nose. Such a person is a son devoted to non-violence <note>original: "himsatratah" - though the OCR suggests a vow of violence, in this context of "subhago" (fortunate), it likely refers to the discipline of power</note>, fortunate, and esteemed by kings. In this fifth division, one becomes majestic. 5.

One is lovely, powerful, and energetic. They are influential but may have a hot temper <note>original: "ushmasiko" - literally 'hot-nosed'</note> and are full of pride. Though they may lack wealth, they are skilled in their own work and devoted to their wife. 6.

One is a good friend, full of self-respect, and possesses a taste for the first and finest things. They may serve as a minister or counselor. They are diligent, though perhaps lacking in traditional character, and associate with those who take risks. 7.

One is tall, prominent, and steady. They are lazy in the arts but have blood-red eyes and distinctive hair. They may be slow or sickly, yet they possess a sense of shame and modesty. 8.

One is short, intoxicated with passion, yet remains resolute and wise...

### Thus ends the chapter named 'Anahrit' in the Saravali <note>The text refers to 'Sāravatī', likely a variant or specific commentary on the Sāravālī, a foundational 8th-century astrological treatise by Kalyānavarman</note>, a jewel of thought on the divisions of the signs.

Regarding the clear strength of the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> and other factors for men: a wise man should understand the comfort of the bed, the pleasure of love, eating, wealth, and dreams. 3. Now, if a planet is situated in the first decan <term>Drekkana: a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</term> of the Ascendant, it indicates the state of 'Shayana' <note>literally 'lying down' or 'sleeping'</note>, followed by 'Upavesha' <note>sitting</note> and so on in sequence. If one is lying down with Jupiter, Venus, Saturn, or Mars, the results should be determined through the meanings of the decans; so it is for the others. 3.

The calculations for others are as follows: some say that the degrees of the Ascendant and others, when multiplied, and divided by five, yield the result for Jupiter. 4. By estimating the age and other factors here, one determines the state. In the case of wine and pleasure, the possibility of eating <note>original: "jemam", referring to consumption or meals</note> is seen in reverse. 5. Following the degrees of the Ascendant in order, one should understand the time. Whichever sign in the trine <term>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term> of the Ascendant is strong, that indicates the nature of the meal. 6.

From the head onwards, according to the placement in the signs, one by one. As far as the result of the Ascendant is concerned, the meal is like that of a king. 7. If Pisces is the rising sign, the result is according to the degrees of the Ascendant. From the Ascendant, in the ninth or the trine, the cycle of sleeping and waking is told. 8. A wise man should speak from the Ascendant; such is the instruction of the Scripture <term>Shastra: systematic treatise or sacred theory</term>.

->**The Determination**<-

...certainty. Whether in the Ascendant <term>Lagna; the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> or in the Serpent <note>likely referring to the lunar node Rahu</note>, and likewise in the northern Meru, one should provide instruction on devotion to the Sun for those who are apprehensive. 10. The strength of the solstice <term>Ayana; the sun's northward or southward journey</term> is calculated; one should state the measure of the centers <term>Kendras; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term> starting from the Ascendant in the order of the birth ceremony. The multiplication should be performed by the qualities of the names, months, and so forth, combined with the multipliers. By dividing by three and taking the remainder, the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra; one of the 27 sectors of the lunar zodiac</term> is found according to the tradition. 11.

In Taurus and Leo, the measure is eight fingers; in Gemini and Aquarius, it is three; in Libra and Aries, it is ten. The sages say that in Virgo and Capricorn, it is seven; the remaining signs are determined by their own specific measures. 12. By the multipliers of three and nine, and by the wisdom of Bhrigu <note>a legendary sage and astrologer</note>, the divisions of the sky are determined by the suns <note>original: "bhanubhih," referring to the number 12</note> respectively. The year, the month, and the day-night divisions are again calculated by the strength of the solstice portions. 13.

Having performed the calculations in this order, the individual variations are known from the remainder. Thus they exist in truth; I shall speak of them again through purification <note>correction or refinement of calculations</note>. 14. All of this was declared here by the Lords of the **Yavanendu Dashas** <note>original: "Yavanendu-dasha-nathaih"; refers to the "Lord of the Greeks" system of planetary periods, indicating the influence of Greco-Babylonian astrology on Indian texts</note>; however, not all of it is applicable to the children of this world. 15.

One should consider the division of the days by halves of the measures of day and night, in direct and reverse order, with each name being equal, designated as day or night. 16. In the sequence of the quarters, having multiplied by the mountains <note>the number 7</note> and qualities <note>the number 3</note>, the calculation is made; in this human knowledge, the twenty-seven lunar mansions are purified from the knots <term>Granthi; difficult points or joints in the calculation</term>. 17. The written forms are of equal appearance; the remainder becomes the day of the week starting from Sunday; the fifth is declared as...

->Summary of the Text<-

...if the planets are in the descending order, the calculation should be done accordingly. [18] When the planets are rising <note>appearing in the eastern horizon</note>, the periods are calculated in direct order; if they are setting or retrograde, the results are determined in reverse. Experts in time-calculation should apply these proportions wisely. [19] The multipliers are determined by the planetary strengths; when planets are associated with their friends or in their own signs, they bestow full radiance. It is said by the wise that the years are multiplied by two in such cases. [20] In this manner, the years are calculated for all, even for kings, through these divisions. When three or more planets are involved, the specific results are explained according to their own nature. [21]

From the Ascendant <note>the rising sign</note>, it is auspicious in the 3rd, 4th, 6th, 10th, 11th, and 12th. [2]

The <term>Moon</term> <note>original: Himakara, "The Cool-Rayed One"</note> is auspicious in the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th positions from the Sun. From itself, it is auspicious in the 1st, 3rd, 6th, 7th, 10th, and 11th. From Mars, it is auspicious in the 2nd, 3rd, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th, and 11th. Birth 2 || Thus ends the section on the **Moon**. || When Jupiter is in the twelfth, eleventh, sixth, or seventh houses from Venus; or when Jupiter is in the sixth, eleventh, or tenth houses from its own sign, it brings enjoyment. When the Sun and Saturn are in the third, seventh, or sixth houses from the birth sign, they are considered malefic. Mercury is auspicious in the sixth, second, and eleventh houses. From the <term>Ascendant</term><note>The zodiacal sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note> and the <term>Upachaya</term><note>The 'houses of increase' (3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th) where planets grow stronger over time.</note> houses, if the Sun is placed in masculine signs, it grants power. 4 **Saturn-Mars.** || One becomes a conqueror when the two malefics are in the directions of their strength. Venus brings brothers and wives. Jupiter is associated with the study of the <term>Shastras</term><note>Sacred scriptures or systematic treatises of knowledge.</note> when positioned in the enemy's house, the eighth house, or the twelfth house. Just as the benefics are in the previous twelfth and sixth houses, so too Saturn is auspicious in the second, twelfth, <term>tenth</term>, sixth, and eighth houses from the Ascendant. 5 **Saturn-Mercury.** || From the lord of the <term>Kendra</term><note>The pivotal 'angular' houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th.</note> in the second house; from the son of the Earth <insert>Mars</insert> in the twelfth; from the Sun with humility; from Venus in the ninth, eleventh, or sixth houses. From the Moon in the ninth, eleventh, seventh, or twelfth houses. From Saturn in the <term>Trikona</term><note>The 'trine' houses (1st, 5th, and 9th), considered highly auspicious.</note>, the sixth, or the fifth. From the Sun in the third, eleventh, or sixth houses. From the significator of brothers <insert>Mars</insert> or from the son <insert>Mercury</insert> in equal positions. || Thus ends the section on **Jupiter**. || From the Ascendant, if planets are placed in the ninth or the seventh, or from the twelfth to the end, they are auspicious. In the eleventh, seventh, and fourth houses, or in the houses of brothers, enemies, or children, or in exaltation. In the eighth, eleventh, or sixth houses from Jupiter, or in the fifth or eleventh. || Thus ends the section on **Venus**. || From its own sign, in the eleventh, twelfth, sixth, or third houses; from Saturn and the Moon in moving signs; from Mars...

If the Sun is situated in one of the four <term>Kendras</term><note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, which are the pillars of a horoscope.</note> and the lord of that sign is strong, the person born will be famous. If the lord of the <term>Navamsha</term><note>The ninth divisional chart, used to determine the true strength of a planet.</note> occupied by the Sun is associated with or aspected by Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus, the person will be free from miseries and gain wealth with little effort, living like a lord among the twice-born <note>original: "dvijavarā"; referring to the priestly or scholarly class</note>. Thus ends the Solar section. If the planets are malefic, the results are as stated; if auspicious, they are favorable. The planets in the sky do not give their full results without considering the specific time of their application. A man cannot succeed without help, just as a single wheel cannot move a chariot; similarly, the planets require collective strength to manifest their full effects. If the Sun and Moon are in their own signs, or if Jupiter and Venus are in the middle of the planets, or if Saturn is in the ascendant, they provide results according to their strength.

->**Thus ends the Chapter on Ashtakavarga in the Sārāvalī.**<-

Having bowed to the Sun, who is the source of light for the world of the gods, I shall now declare the <term>Yoni-Bheda</term><note>The "distinctions of origin," referring to the various types of births (human, animal, etc.) based on planetary positions.</note> as taught by **Kanakacharya** <note>An ancient authority on astrology cited by Kalyāṇavarman.</note>. Such a person enjoys pleasures in this world like a god among the beautiful women of the three worlds. Just as the movements of living beings vary according to the time and place, I shall describe the various forms of birth. When the cruel planets are endowed with their own strength...

# On Non-Human Births

When the **Ascendant** <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> is in a non-human sign and is occupied by **benefic planets** <note>original: "saumya"; refers to naturally auspicious planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a strong Moon</note>, or if the benefic planets are in the **cardinal houses** <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope</note>, or in their own divisions, then a non-human birth occurs. 4. Regarding the place of birth and residence, one should determine it from the **Moon**; whichever sign the Moon occupies, or the Ascendant, that shall indicate the place of birth. 5.

The various distinctions of **color and form** for these non-human species have been described by the ancient sages. Here, I shall explain the essential points specifically regarding their physical proportions. 6. 

### The Anatomy of the Zodiac
->The Body Parts of the Animal Kingdom<-

| Zodiac Sign | Corresponding Body Part |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Aries** | The Head |
| **Taurus** | The Face and Throat |
| **Gemini** | The Shoulders and Forelegs |
| **Cancer** | The Heart, Belly, and Sides |
| **Leo** | The Back and the Heart |
| **Virgo** | The Hips and the Genitals |
| **Libra** | The Lower Abdomen |
| **Scorpio** | The Anus and Tail |

7-8. For four-legged creatures, the first six signs starting from **Aries** represent the left side of the body. The remaining signs, starting from **Libra**, represent the right side. <note>The text describes a mapping of the macrocosm of the stars onto the microcosm of an animal's body</note> 9.

The specific appearance and color are determined clearly by the rising sign and the strength of the planets. Through the combination of the **vowels** <note>referring to the phonetic associations of the signs</note> and the **divisional charts**, one should describe the colors of the limbs. 10.

If planets are situated in the **seventh house**, they indicate the number of offspring or the variety of colors in the non-human birth. As many planets as there are, so many will be the colors of the non-human creature. 11. 

### Planetary Colors
The strongest planet determines the primary color of the non-human creature:
*   **Jupiter** makes the color yellow.
*   **Venus** <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> makes the color white or variegated.
*   **The Sun and Mars** <note>original: "dinakara-rudhirau"</note> create red or copper tones.
*   **Saturn** creates black.
*   **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note> creates a dark or green hue. 12.

If a planet is in its own **ninth-division** <note>term: Navamsha; a 3.33 degree segment of a sign used for fine-tuning predictions</note> or in the latter part of a sign, the effects are intensified.

# On Non-human Births

The first of these <note>referring to the divisions of the signs</note> is the primary one, while the others are secondary. When the Ascendant <term>Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> is occupied by strong benefic planets, one should speak of a non-human birth. 13.

When malefic planets are in their own signs or in their signs of exaltation, and are associated with powerful planets and signs, they indicate the birth of creatures that are knowledgeable of others. 14. When the planets are released from the influence of the cruel <note>malefic</note> planets, or are aspected by them, there are twenty-seven varieties of creatures born from moisture and heat <note>insects and small organisms</note>. 15.

The combination of planets is explained in the context of their strength. When the Moon and the Sun are weak, and Aries is the rising sign, and the Sun is in the eighth house while other planets are in the tenth and twelfth houses, a birth occurs. 17. I have briefly described the general category called "non-human"; now listen, for I shall specifically explain the curious nature of these births. 18.

Whatever twelve-fold divisions <term>Dvadasamsa; a 2.5-degree division of a zodiac sign</term> are known in each sign, all of them are said to result in non-human births when the Ascendant is occupied by powerful malefic planets. 19.

The teachers say that in the portions of Aries, Taurus, and Leo, the birth of goats and similar animals occurs. For cattle and buffaloes, which have many forms, the birth is determined by the lord of the sign. 30. <note>The manuscript jumps from verse 19 to 30</note>.

In the fourth portion, the birth of those born in water, such as tortoises, occurs. Beyond that, according to the knowledge of the Greeks <note>original: "Yavana"; referring to Indo-Greek astrological traditions</note>, the birth of tigers and similar predators is explained. 31.

When the portion is strong and has a human form, but the third part is reached, the birth is of a serpent. In the ninth part, it is of donkeys and similar hoofed animals. In the tenth part, it is of deer and birds. 32. Among them, those who are knowledgeable say that the eleventh part results in the birth of bushes and shrubs, or various types of creatures born from the forest.

<summary>This page outlines the astrological conditions, specifically the divisions of zodiac signs and planetary strengths, that result in the birth of animals, insects, and plants rather than humans. It highlights the influence of "Yavana" (Greek) astrological thought on the classification of predatory animals.</summary>

<keywords>Vedic astrology, Viyonijanma, non-human birth, Dvadasamsa, Yavana, animal births, zodiac divisions, malefic planets</keywords>

(42) Likewise of spiders, mongooses, and various types of scorpions; for those who dwell in hidden places, their endurance and nature are known through these divisions. (43) Thus, in the Ascendant of a king <note>Sanskrit: *naradeva*; literally 'god among men'</note>, the Moon and Sun should be examined through the twelfth-part...

The birth of these occurs through the influence of the moon's counselor <note>likely referring to Jupiter, the 'minister' of the planets</note> and the jewel of the night. 46. The birth and death of insects and similar creatures <note>verse 52</note> are not seen to be the same as those of humans or gods. When auspicious planets move in masculine signs, <insert>and are located in the ascendant</insert>, they become like a garland of swans <note>likely a metaphor for a noble or beautiful animal birth</note> 53. With the rising of the sign Leo, or by the strength of the wind <note>the element Vayu</note>, the wise should determine the behavior of the creature. If the birth occurs through the "cruel" <term>Ukatam; likely referring to malefic influences</term> path, it leads to the death of the females 54. When a fixed sign is rising and is not aspected by the planet Saturn, or if the hand <note>the "Hasta" or influence</note> of Mars and others is present, the results should be understood as previously stated 55. Thus ends the forty-second chapter, titled "Births in Non-Human Species," in the *Saravali* composed by the illustrious Kalyanavarman. Among hundreds of years, no one has remained <unclear>permanent</unclear>; having heard this from the people, <note>Kalyanavarman</note> composed this *Saravali* which illuminates the characteristics of the stars.

# The Sārāvalī (Astrology)

<note>The Sārāvalī is a foundational 8th-century Sanskrit treatise on predictive astrology composed by Kalyāṇavarman. It is considered a bridge between early Greek-influenced Indian astrology and later medieval systems.</note>

<note>original: "सारावली" (Sārāvalī) - literally "The Essence-Row" or "The Chain of Essentials."</note>
<note>original: "ज्योतिषम्" (Jyotiṣam) - The traditional Indian science of astronomy and astrology.</note>
<note>original: "पत्राणि" (Patrāṇi) - Leaves or folios of the manuscript.</note>
<note>original: "संपूर्णम्" (Sampūrṇam) - Indicates the text is finished and no parts are missing.</note>
<note>original: "पृष्ठानि" (Pṛṣṭhāni) - Individual pages (two per folio).</note>
